# HANDOFF — Session Continuity Document

> **Purpose of this file:** This is the living memory of the PaperTrader project. If you are a new Claude session (Cowork or Claude Code) picking this up cold, read this file FIRST, then `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and `ROADMAP.md`. It records who we are, what we've decided, what's done, what's next, and every important decision and its reasoning — so no context is ever lost between sessions.
>
> **Maintenance rule:** Claude (acting as CTO in Cowork) updates this file at the end of any meaningful exchange — new decisions, completed work, changed direction, new blockers. Append to the Changelog at the bottom every time. Keep it elaborate. When in doubt, over-document.

**Last updated:** 2026-08-21 (PART 8 — the closing pass on this incident: raised `THINKER_CONCURRENCY` to 8 for real (not left in reserve until forced), labelled the margin status badge with when it was last computed, adopted the convention-discoverability recommendation as a standing CLAUDE.md rule — BUILT, VERIFIED live in browser, **NOT pushed, reporting first.** `1dae1bd` (PART 7, immediately below) **pushed to `origin/main` this round, on explicit instruction.**

**1. `THINKER_CONCURRENCY` raised 5→8, deliberately, while calm rather than under pressure.** The prior round measured 8 at 1.85x faster than 5 with zero errors, then left the deployed bound at 5 anyway — holding a free, already-proven improvement in reserve until a capacity trigger forced the issue. Corrected: changed now. Re-measured TWICE more to confirm — once as part of the original 3-level sweep, once again as an independent confirmation AFTER the constant was actually changed in code — and concurrency=8's own number was remarkably stable both times (32.1s, both runs) even though concurrency=5's number moved between runs (real day-to-day Claude-latency variance). New derived ceiling: **~121 eligible agents** (up from ~65 at concurrency=5), warning/action triggers moved to 61/83. The one real, explicit tradeoff (asked for directly, not glossed over): at concurrency=8, worst-case simultaneous Finnhub fallback exposure (8) now exceeds `ratelimit.server.ts`'s 6-concurrent cap by 2 — not a failure (the limiter queues, never rejects, confirmed by reading its implementation), just occasional brief queuing on an already-rare path. No other cost — identical total Claude calls either way. The 8→10 flattening from the prior measurement is recorded as a genuinely OPEN question, not a settled "10 doesn't help" — flagged for re-measurement at a larger synthetic batch (40-50 agents) before relying on it, since 13-agent tests can't cleanly separate a real ceiling from concurrency=10 simply being close to full-batch parallelism at that scale.

**2. Margin status badge — the cheap half of the staleness rule shipped, the expensive half deliberately parked with a written assessment.** `app.margin.tsx`'s ok/warning/call badge reads the STORED `profiles.margin_status` (written only by `runMarginMonitor()`, daily snapshot cron + watchdog every 30 min market-hours) right next to LIVE equity/maintenance numbers — flagged last round, left unfixed pending a scoping decision. Shipped the cheap half now: `getMarginStateFn` returns `marginStatusCheckedAt` (the more recent of the `snapshot`/`agent-watchdog` cron_heartbeats — both jobs run the monitor for every margin-enabled user, so whichever ran most recently genuinely reflects when THIS user's status was last confirmed), no migration needed, reusing already-tracked data. `StatusBanner` now shows "As of [time]" under the verdict in all three states (ok/warning/call). **Verified live** with a real throwaway margin-enabled account: banner correctly showed "As of Aug 21, 5:13 PM," a real timestamp from the actual last snapshot/watchdog heartbeat, distinct from (and older than) the account's own just-now "margin enabled" event — proving it's pulling genuine cron data, not a placeholder. The live-recomputation half (making the LABEL itself always-current, not just timestamped) recorded as a new PLAN.md §6h backlog entry with an honest assessment of why it's genuinely harder than the underfunded banner was: the real enforcement action (forced liquidation) is inherently periodic, so a naively "live" status label risks being MORE misleading in the other direction (implying an action already happened when it hasn't) unless the copy is written very deliberately — not a mechanical port of the same fix.

**3. Convention-discoverability recommendation adopted as written, not left as a proposal.** Added one non-growing line to CLAUDE.md's Working style section: grep sibling files in the same domain before introducing a new tolerance/threshold/concurrency constant, with the three concrete incidents named directly (sector helper, brief concurrency, `closeToLive`) so it's memorable rather than generic advice. Deliberately NOT a growing catalog — that shape was already rejected as the "stated policy, not structural prevention" anti-pattern this project keeps re-learning.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-21 (PART 7 — closes the capacity-triggers thread with corrected concurrency math; fixes a real, separate bug (underfunded banner showing a stale verdict); sweeps for the same stale-computed-state pattern elsewhere; adds `scripts/poll-until.sh` after a real 86-hour zombie `vercel ls` poll loop was found and killed — BUILT, VERIFIED live in browser with a real account crossing the threshold, **NOT pushed, reporting first.** `2a6ac3c` (PART 6, immediately below) **pushed to `origin/main` this round, on explicit instruction.**

**0. Background-process hygiene — new standing discipline, applied starting this entry.** A `until npx vercel ls | grep Ready; do sleep N; done` loop, hand-rolled and backgrounded in an earlier session, ran for 86 hours after its deploy had long finished (~60k wasted CLI calls); a dev server sat idle 44 hours. Both found and killed manually — not by any tooling. Fixed structurally, not just by promising to remember: (1) `scripts/poll-until.sh` — bounded polling with a MANDATORY `--timeout` (no infinite default; refuses to run without one), loud non-zero-exit on timeout naming what it was waiting for and for how long. Tested: missing-timeout rejection, success path, and a real bounded timeout that stopped in exactly 3s. (2) Standing report format, from now on: every substantive report ends with an explicit line disclosing any background process left running, or "none" — see the end of this session's own report for the first real instance (the dev server, kept running deliberately for this round's browser verification, disclosed with why).

**1. Concurrency reasoning corrected — the previous entry's core claim was WRONG, and re-measured rather than re-argued.** Challenged directly: "latency is precisely what concurrency relieves." Correct, and the prior text ("more concurrency doesn't relieve Claude call latency") had it backwards. Re-measured `agent-thinker` at concurrency 5/8/10 against the same 13 real-scale throwaway agents and one shared prefetch (`thinker-concurrency-timing.ts`, extended to sweep multiple levels in one run; `runThinkerForAllAgents` gained a test-only `concurrency` override so this needed no production code path change): **5→59.4s, 8→32.1s (1.85x faster), 10→31.5s (1.89x faster)** — confirms concurrency scales throughput almost exactly as the math predicts up to 8; the 8→10 flattening shows zero errors and no sign of a real external wall, more plausibly a small-N artifact (concurrency=10 is already near full-batch parallelism at only 13 agents) than a discovered ceiling — flagged for re-measurement at larger N if the trigger ever gets close. Also re-derived the thinker's ceiling using this run's own (slower, more conservative) concurrency=5 number: **~65 agents** (down from ~80, purely real day-to-day Claude-latency variance, not a regression), with warning/action triggers moved to 33/45 agents accordingly. PLAN.md §6g updated with the real 5/8/10 table and the corrected explanation.

**2. Underfunded banner bug — diagnosed, confirmed, fixed at the source, verified live end-to-end.** Diagnosis confirmed exactly: `/app/agent`'s "too small to invest" banner read `underfunded`/`suggested_min_funding` off the MOST RECENT `agent_decisions` row with `action='rebalance'` — a snapshot of what the thinker found on its LAST run, not the account's current state. Funding an underfunded agent didn't clear the banner until the next scheduled thinker run, up to 24h later. Fixed at the source, not patched: extracted `GUARDRAILS` out of `execute.server.ts` (server-only, pulls in the service-role client) into a new bundle-safe `lib/agent/guardrails.ts`, alongside a new pure `suggestedMinFunding(riskLevel)` function (no live market data needed — a pure function of risk-level constants only). `thinker.server.ts` now calls this SAME function instead of its own inline copy of the formula (single source of truth, no drift risk). `/app/agent` now computes `isUnderfunded` live on every render from `totalValue` (already-computed current `agent_cash` + live holdings prices) vs. `suggestedMinFunding(risk_level)`, gated on `allocated > 0` (reusing the same "has this agent ever been engaged with" signal the activity-status section already uses, so a brand-new never-funded agent doesn't show an alarming banner before the user has done anything). Extracted the banner itself into a new presentational `AgentUnderfundedBanner` component (plain props, matching `AgentActivityStatusLine`'s existing testable-component pattern) — 4 new component tests plus 5 new tests for `suggestedMinFunding` itself (including the exact $25 figure from the real bug report).
  - **Verified live, not just unit-tested:** created a real throwaway agent, funded it $10 (below balanced's $25 minimum), ran the REAL thinker to log a genuine `underfunded:true` decision — banner correctly showed "Consider funding at least $25.00." Then funded it to $60 via the actual UI Fund flow — **banner disappeared immediately**, while the page's own "Last decision" and Decision log both STILL showed the identical stale Aug-21 5:01pm entry ("Consider funding at least $25") and "Next scheduled run" was still the following day — direct, airtight proof the fix works via live derivation, not a hidden re-run.
  - **"Run agent now" surfacing decision, reasoned rather than defaulted either way:** NOT an automatic run (explicitly not asked for, and the button was already live-enabled the moment `agent_cash` crosses 0 regardless). Instead, the funding-success toast now specifically calls out "enough to invest — click Run agent now below" ONLY when that exact funding action just crossed the underfunded→funded boundary — not on every top-up, so it doesn't become boilerplate noise, but a user who just fixed "too small to invest" is told what to do next instead of left to notice the button unprompted.

**3. Swept for the same stale-computed-state pattern elsewhere — one more real (but lower-severity, already-mitigated) instance found, several surfaces confirmed clean.**
  - **Found — margin status badge (`app.margin.tsx`'s `StatusBanner`), FLAGGED not fixed.** `getMarginStateFn` computes `equity`/`buyingPower`/`maintenanceRequirement` LIVE (fresh `getPositionsValue` every call) but reads the ok/warning/call STATUS LABEL directly from the stored `profiles.margin_status` column — written only by `runMarginMonitor()` (daily snapshot cron + the watchdog every 30 min during market hours). Same defect shape as the underfunded banner: the numbers are live, the verdict label isn't. Meaningfully lower risk than the banner bug was, though: the staleness window is bounded to at most ~30 minutes during active market hours (vs. up to 24h) and zero overnight/weekends when prices don't move, because the watchdog exists specifically to catch this quickly. NOT fixed this round — deliberately, not an oversight: the real enforcement action (forced liquidation) is inherently periodic/event-driven, so a client-computed "live" status label would be informational-only and could actually mislead in the other direction if it diverges from what real enforcement will do. Flagging for a scoping decision rather than either fixing it hastily or ignoring it.
  - **Confirmed CLEAN (computed live or already correctly labeled):** the Coach nudge (`computeBehavioralAnalytics` runs fresh off the real `transactions` table every load, no cache); the Markets/Watchlist news+insight indicator dots (`getContentAvailabilityFn` recomputes `today` and queries `insights`/`price_cache` fresh server-side on every call — the same correct pattern the brief fix now uses); the Stock Detail AI Insight card (already explicitly labeled "Generated [time] · next refresh [time]" from the earlier Commit A fix this session); `AgentActivityStatusLine` (already hardened in AGENT-AUDIT.md Part 8 to report facts only, never a health verdict).
  - **Already fixed earlier this session, listed for completeness:** the insight-card on-demand-generation copy (Commit A), and the daily-brief "Today's market brief" staleness bug (`getTodaysBrief`'s `isToday`, PART 3 of this incident).

**4. Convention-discoverability — proposal only, not built, per instruction.** Three times in one session, a correct pattern existed and wasn't found before writing a near-duplicate: the sector helper never wired into Markets, the concurrency fix applied to the thinker but not the brief, `closeToLive` sitting unused one file over from a hand-rolled $200 tolerance. Honest read: a growing "conventions catalog" in CLAUDE.md is the WEAK option — it's exactly the "stated policy, not structural prevention" shape this project has already learned doesn't hold (the flakiness trigger that couldn't fire, being the clearest precedent). Recommended instead: (a) one short, NON-growing process line in CLAUDE.md's Working style section — "before introducing a new tolerance/threshold/concurrency constant, grep sibling files in the same domain for an existing one" — cheap, doesn't rot the way a catalog does, and names the actual failure (not searching) rather than trying to enumerate every convention in advance; (b) keep reusable primitives in predictable, domain-grouped locations (matching how `closeToLive` WAS findable the moment someone actually grepped for it) rather than scattering them, since a predictable location is what made this session's own sweep work. Explicitly flagged as incomplete: no documentation-only fix fully solves "an agent didn't search," and a heavier structural option (automated duplicate-pattern detection) would be the real fix but wasn't sized or built — left as a genuine open question, not a decision.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-19 (Agent-thinker/daily-brief incident, PART 6 — the closing pass: agent-thinker's triggers harmonised onto the same growth-rate-derived methodology as the brief's (with a separately-justified, deliberately conservative funded-agent growth rate, not the brief's 5/day copied unexamined); the `filled > 1` magic-number defect fixed properly (assert equality against the independently-computed value, not a guessed floor) and swept for elsewhere in the suite, finding and fixing one more real instance; thinker's concurrency=5 bound reassessed and confirmed still correct — BUILT, VERIFIED, **NOT pushed, reporting first.** `4d25e01` (PART 5, immediately below) **pushed to `origin/main` this round, on explicit instruction** — this entry's own work is a follow-up round on top of that, still local-only.

**1. agent-thinker's triggers harmonised onto the brief's methodology — with a separately-derived growth rate, not a copy-paste.** Now that the brief is parallelized (~109-user ceiling), agent-thinker (~80-agent ceiling) is the binding constraint again, and its 40/55 triggers predated the growth-rate reasoning applied to the brief. Checked the real historical basis before picking a number, same discipline as before: only **3 real users have ever funded an agent**, across ~55 days — far too thin a sample (unlike the 7-signup dataset behind the brief's figure) to independently fit a daily rate with any real confidence. Rather than inventing a number OR copying the brief's 5/day unexamined (explicitly warned against — funding is a materially higher bar than a watchlist add), the assumption is TIED to the brief's already-stated rate, scaled by the one real signal available: of the 7 real signups to date, 3 have gone on to fund an agent (~43% conversion, explicitly flagged as directional from n=7, not precise). `5/day × 0.43 ≈ 2.15/day`, rounded DOWN to **2 newly-funded agents/day** — conservative in both directions, per the explicit instruction to pick something deliberately conservative when the historical basis is this thin. Resulting runway: **warning at 40 agents (~20 days from a launch event), action-required at 55 (~28 days)** — correctly LONGER than the brief's 11/15-day runway, which is the direct, correct consequence of funding's higher bar, not a looser standard applied here (same underlying signup assumption, only the conversion step differs).

**2. `THINKER_CONCURRENCY = 5` reassessed, not just left alone by default.** Explicit question: does the brief's deeper look at `RateLimiter` queuing behavior change whether 5 is still right for the thinker? **Answer: still 5, and it could safely go higher, but there's no real benefit today.** The thinker's Finnhub exposure is almost entirely absorbed by its ONE shared universe prefetch — unlike the brief, raising `THINKER_CONCURRENCY` wouldn't meaningfully increase Finnhub load, so the queuing-safety argument doesn't change at a higher number. But the measured ceiling is bottlenecked by CLAUDE call latency (`claudeReason` per agent), which more thinker-side concurrency doesn't parallelize any further in a way that would show up as materially faster throughput — Claude's own account-level concurrency is nowhere near binding at 5 or higher. Raising it today would buy insurance against a FUTURE where per-agent Claude latency itself grows, not more headroom against today's real numbers. **Not changed** — no trigger has fired, and moving a number "because we can" without a reason to move it is exactly the class of unjustified action this whole exercise exists to avoid.

**3. `verify-options-trade-live.ts`'s `filled !== 0.01 && filled > 1` — fixed properly, not just patched around.** Diagnosed what the assertion was actually FOR: proving the client's doctored `premium: 0.01` field never reaches the fill, i.e. the executed price is the server's OWN independently-computed value. The `> 1` clause never served that purpose — it encoded an unstated, false assumption ("real premiums are always above $1") and failed today on a legitimate $0.87 fill. Fixed by making the harness's `runTrade` helper return the SAME `priced.premium` value it already independently computes and passes to the RPC as `expectedPremium`, then asserting the fill equals it exactly (`closeTo(filled, expectedPremium, 1e-9)`, the file's existing float-tolerance convention) — a strictly stronger proof (equality to a known-correct value, not "not-equal-to-one-specific-wrong-value AND arbitrarily-large") that's also immune to the real premium's actual dollar level. Verified live: re-ran twice, once landing on a real $5.00 fill, both green.

**4. Swept the rest of the suite for the same defect class — one more genuine instance found and fixed.** Delegated a systematic grep-and-read pass across all ~35 `verify-*.ts` scripts for hardcoded thresholds encoding a market-price, market-scale, or timing assumption rather than a correctness property. Found: **`verify-margin-live.ts`'s equity-unchanged-by-borrowing check** — comment promised "within a few cents" but the code allowed a flat **$200** of drift on a live-quote-derived equity figure for a ~$37k+ leveraged position, where the ACTUAL codebase convention for exactly this situation already exists a file over (`verify-hardening-valuation.ts`'s `closeToLive`, 5 basis points of the reference value, min 2¢) but wasn't reused here. Fixed: added the same relative-tolerance convention locally (`liveTolerance`, matching this file's existing pattern of not sharing helpers across scripts), explicitly scaled by `positionsValueAfter` — the actual price-sensitive quantity — rather than by `p0.cash` (which has no live-quote dependency and would have UNDERSTATED the real tick-noise bound, given the position is ~1.5x cash on margin). Verified live: re-ran standalone, real numbers `24989.56 vs 25000 (tolerance $18.74)` — correctly scoped and passing. Two other candidates surfaced by the sweep (`verify-eventstudy-live.ts`'s `events_found >= 3`, `verify-live-prices.ts`'s provider-call-count bands) were checked and judged genuinely justified — both are self-relative or comment-documented-and-derived, not guessed — left unchanged.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-19 (Agent-thinker/daily-brief incident, PART 5 — the Sentry fingerprint fix built and tested; daily-brief PARALLELIZED (the fix PART 4 correctly flagged as stopped-one-step-short); triggers reset with real runway — BUILT, VERIFIED with real before/after timing, **NOT pushed, reporting first.** `26d3933` (PART 4, immediately below) **pushed to `origin/main` this round, on explicit instruction** — this entry's own work is a follow-up round on top of that, still local-only.

**1. Sentry per-day fingerprint — built and tested exactly as recommended in PART 4, held pending confirmation.** `captureServerError()` (`sentry/server.ts`) gained an optional third `fingerprint?: string[]` param — omitted, behavior is byte-identical to before for every existing call site (Sentry's normal stack-trace-first grouping); passed, it bypasses that grouping entirely via `scope.setFingerprint(fingerprint)`. New pure `staleFingerprint(jobName, now)` in `staleness.server.ts` returns `["staleness", jobName, YYYY-MM-DD]` (UTC calendar day, matching this codebase's existing day-boundary convention); `checkStaleHeartbeats()` now passes it on every `captureServerError` call. **Tested properly, not just non-null-checked, per explicit instruction:** the SAME job at two different times on the SAME UTC day (00:00:01 vs 23:59:59) produces an IDENTICAL fingerprint; the SAME job one second apart but across a UTC-midnight boundary produces a DIFFERENT one; five consecutive days produce five distinct fingerprints (not just day-2-vs-day-1); different JOBS on the same day differ too (the fix is job-scoped, not just date-scoped); exact shape asserted directly. 6 new tests, all passing.

**2. Daily brief PARALLELIZED — the finding PART 4 stopped one step short of acting on, now fixed.** `runDailyBriefs` (`insights.server.ts`) had ZERO concurrency at all — a plain sequential per-user loop — even though PART 4's own measurement showed it was the TIGHTER capacity constraint of the two batch jobs. Fixed with the identical primitives already proven on `agent-thinker` (`batchUtils.ts`'s `shuffle` + `mapWithConcurrency`), at `BRIEF_CONCURRENCY = 5` — **not assumed to transfer unexamined, justified separately** (full reasoning in `insights.server.ts`'s new header comment): the real difference from the thinker is Finnhub exposure — `agent-thinker` prices its universe via ONE shared prefetch, so concurrency barely touches Finnhub at all, while the brief has NO shared prefetch, so 5 concurrent users with fully disjoint symbol sets could want up to 70 simultaneous Finnhub requests, a materially different worst case. Confirmed safe anyway by reading `ratelimit.server.ts`: `RateLimiter.acquire()` queues past its 6-concurrent cap and 50/60s window rather than ever rejecting (excess demand adds wait time, not failures — the same property the thinker's justification relies on), and `fhCompanyNews` is cached PER SYMBOL for 60 minutes GLOBALLY (`durableCached`), so real users' natural ticker overlap makes the fully-disjoint worst case a stress scenario, not the typical one. The real proof is the measurement below, not the reasoning alone.

**3. Fairness bug — the SAME bug class as the thinker's, confirmed present (not assumed by analogy) and fixed the same way.** `runDailyBriefs`'s per-user `bySym` Map was built by iterating the `holdings`/`watchlist` reads in whatever order Postgres returned them — no `.order()` clause, same stable-but-arbitrary ordering that caused the thinker's systematic unfairness. Confirmed by reading the code, then fixed: `shuffle([...bySym.entries()])` before processing, identical pattern to the thinker's fix.

**4. Measured, real before/after — same rigor as the thinker's measurement (`brief-capacity-timing.ts`, rewritten this round to do a genuine before/after rather than a single snapshot):** 15 throwaway users, 2 real distinct watchlist symbols each (deliberately disjoint across users — the stress case for Finnhub exposure, not an optimistic cache-warm one).
  - **BEFORE (one user at a time — the real `runDailyBriefs({onlyUserIds:[id]})` looped, exercising the exact same per-user logic the old sequential loop did): 196.7s for 15 users, 13.11s/user.**
  - **AFTER (concurrency=5, shuffled): 41.2s for 15 users — 4.78x speedup, 13.7% of the 300s budget.** Zero errors across all 15 users.
  - Honest caveat stated in the script's own header: the BEFORE measurement re-runs the (cheap) holdings/watchlist read on every single-user call instead of once for the whole batch the way the true original loop did — negligible next to the ~13s/user Claude+Finnhub cost this measurement is actually about, but not hidden.

**5. Triggers reset in PLAN.md §6g — with real runway this time, and the assumed growth rate stated plainly, per explicit instruction.** New derived ceiling: **~109 eligible users** (300s / 2.744s-per-user throughput from the AFTER measurement) — HIGHER than `agent-thinker`'s own ~80 now that both are parallelized; the earlier "brief is the tighter ceiling" finding was entirely an artifact of the brief having no concurrency yet, not an intrinsic property of the job. Checked real signup history before choosing a growth-rate assumption rather than guessing: **7 real users over 60.5 days, ~0.116/day** — a pre-launch friends-and-family trickle that would put a naive-extrapolation warning trigger ~475 days out and give false comfort against exactly the scenario that prompted this whole line of questioning ("I could plausibly reach it in one afternoon of sharing the link"). Triggers instead sized against an assumed **aggressive post-launch rate of 5 newly-eligible users/day** (10x the historical rate, a deliberate stand-in for a real marketing push, not a forecast): **warning at 55 users (50% of ceiling, ~11 days of sustained growth from a launch event), action-required at 75 users (~69% of ceiling, ~15 days)** — both explicitly noted as measuring runway in DAYS under an aggressive scenario, not years, and worth re-checking specifically around any real launch/marketing event rather than only on the periodic HANDOFF cadence.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-19 (Agent-thinker/daily-brief incident, PART 4 — capacity ceilings derived and recorded as explicit triggers (PLAN.md §6g); Sentry recurring-issue alerting gap confirmed, fix recommended but HELD pending confirmation, per explicit instruction — investigation BUILT and VERIFIED, **NOT pushed, reporting first.** `14d3add` (PART 3, immediately below) **pushed to `origin/main` this round, on explicit instruction** — this entry's own work is a follow-up round on top of that, still local-only.

**1. "Concurrency divides growth, it doesn't remove it" — the exact challenge raised, answered with real numbers, not reassurance.** Both batch crons run inside a Vercel function bound by Hobby's fixed 300s ceiling. Derived both ceilings from the real measurements already taken (and one new one), recorded as explicit warning/action triggers in **PLAN.md §6g** (same "at what count do we act, and what do we do then" convention as §6c/§6d's flakiness triggers):
  - **agent-thinker (concurrency=5): ~80 eligible agents before a real risk of timing out** (300s / 3.692s-per-agent throughput, derived from the 48.0s/13-agent measurement). Warning at 40, action-required at 55 (raise concurrency further — safe past Finnhub's 6-concurrent cap since `RateLimiter.acquire()` queues rather than rejects, confirmed by reading `ratelimit.server.ts` — then Vercel Pro's 800s, then split the batch). Today's real count: 13 (16% of ceiling).
  - **daily-brief: ~24 eligible users — the TIGHTER ceiling, and confidently so** (a NEW real measurement, not extrapolated from the thinker's numbers: 15 throwaway users with real watchlist symbols → 185.5s, 61.8% of budget, 12.37s/user — `app/brief-capacity-timing.ts`, same throwaway-agent discipline as `thinker-concurrency-timing.ts`, kept off the `verify-*.ts` glob for the same reason). **This job currently has ZERO concurrency at all** — it was extracted to its own schedule but never parallelized, so its ceiling in absolute headcount is meaningfully lower than the thinker's despite a comparable per-item cost, and its eligibility bar is lower (any user who's ever added a watchlist symbol qualifies, not just funded agents). Warning at 8, action-required at 15 (apply the SAME shuffle+bounded-concurrency treatment already built and tested this incident — `batchUtils.ts`'s primitives, zero new code — as the obvious first lever). Today's real count, checked live: 5 (21% of ceiling).
  - **Confidence stated plainly, per instruction:** both numbers are extrapolated from ONE measurement each at one scale — small-N worker-pool overhead could make the real ceiling somewhat HIGHER than stated, while per-item cost growing with portfolio/watchlist size could make it LOWER; neither effect is isolated. Re-measure with the kept scripts whenever a trigger fires rather than trusting the extrapolation at the moment of action.

**2. Sentry recurring-issue alerting — investigated and the gap CONFIRMED, exactly as suspected. Reporting per explicit instruction; the code fix below is recommended but NOT YET APPLIED.**
  - **How `checkStaleHeartbeats()` reports today:** `staleness.server.ts` calls `captureServerError(new Error('[staleness] cron "${job.jobName}" is stale'), {...})` for each stale job. `captureServerError` (`sentry/server.ts`) calls `Sentry.captureException(error)` with only `scope.setExtra(...)` for context — **no `scope.setFingerprint(...)` call anywhere in this path.**
  - **Confirmed via Sentry's own grouping docs, not assumed:** Sentry's default grouping is **stack-trace-first** — "grouping is effectively based entirely on the stack trace" when one is present, considered even before the exception message. Since `new Error(...)` is constructed at the exact same line, in the exact same call chain, every single day a given job stays stale, the stack trace is byte-identical run to run. **This means a persistent staleness condition creates exactly ONE Sentry issue on day 1, and every subsequent day's detection becomes a new EVENT on that SAME issue, not a new issue** — and a project Alert Rule scoped to "when a NEW issue is created" (the default rule, confirmed live-received working this round) fires once, on day 1, then goes silent for as long as the condition persists. **Confirmed: this is exactly the silent-failure shape the whole incident was about, reintroduced one layer up, in the alerting layer meant to catch it.** Also confirmed changing the MESSAGE text alone (e.g. embedding `ageHours`) would NOT reliably fix this — grouping is stack-trace-first, so a differing message on an identical stack trace is not guaranteed to force a new issue.
  - **Recommended fix (not yet applied):** add an optional `fingerprint?: string[]` parameter to `captureServerError()` (default omitted → today's normal stack-trace grouping, unchanged for every other of the ~20 call sites, all genuine one-off bugs that SHOULD dedupe normally) and have `staleness.server.ts` pass an explicit **per-job, per-calendar-day fingerprint** (e.g. `["staleness", job.jobName, YYYY-MM-DD]`). This guarantees a fresh Sentry issue — and therefore a fresh "new issue" alert email — every calendar day the condition persists, while still deduping multiple checks within the SAME day (the snapshot cron the staleness check rides on only runs once daily) into one issue rather than spamming per-check. Confirmed `scope.setFingerprint(fingerprint: string[])` exists on the exact `scope` object `captureServerError` already has in hand (`@sentry/core`'s `Scope` type, `@sentry/node@^10.69.0` — the version already in `package.json`), so this is a small, additive change, not a new dependency or API surface. **Held pending your confirmation of this approach before touching code**, per this round's explicit instruction to report on the gap first.
  - **Also flagged, not part of the code fix:** you're scoping the Alert Rule to `environment=production` on the Sentry side (the received test alert was `environment=development`) — worth double-checking after that change that the rule's condition doesn't ALSO need to be told to fire on existing-issue reoccurrence (a "seen more times" / reoccurrence condition, configurable in the Sentry UI, independent of the code fix) as a belt-and-suspenders second layer, though the per-day fingerprint should make that unnecessary on its own.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-19 (Agent-thinker/daily-brief incident, PART 3 — the structural fix: daily brief moved off Vercel Cron entirely, thinker batch parallelized + made fair-by-construction, watchdog+brief heartbeats closed, cross-job staleness alerting wired through Sentry — BUILT, VERIFIED with real measured timing, **NOT pushed, reporting first per instruction.** Closes out the incident first surfaced two entries below ("48h stale heartbeat" → real root cause: Vercel Hobby's 300s function ceiling → two specific real users missing their brief on both affected days).

**1. Root cause of WHY it was the SAME two users, not random ones — confirmed and fixed at the source.** `runThinkerForAllAgents()`'s `agent_config` query had no `.order()` clause — Postgres returns rows in a stable-but-arbitrary order, so whoever landed last in that order was systematically first to be cut any time the batch ran out of budget. Not "occasional bad luck," but "these two people lose out every time," indefinitely, until fixed. Fixed at the root, not just incidentally by parallelizing: new `lib/agent/batchUtils.ts` (`shuffle` — Fisher-Yates, pure, swappable RNG for deterministic tests) reorders the queue every run, so any future partial run spreads its cost across a different set of users each time instead of the same two forever.

**2. Bounded concurrency, not `Promise.all`.** Same file's `mapWithConcurrency(items, concurrency, fn)` — a small worker-pool primitive (no new dependency; the codebase already avoids adding libraries for things this short), preserving each result at its original index regardless of completion order, isolating one item's failure from the rest. `runThinkerForAllAgents()` now shuffles the queue, then runs it through `mapWithConcurrency(..., 5, ...)` — concurrency=5 chosen and justified against all THREE ceilings that actually apply (not just providers'), documented in `cron.server.ts`'s header: Claude's own published floor (1,000 req/min even at the lowest tier) makes 5 concurrent effectively unconstrained; Finnhub's real exposure is only the rare per-agent "held symbol missing from the shared universe scan" fallback (the universe itself is ONE shared prefetch, not per-agent), so worst-case 5 concurrent fallback calls sits under `ratelimit.server.ts`'s own `RateLimiter(50, 6)` 6-concurrent cap with 1 slot of headroom to spare; and the app's own per-user abuse guard (`RATE_LIMITS.agentRun`, `check.server.ts`) doesn't apply to the cron path at all — confirmed by grep, it's only ever called from the manual "Run agent now" button.

**3. The daily brief moved OFF Vercel Cron entirely — its own GitHub Actions schedule (`.github/workflows/agent-brief.yml`, 22:00 UTC), same pattern the watchdog already used to sidestep Hobby's 2-cron-per-day cap.** New `lib/insights/cron.server.ts` (`handleDailyBriefRequest`) is now a fully separate endpoint (`/api/cron/agent-brief`), routed in `server.ts`, with the SAME `CRON_SECRET` auth every other cron endpoint uses. This takes the brief job completely out of `agent-thinker`'s shared 300-second Vercel Hobby budget — the actual fix for the incident, not a workaround around it.

**4. GitHub Actions' OWN silent-failure modes handled explicitly, not assumed away.** Two real, documented GitHub behaviors: (a) a scheduled workflow auto-disables after 60 days with zero repository activity; (b) scheduled run timing isn't guaranteed and can be delayed under platform load. Both manifest identically from a heartbeat's point of view — it just stops advancing — so ONE sufficiently generous staleness threshold per job, feeding the same alerting mechanism, covers both without separate detection logic (documented in `agent-brief.yml`'s own header and `staleness.server.ts`'s threshold-justification comment).

**5. New `lib/health/staleness.server.ts` — proactive cron-staleness alerting, routed through Sentry, piggybacked on the snapshot cron rather than becoming a 4th scheduler of its own.** `checkStaleHeartbeats()` runs right after `snapshot`'s own heartbeat write (`snapshots/endpoint.server.ts`, both success and error branches) — deliberately riding on the one job with a 100%-reliable daily heartbeat of its own, rather than adding a new scheduled job whose own reliability would need watching (the exact "closed one blind spot, opened another" failure this whole incident is about). Per-job thresholds, each justified: `agent-thinker`/`snapshot` 26h (daily Vercel Cron, fixed UTC time + drift buffer, matching `check.server.ts`'s existing constant); `agent-watchdog` 96h (GitHub Actions, market-hours only — must absorb a 3-day weekend without a false alarm); `daily-brief` 48h (GitHub Actions, daily — absorbs one delayed/missed run before alerting, catching a genuine 2-day gap, the exact shape of the original incident). Pure `findStaleJobs()` is unit-tested (see below); `checkStaleHeartbeats()` never throws, matching `heartbeat.server.ts`'s own contract, and reports each breach via the SAME `captureServerError()` pipeline every real production error already uses.

**6. Watchdog heartbeat closed — it had NONE before this (AGENT-AUDIT.md Part 8 backlog item).** `handleAgentWatchdogRequest()` now writes a `agent-watchdog` heartbeat (ok, including on a market-closed no-op — that IS the job succeeding, not a gap) and on error, the same blindness-by-construction this whole incident was about, closed for the last untracked cron.

**7. "Three schedulers now" — stated plainly as a real, ongoing cost, not hidden.** Vercel Cron (agent-thinker, snapshot) + GitHub Actions (agent-watchdog, and now daily-brief) — three places to check when something's wrong, three schedule definitions, one `CRON_SECRET` shared across three auth surfaces. Documented directly in `lib/insights/cron.server.ts`'s header as something to consolidate onto one platform once Vercel Pro removes the cron-count pressure that caused the split (Pro is being bought anyway for PLAN.md's commercial-use requirement, not as a fix for this) — not something to let grow to a fourth.

**8. Measured, not claimed — real before/after wall-clock timing (`thinker-concurrency-timing.ts`, kept in the repo but deliberately named OFF the `verify-*.ts` glob so it doesn't become a recurring 26-real-Claude-call cost on every suite run; rerun manually if the concurrency bound ever changes).** Built 13 throwaway `@example.org` agents — 13 chosen to match today's REAL production eligible-agent count, confirmed live (`agent_config` where `enabled=true and agent_cash>0`) before writing the script — funded, enabled, real Claude+Finnhub calls throughout (no `disableAi` shortcut; the whole point was measuring the real cost). Same shared universe prefetch reused for both runs to isolate the ONE variable being measured (loop shape):
  - **BEFORE (sequential loop — the exact shape `runThinkerForAllAgents` had prior to this fix): 252.4s** for 13 agents. This is the number that should reframe the whole incident: even WITHOUT the daily-brief job stacked on top, the thinker batch alone was already consuming **84% of Vercel Hobby's 300s hard ceiling** at today's real scale. The brief wasn't an unlucky bystander that happened to get killed — the batch itself was already close enough to the wall that any modest user growth would have started timing out the thinker on its own, brief or no brief.
  - **AFTER (real `runThinkerForAllAgents`, shuffle + concurrency=5): 48.0s** — **5.26x speedup**, using **16.0% of the 300s budget.** Zero errors across all 13 agents in the AFTER run.
  - Throwaway agents fully cleaned up (auth users deleted) after the run, success or failure — same test-account discipline as every other verify-*.ts script.

**9. Sentry issue → EMAIL delivery — investigated, not assumed. Honest limit stated up front: I have no Sentry dashboard/API access (only the write-side DSN `captureServerError()` uses), so this can't be directly confirmed from here — it needs a manual check on your end.** Per Sentry's own docs (confirmed via their alerts/notifications and issue-alert-config pages), whether a captured error's issue reaches an inbox depends on TWO separate gates, both of which have to be right — a project Alert Rule alone or a personal notification setting alone isn't sufficient:
  - **Gate 1 — a project Alert Rule with an EMAIL action must exist.** Every new Sentry project gets a default alert rule ("Send a notification for new issues"), but its action can be un-set or repointed to a different integration (Slack, etc.) without anyone noticing, and a rule's TRIGGER ("a new issue is created") is a separate configuration from its ACTION ("send an email") — having the trigger configured does not guarantee the action is email. **Check: Sentry → your project → Alerts → the issue alert rule list → open the "new issue" rule (or create one if it's missing) → confirm it has an action step reading something like "Send a notification to [Issue Owners / a specific email/team] via Email," not just Slack/Discord/webhook.**
  - **Gate 2 — YOUR personal account notification setting must not be muted, independent of the project rule.** Sentry's own docs describe workflow/alert emails as going only to "members who are subscribed" and gate delivery on the recipient's own notification preferences. **Check: Sentry → your user avatar → Settings → Notifications → confirm "Issue Alerts" (or the equivalent alert-email toggle) is ON, and that your account email address itself is verified.**
  - Recommendation: after confirming both, trigger a real test — either wait for `checkStaleHeartbeats()` to next find something stale, or manually break a heartbeat temporarily to force one — and confirm an actual email lands, since "the settings look right" and "an email actually arrived" are two different claims and this incident's whole thesis is not to conflate them.

**10. Fixed a stale comment while in this code**, `verify-hardening-cron-chain.ts`'s header and inline comments still described the daily brief as running INSIDE `agent-thinker`'s composition — corrected to describe the real, current architecture (thinker-only, brief as its own separate GitHub Actions step) rather than leave a script's own comments actively misleading about production reality, the same standard applied to the stale-brief-display UI fix earlier in this incident.

**11. New tests — 24 checks across two new files, both passing:** `lib/agent/batchUtils.test.ts` (`shuffle`: never mutates input, preserves the exact multiset including duplicates, empty/single-element pass through unchanged, a deterministic RNG produces a deterministic and exactly-verified Fisher-Yates permutation — not just multiset equality — real `Math.random()` usage still returns a valid permutation; `mapWithConcurrency`: preserves result order at each item's ORIGINAL index regardless of completion order, never exceeds the concurrency bound — measured live with an in-flight counter, one item throwing doesn't cancel or block the others, concurrency greater than item count still works, empty input short-circuits with zero `fn` calls, both item and index are passed through correctly) and `lib/health/staleness.test.ts` (no heartbeats at all flags every tracked job with `null` age; all-fresh flags nothing; every job's threshold boundary tested exactly AND one hour short — inclusive-boundary semantics deliberately chosen to match this codebase's existing `activityStatus.ts` convention, which required fixing `findStaleJobs`'s comparison from `>` to `>=`, caught by the boundary test itself; an untracked `job_name` in the heartbeats table is correctly ignored; `ageHours` is numerically accurate for a genuinely stale job).

**tsc --noEmit clean. `npx vitest run` 76/76 green (24 new). Full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite: 35/35 green, zero errors anywhere in the log. `npm run build` clean.** Also carries the already-complete, previously-uncommitted stale-brief-display fix from earlier in this same incident (`insights/api.ts`'s `getTodaysBrief` now returns `isToday`, `MarketBriefCard.tsx` shows a prominent amber banner instead of silently relabeling a stale brief as "Today's") — verified live in browser in the prior round, committed together here since it's part of the same incident response.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-19 (Config-drift tool fixed before its first real use exposed the flaw — BUILT, VERIFIED live against `mypapertrader.com`, **NOT pushed, reporting first per instruction.** `9bacfce` (yesterday's fingerprinting commit) pushed and deployed first, on explicit instruction, so this fix's local half could run against a real production `/api/health`.

**The flaw**: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was made deliberately per-environment yesterday — `papertrader-dev` locally, `papertrader-prod` in Vercel, the whole point of splitting dev/prod spend. Without a way to declare that, `check-config` would report it as a mismatch on every single run, forever, correctly and uselessly — the exact "detector drowned in its own noise" shape as the badly-written per-script flakiness trigger earlier in this project (PLAN.md §6d): a permanently-correct alarm trains the reader to stop reading it, so the one time it fires for a real reason, it's already been tuned out.

**The fix — an allowlist of exceptions, not a manifest**: `fingerprint.ts`'s new `EXPECTED_PER_ENVIRONMENT_VARS` (currently one entry: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`). Deliberately NOT the manifest-of-expected-values shape the 2026-08-17 assessment already rejected — that goes stale on every routine key rotation, and the person rotating a key has no reason to also be touching a manifest. This list only changes on a deliberate architecture decision to scope a credential per-environment (rare — happened once, yesterday), made by the same person who'd naturally also flip this flag while making that change, not a separate thing to remember afterward. Defaults to strict: anything NOT listed is expected to match, so a wrong classification fails safe (an unlisted-but-actually-fine divergence is a loud, self-correcting false alarm; something wrongly added to the list is the only way to accidentally suppress real drift — kept rare by keeping the list short and the bar for adding to it high).

**The disguised case, fixed and tested**: "expected to differ in VALUE" must never become "expected to be absent." Presence and value-equality are separate axes in `diffFingerprints` — a variable on the allowlist that's missing on either side is still `remote_missing`/`local_missing` (an unconditional problem), never silently folded into `expected_divergent`. Proved this isn't accidental: the reviewed logic checks presence FIRST, unconditionally, before ever consulting the allowlist.

**A real bug in the tool itself, found by actually running it against production**: `/api/health` returns HTTP 503 (by design — see `endpoint.server.ts`'s own header) whenever ANY other check fails, even though `config.secrets` is still present and valid. The script's `!res.ok` check treated 503 as fatal and refused to read the (perfectly good) fingerprint data underneath it — exactly the kind of thing "build it, then actually run it" catches that reasoning about it in the abstract doesn't. Fixed to accept 200 or 503 and reject only genuinely unexpected statuses.

**Real production run, actual output** (after the 503 fix — production was legitimately unhealthy at the time: `agent-thinker` cron 48h stale, unrelated to this work, not investigated here, flagging for awareness):
```
████ Config drift — local app/.env vs. production ████
  ✅ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY          present in both — values differ by design (per-environment)  (local=d3dec16b remote=926b4619)
  ✅ CRON_SECRET                match  (local=36e52c3a remote=36e52c3a)
  ✅ SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY  match  (local=84f9db60 remote=84f9db60)
  ✅ FINNHUB_API_KEY            match  (local=9f970a88 remote=9f970a88)
  ✅ TWELVEDATA_API_KEY         match  (local=605a35ef remote=605a35ef)

SUMMARY: 5/5 checked — 4 matches, 1 intentionally per-environment (confirmed present in both), no unexpected drift.
```
Also cleaned up the output shape while implementing this: the first working version printed every row twice (once from a manual `console.log`, once from `assert()`'s own line) — consolidated to one line per variable, exactly the "scan in three seconds" bar asked for.

**Tested**, including the case specifically requested: a variable on the expected-per-environment allowlist that's actually absent in production must fail loudly, not be silently waved through as "differs by design." Proved non-tautological the same way as yesterday's fingerprint tests: reordered the real logic to check the allowlist before presence (reintroducing the disguised-case bug), reran — 6 tests failed including the purpose-built disguised-case test, restored, all green again. 11 new/changed tests, 52 total in the suite now.

`tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npx vitest run` 52/52 green, full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite green.)

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-18 (Config-drift detection extended from presence to VALUE — BUILT, VERIFIED, **NOT pushed, reporting first per instruction.** Follow-up to the same-day incident writeup below: the recommended fingerprinting option is now real code, not just an assessment.

**The gap fingerprinting closes**: presence alone (`sentryServer: true`) only catches a variable being ABSENT. It says nothing when a variable is PRESENT but WRONG — exactly the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` incident below. A fingerprint that can be independently recomputed closes that.

**Two halves, built together so they can never compute the hash differently by accident**: `lib/health/fingerprint.ts`'s `computeFingerprints()` is called by both. (1) `check.server.ts` — `/api/health`'s `config.secrets` now reports an unsalted SHA-256 (first 8 hex chars) per high-entropy secret (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CRON_SECRET`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `FINNHUB_API_KEY`, `TWELVEDATA_API_KEY`). (2) `check-config-fingerprint.ts` (new, `npm run check-config`) computes the SAME fingerprints from local `app/.env`, fetches production's, and prints a per-secret ✅ match / ❌ mismatch diff — a fingerprint alone is an opaque string; this is what makes it an actual answer.

**Deliberately excluded from fingerprinting, and why — `AGENT_MODEL`**: it's config, not a secret, but critically it is also LOW-entropy (one of a handful of real Anthropic model names) — a truncated hash of a low-entropy value gives an attacker a free oracle (hash every candidate, compare) with no brute-force cost, so fingerprinting it would create a false impression of protection where none exists. Reported as a plain string instead, unchanged from the prior entry.

**Salting: considered, deliberately rejected.** Every fingerprinted value here is a long, randomly-generated secret already computationally infeasible to brute-force whether salted or not — salting defends against a precomputed-rainbow-table attack on a SMALL candidate space, which is the `AGENT_MODEL` risk above, not this one. Salting would also need the salt itself to be a new shared secret present in both `app/.env` and Vercel, i.e. a new value that could itself drift between the two and produce a false-positive mismatch unrelated to the actual secret changing — reintroducing the exact failure class this feature exists to eliminate, for no matching security benefit.

**The one thing keeping this safe to expose at all, documented directly in `fingerprint.ts`'s header, not just here**: `/api/health` is CRON_SECRET-authenticated. An unsalted hash of a high-entropy secret is safe against COLD brute force regardless of who can see it, but it is NOT safe against someone who already holds a candidate value from another leak and wants to confirm it's still live in production — for that attacker, an exposed fingerprint is a free, unlimited oracle. If `/api/health` is ever made public or given weaker auth, the fingerprints must be removed FIRST — the in-code comment says this explicitly so a future change doesn't discover the dependency the hard way.

**Tested**: fingerprint changes when the input changes (even by one character), is stable for the same input, is always exactly 8 lowercase hex characters, never contains the raw secret or any non-trivial substring of it, and returns `null` (not a hash) for an unset value so "not configured" can never collide with a real fingerprint. `diffFingerprints()` covers all five shapes: match, mismatch (the exact `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` incident shape — both present, different value), remote-missing (the exact Sentry incident shape — set locally, never reached the deploy), local-missing, and both-missing (correctly NOT flagged as drift). Proved the tests aren't tautological: reverted `fingerprint()` to a constant return, reran — 2 tests failed with exactly the expected reproduction, restored, all green again.

**Live proof, partial — reported honestly rather than overclaimed**: ran `npm run check-config` against production as it stands right now (this feature not yet deployed) — it correctly computed all 5 local fingerprints, reached `/api/health`, and failed with a clear, actionable message ("is production running the version of the code that reports fingerprints?") rather than crashing or silently misreporting a match. Full end-to-end proof — a real ✅ match against production's own computed fingerprints — needs this commit deployed first; the diff logic itself is proven by the unit tests above, which exercise the exact comparison this script performs.

`tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npx vitest run` 49/49 green (11 new). Full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite green.)

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-18 (Two production config-drift incidents found and closed same day — BUILT, VERIFIED live against `mypapertrader.com`. Committed locally — **NOT pushed** (the code fix itself WAS pushed and deployed on explicit instruction, mid-session; this HANDOFF/assessment commit is separate and held per the standing "report first" default).

**Incident 1 — Sentry inert in production for a week.** `SENTRY_DSN`/`VITE_SENTRY_DSN` were only ever set in local `app/.env`; production (Vercel) never had them, from 2026-08-10 (when Sentry was built and verified — locally only) until 2026-08-17. Error monitoring was completely inert in production the entire time despite being disclosed as live in `legal/privacy.md`. Found by direct inspection, not by any signal the app produced. Venky added both vars to Vercel (Production + Preview) and redeployed.

**Incident 2 — an intentional key rotation silently didn't apply.** Separately, Venky had already tried to update Vercel's `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to a new value; the update didn't take, and production kept running on the old key with no error, no warning, nothing — indistinguishable from success until this session's env-var audit (prompted by Incident 1) surfaced it. Fixed same session (Venky re-set it in Vercel). The old key was explicitly NOT revoked until this fix was confirmed live, in case production was still depending on it.

**Both incidents share one root cause: nothing ever verified that Vercel's actual configuration matched intent** — not "unset" (Incident 1) and not "unset OR wrong value" (Incident 2). `verify-sentry.ts` proved local delivery; nothing proved production delivery, ever, on any commit that touched this.

**Fixed structurally, verified live against `mypapertrader.com` (not assumed from the variable being present):**
- `check.server.ts`: new `config` section on every `/api/health` report — `sentryServer`/`sentryClient`/`agentModel`, deliberately separate from the pass/fail health gate (every field here is optional-by-design; folding it into `ok` would misrepresent "not configured yet" as "broken"). **Confirmed live:** `GET /api/health` against production now returns `"sentryServer": true, "sentryClient": true"` — matches Vercel's actual state.
- Two ways to fire a REAL, marked Sentry test event through the exact seam every genuine production error uses (`captureServerError`), so "is monitoring alive right now" is a repeatable live check, not a one-time proof that goes stale the next time the DSN changes: `POST /api/health?action=test-sentry` (CRON_SECRET-authenticated, scriptable) and an admin-console button (session-authenticated, for a human). **Confirmed live**: fired against production — `eventId=7f50f7fc244b4e7e8c6825415c5f5747`, `marker=health-endpoint-test-sentry-1787027398846`, `flushed=true`. Venky to independently confirm this event ID/marker lands in the Sentry dashboard.
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` specifically: **could not be confirmed from the app side**, and this is stated honestly rather than guessed — the value must never be exposed by any endpoint (including a fingerprint of it — see the assessment below for why that's the actual fix), so there is no safe app-side signal that distinguishes "the new key" from "the old key" while both remain valid. Venky to cross-check the Anthropic Console's Usage page in a day, as planned.

**Assessed, not built (explicit instruction) — extending `/api/health`'s config section to catch this class structurally, not just today's two instances:**

Three options considered for making config drift self-announcing:
1. **Presence + a safe fingerprint (short hash, not a raw substring) for every required env var, reported in `/api/health`'s existing `config` section.** Cost: low — the plumbing already exists from today's fix; adds a hash helper + a loop over the required-var list, no new infrastructure. **Recommended.** This is the only one of the three that would have caught BOTH of today's incidents with the same mechanism: presence catches Incident 1 (absent), a fingerprint catches Incident 2 (present but wrong value — presence alone would have said "set" and missed it). Must be a proper one-way hash (e.g. SHA-256, first 8 hex chars), never a raw substring like "last 4 characters" — a hash of a 100+-char random key is safe against reversal even in full; a raw substring leaks real entropy for no comparison benefit a hash doesn't already give. Already CRON_SECRET-gated (the HTTP endpoint) or admin-session-gated (the console) — same bar the rest of this surface already clears.
2. **A startup check that logs loudly when a required variable is absent.** Cost: low-medium. Rejected as the primary fix: catches Incident 1's shape (absent) but NOT Incident 2's (present, wrong value) — a presence-only check would have logged "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: set" the whole time and missed exactly the bug that happened today. Also push-based (a log line in Vercel's function logs) rather than pull-based (a page someone actually checks) — relies on someone watching, the same "remembering" failure this assessment exists to remove.
3. **A periodic comparison against a checked-in manifest of expected fingerprints.** Cost: medium-high — needs a manifest file, a scheduled job, and critically, a human updating the manifest on every legitimate key rotation. Rejected: this just moves the "someone has to remember" burden from "check Vercel matches local" to "update the manifest after rotating" — it's the most theoretically complete option but reintroduces the same class of failure it's meant to prevent, for real ongoing cost.

**The lesson, stated plainly:** local verification proves the code is correct; it says nothing about whether production's configuration matches what the code assumes. Every future "built and verified" claim for anything gated by a Vercel env var needs a live check against the deployed environment, not just a local one — `verify-sentry.ts`'s existing local proof was never wrong, it was just answering a narrower question than the one that mattered.

`tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npx vitest run` 38/38 green, full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite green.)

**2) Suite hangs made self-limiting — the third hang in this project's history.** `verify-suite.sh`'s glob-collision fix (previous entry) fixed the TRIGGER; this closes the actual gap: a script producing no output could stall the whole run indefinitely, with nothing but a human noticing the terminal went quiet. Added a per-script INACTIVITY timeout (300s / 5 minutes) — kills the whole process group (not just the immediate child; `set -m` job control + negative-PID kill, so no orphaned `vite-node`/`node` processes) and marks the script failed with a clear reason, run continues. **N=300s justified against the slowest known legitimate silent gap**: `verify-live-prices.ts` runs two real 120-second silent steps (a single before/after log line bracketing a 2-minute background poll) — 300s gives that ~2.5x headroom. Proved BOTH directions live, not assumed: a synthetic script that hangs forever with zero output gets killed at exactly 10s under a shortened test timeout, cleanly, no orphaned processes; a synthetic script that fails fast still reports "non-zero exit" correctly; the normal happy path (two real scripts) still streams live output and passes as before.

Already named as exactly this under Phase C2's own "churn" line. Recommended for after launch, scoped alongside C2's admin revenue reporting, not as a standalone pre-launch task — unlike the agent status line, there's no one to serve until there's a real user base, and turning the detection into something useful is genuine product work (copy, send infra, segmentation), not a read-query-plus-a-sentence.

**Hard constraint carried over from Part 8's own finding**: we can distinguish "correctly holding" from "stuck" only for the ONE failure mode already patched (the underfunded flag) — for anything else, we cannot tell. Every string this feature produces is therefore a FACT (when the agent last acted, what it last decided, when it runs next), never a health verdict ("healthy," "on track," "nothing to worry about"). This rule is written directly into `activityStatus.ts`'s and `AgentActivityStatusLine.tsx`'s header comments, and a component test (`REASSURANCE_WORDS` regex) fails the suite if reassurance language is ever reintroduced — proved this by temporarily injecting "Everything's fine"/"your agent is healthy" into the component and confirming 5/6 and then all 6 tests failed, before reverting.

**`lib/agent/activityStatus.ts` (new, pure, no I/O)** — the one shared computation both the status line and the admin list use, so "idle" can't mean two different things in two places. `computeAgentActivityStatus(decisions, now)` reads ONLY `agent_decisions.action` — `buy`/`trim`/`sell` are the only three values that correspond to a real trade; `rebalance`/`watchdog`/`hold` are narrative/no-op entries — deliberately NOT the `agent_run` analytics event, since Part 8 §1 found that event proves the cron cycle completed, not that anything happened. Two thresholds, justified in Part 8 §4 and carried as exported constants (`NEVER_TRADED_IDLE_DAYS = 3`, `WENT_QUIET_DAYS = 14`): a never-funded-into-a-position agent is flagged sooner (both real aggressive agents placed their first trade within a day or two) than a previously-invested one gone quiet (a calm market can legitimately leave a diversified, at-target portfolio untouched for a while — the drift band doing its job, not a malfunction). `nextAgentThinkerRunIso()` computes the next `30 21 * * *` (vercel.json) occurrence as a real instant.

**`AgentActivityStatusLine.tsx` (new) — always visible on `/app/agent`** once the agent has ever been funded (gated on `allocated_total > 0`, not current cash/holdings, so it still reports honestly after a full withdrawal). Three fact rows, every time shown via `formatInstant` (viewer's local zone, per the existing datetime.ts convention): Last trade (or "None yet — first run was N days ago" / "The agent hasn't run yet" for the two pre-trade states), Last decision (the agent's own most recent logged rationale, quoted verbatim — not synthesized), Next scheduled run. Absence of activity is never ambiguous: the user always sees the LAST thing the agent actually said, even when that thing is "nothing to do."

**Admin idle-agents list (`app.admin.index.tsx` + `getIdleAgentsFn`)** — funded (`allocated_total > 0`), enabled agents past either threshold, reusing the exact same `computeAgentActivityStatus`/`isIdle`. Adding `shrink-0` to all three fixed the width — which then exposed a SECOND real issue: the header started genuinely overflowing its own box by 14px, because the search box's wrapper had no `min-w-0`, so its `flex-1` couldn't shrink past the `<input>`'s own browser-intrinsic minimum content width. Fixed with `min-w-0` on the wrapper — the standard fix for exactly this flexbox-plus-input gotcha. **Verified end to end, not just by class inspection**: measured every fixed control's real `getBoundingClientRect()` at 375px on a throwaway signed-in account — hamburger/theme/avatar all exactly 44×44, `header.scrollWidth === header.clientWidth` (no overflow) at 375, 430, and 1280px (desktop confirmed unaffected — no visible crowding, screenshotted). `WatchlistStar` measured 44×44 live on Markets (8 real rows), row height 69px (comfortably taller, no layout disruption). Watchlist's remove button measured 44×44 on a real added ticker (TSLA). The search "Clear" button's pseudo-element computed to `inset: -12px -8px` as intended, expanding a 30×32 visible box to a ~46×56 effective tap target. Mobile-nav overlay's close button measured 44×44 with the panel genuinely open.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-17 (Closed the verification gap that let "Invalid Date" and the blank donut ship through a green suite — BUILT, VERIFIED. Committed locally — **NOT pushed, reporting first per instruction.** **Honest diagnosis first**: the 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite verifies data and server logic end-to-end (real Supabase writes, real RPC results, real balance math) and never once asserts on RENDERED OUTPUT — no test reads a formatted string, a chart's DOM, or a component's visible text. That's exactly the shape of both bugs that slipped through: `formatCalendarDate`'s "Invalid Date" and the donut's intermittent blank render are both pure client-side rendering failures with correct underlying data — invisible to a suite that only checks the data. **Closed the cheapest, highest-value slice of that gap, not a full browser suite** (explicitly not built, per instruction — see the cost estimate below instead). **Introduced `vitest`** (new dev dependency, `npx vitest run` / `npm test`; standalone `vitest.config.ts`, deliberately NOT touching the managed `vite.config.ts` wrapper) — the first real unit-test framework in this codebase, chosen because `lib/format/datetime.ts` is pure, dependency-free logic with zero DB/network/auth coupling, exactly what a fast unit-test layer is for, unlike the live-data `verify-*.ts` scripts.
  - **`src/lib/format/datetime.test.ts` — 14 tests covering every exported function**, including the EXACT input shape that broke production: a full ISO instant string (`"2019-06-03T00:00:00.000Z"`, a daily provider candle's `t`) fed into `formatCalendarDate`. Timezone-dependent tests mutate `process.env.TZ` per-assertion (Node's ICU re-reads it on every call) rather than trusting the machine's default zone, matching the technique already used for this project's earlier live `TZ=...` checks. **Proved the tests actually catch the regression, not just pass by coincidence**: temporarily reverted `formatCalendarDate` to its pre-fix body (`` `${dateOnly}T00:00:00Z` ``, no `.slice(0,10)`), reran — 2 of 14 failed with the literal reproduction (`expected 'invalid date' not to contain 'invalid'` / `expected 'Invalid Date' to be 'Dec 25, 2024'`), the other 12 (functions/paths the revert didn't touch) still passed correctly — then restored the fix and confirmed all 14 green again. This is the shape of proof asked for: fails on the broken code, passes on the fix, isolates exactly the regressed behavior.
  - **Audited every remaining call site of the five formatting functions app-wide** (~25 call sites across ~18 files) against what value each one actually receives, not just what the function signature claims. Confirmed correct: `PortfolioValueChart`/`ScenarioChart`/`app.scenarios.*` (`portfolio_snapshots.captured_at`, `scenario_transactions.sim_date` — genuine Postgres `date` columns), option expiry (`chain.server.ts`'s `isoDate()` helper already slices to bare date at the source), `MarketBriefCard`'s `createdAt` (`insights.created_at`, also a genuine `date` column, confirmed against the migration), transaction/decision/audit logs and `InsightUI`'s footer (all genuine `timestamptz` columns or JSONB-embedded `toISOString()` instants), the mock `PriceChart`'s own generated instants, and the Finnhub-sourced earnings-date/analyst-period fields (third-party API values, documented by Finnhub as bare dates — now doubly protected regardless, since the fix's `.slice(0,10)` tolerates either shape). **Found a THIRD real bug this same audit turned up, previously unknown, now fixed**: `JournalEntryCard.tsx` rendered `journal_entries.entry_date` (confirmed via `0023_journal.sql`: `entry_date date not null default current_date` — a genuine calendar date, its own type comment in `supabase/types.ts` even says so) through `formatInstantDate`, the VIEWER-LOCAL instant formatter, instead of `formatCalendarDate`. Since a bare date string parses as UTC midnight and `formatInstantDate` has no UTC pin, this silently rendered the journal entry's date ONE DAY EARLY for any viewer west of UTC — the mirror-image failure mode of the "Invalid Date" bug (too little information fed to the wrong formatter, instead of too much), and arguably worse: it never crashes or shows an obviously-wrong string, so nothing would ever have looked broken to catch it by eye. Fixed by splitting `JournalEntryCard`'s two different date needs apart: `outcome.asOf` (a real transaction's `created_at`, correctly stays `formatInstantDate`) vs. `entry.entry_date` (now correctly `formatCalendarDate`), with a comment explaining why they're not interchangeable despite both being "a date shown on a journal card."
  - **Assessed `NumberInput`/`parseNumberInput` and `sector.ts`'s `isLikelyFund`/`displaySector` for the same "wrong-shaped input silently produces garbage" pattern — audited, not found.** `parseNumberInput` already returns `null` (not a garbage number) for anything that isn't a real value; `NumberInput` never round-trips through `Number()` mid-typing, only sanitizes character-by-character, so it can't produce a "NaN"-style rendered artifact the way a formatter can. Traced every `NumberInput` call site's initial `useState` and every later `setXInput(String(...))` call across all 6 consumer files for the one plausible equivalent risk — a `String(undefined)` → literal `"undefined"` in a field — and found none: every initializer is a hardcoded literal, and the one dynamic case (`limitInput` seeded from a live price) is guarded by `quote?.price ?? 0`, never `undefined`. `sector.ts`'s two functions take a required `sector: string` (not optional) and gate `ok` with strict `=== false`, with an explicit fallback label ("Unclassified"/"Unavailable") on every branch — already the exact honest-degradation discipline this whole audit is about, not a gap. **Genuine, honest conclusion: this specific failure class doesn't reach these two utilities** — reported rather than manufactured to justify more changes.
  - **Cost of a fuller UI-level safety net, estimated, not built:** (1) **Component/rendering tests** (Testing Library + jsdom, layering on the `vitest` now installed) — render `InsightCard`/`SimulatorPanel`/`DonutCard` in isolation with mocked TanStack Query/Router/Supabase-auth context and assert on the actual rendered text/DOM. Setup: ~2–4 hours for the mocking harness plus one working example (the app's heavy real-auth/live-server-function coupling makes the harness the real cost, not the framework). Per-test: ~15–30 min once the harness exists. Run time: negligible, well under 30s even at 50+ tests — this WOULD have caught both the "Invalid Date" text and (with an explicit `<path>`-count assertion) the blank donut, since both are visible in jsdom's rendered output. (2) **Full browser E2E** (Playwright, driving the real dev/preview server against seeded throwaway accounts, reusing this project's existing `verify-harness.ts` test-user tooling) — setup: ~1–2 days for a working harness + CI wiring + one flow; per-flow: ~30–60 min given the app's real-data seeding needs; run time: minutes, not seconds, likely comparable to or slower than the current `verify-*.ts` suite's own ~10–15 min, since it drives a real browser against real (seeded) live-provider data. This tier is the only one that would have caught the ORIGINAL mobile header-reflow bug too, since jsdom doesn't lay out or paint. **Recommendation: component/rendering tests next, if/when this becomes a priority again — not Playwright yet.** They directly close the exact gap this session found (rendered-output correctness) at a fraction of the setup and per-run cost; E2E is the right eventual answer for layout/visual/animation-timing bugs specifically, but is the bigger commitment the user explicitly said not to take on now.
