**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-17 (Mobile responsive audit — BUILT, VERIFIED. `MOBILE-AUDIT.md` written and committed. **PUSHED to origin/main (`e066aec`).** **Triggering bug, fixed**: `/app/stock/$symbol`'s header (`app.stock.$symbol.tsx:133`) used `grid grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto] ... sm:flex sm:flex-wrap` — a forced 2-column grid as the MOBILE-WIDTH base class, only switching to a wrap-capable flex row at `sm:` (640px) and up. Backwards: the non-stacking layout was the default, the reflow-capable one was the override. At 375px this squeezed the identity block (logo/symbol/sector-chip/name) and the price block into two narrow columns, truncating the symbol itself to "NV…" and colliding the price/change/market-status lines against the identity block — exactly the reported symptom. Fixed by flipping the direction: `flex flex-col` (stacked) as the base, `sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap sm:justify-between` from 640px up; price block `text-left sm:text-right`; `MarketStatusBadge`'s alignment likewise `justify-start sm:justify-end`. Desktop row layout confirmed unchanged. **Full sweep, every authenticated route + landing/auth/legal, at 375/390/430px** (live browser, signed in on a throwaway seeded account with real holdings/watchlist/transactions built through actual trades, not synthetic rows) — established that 375/390/430 render identically almost everywhere in this codebase, since Tailwind's first breakpoint is `sm:640px` and nothing in the app uses a custom breakpoint or fixed pixel width inside that range; confirmed this empirically rather than assuming it (zero horizontal overflow found at any width, on any route, before or after fixes). **Two more genuine tier-(a) bugs found during the sweep, neither mobile-specific but both caught here and fixed**: (1) Portfolio's allocation donut chart (`DonutCard`, `app.portfolio.tsx`) intermittently rendered a completely blank ring — zero Recharts `<path>` elements — on a fresh page load, roughly half the time, at any viewport width; root-caused to a known Recharts `ResponsiveContainer`+`Pie` mount-timing race (the animation state machine computing its initial geometry against a stale/zero measurement and never self-correcting once real data arrives). Fixed with `isAnimationActive={false}` on the `<Pie>`, verified clean across 3 consecutive fresh reloads. (2) The What-If Simulator's chart showed "Invalid Date" on its X-axis and the Stock Detail price chart's tooltip showed "Invalid Date" on hover (any range but 1D) — a real regression from the PREVIOUS entry's `formatCalendarDate` utility: two call sites (`SimulatorPanel.tsx`, `LivePriceChart.tsx`) pass it `SimPoint.t`/`Candle.t`, which are full ISO instant strings (`new Date(v.datetime).toISOString()` in `provider.server.ts`), not the bare `YYYY-MM-DD` the function assumed — appending `T00:00:00Z` onto an already-complete ISO string produced an invalid date string. Every other `formatCalendarDate` call site from the prior entry was re-checked and confirmed correct (genuine Postgres `date` columns: `portfolio_snapshots.captured_at`, `scenario_transactions.sim_date`). Fixed at the shared layer, not per call site: `formatCalendarDate` now does `dateOnly.slice(0, 10)` before parsing — a no-op for already-bare dates, correct for full-ISO daily-candle timestamps either way. Live-verified both fixes: Simulator ticks now read real dates ("Jul 21"/"Dec 23"/"Aug 26"); Stock Detail's 1Y tooltip now reads "Feb 18, 2026" against a real hovered point. **Tier-(b)/(c) reported, not fixed, per instruction**: icon-button tap targets under 44×44px app-wide (hamburger 31×36, theme toggle 31×36, watchlist star toggles 32×32 — all genuinely tappable, just under the comfortable-touch-target guideline; recommended a follow-up padding pass, scoped separately since it touches many small independent components); the Simulator's linear-scale chart visually flattens early growth for extreme-return stocks like NVDA's ~6,632% window (a chart-readability characteristic, not a defect); a cosmetic "ACTIONS" header-spacing tightness on the Watchlist table at 375px. **Admin console pages reviewed via source only** — `is_admin` has no client OR service-role write path by design (confirmed via `0026_admin_console.sql` and the existing `verify-admin-live.ts`, which proves a client-side write attempt is rejected by Postgres itself; only settable by direct SQL), so there's no test-safe way to grant admin on a throwaway account; code review found no instance of the grid-never-stacks anti-pattern and no un-wrapped tables in any of the four admin pages, stated as a code-review conclusion rather than a live-screenshotted one. **Prevention, documented in `MOBILE-AUDIT.md`**: the "identity left/numbers right" convention (mobile: `flex-col`, both blocks left-aligned; `sm:` and up: `flex-row justify-between`, numbers right-aligned) is now written down since nothing owned it before this shipped; the app's existing spacing rhythm (`gap-3`/`gap-4` inline, `gap-6` between sections) was confirmed consistent everywhere else, no new scale introduced; did NOT extract a shared header component since Stock Detail is currently the only page with this exact shape — matching this session's established practice of building shared abstractions on a real second consumer, not preemptively. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite green. Throwaway seeded test account and its scripts deleted after use.)

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-17 (Insight-button investigation — INVESTIGATION ONLY, no code/behaviour change. Committed locally as documentation — **NOT pushed, reporting first per instruction, AWAITING A DECISION before any code changes.** Re-opened the "insight timestamp looks wrong" question from the entry below after the user pointed out it had been asked incorrectly the first time: the earlier diagnosis proved the timestamp *renders* accurately, but never checked whether it *should* be reading as stale — "Generated Aug 15 at 10:29 PM" viewed around midday Aug 16 is a perfectly truthful label if the insight only refreshes once a day. So the real question was traced instead: when a user clicks the insight card's control, does it genuinely produce a NEW insight, or return the existing cached one? **Traced the real request path.** There is no separate "refresh"/"regenerate" control anywhere in the UI at all — `InsightCard` (`app.stock.$symbol.tsx`) has exactly one button before any insight is shown ("Get AI insight", fires once per page mount) and one more shown ONLY on an error state ("Try again", a plain `refetch()`). Both funnel into the same `getStockInsight(symbol)` (`insights.server.ts`), which is cached **GLOBALLY per symbol per UTC calendar day, not per user**: a durable DB check (`insights` table, `kind='stock'`, `symbol`, `created_at = today`) returns the existing row and exits *before* the A2 rate-limit guard or the Claude call are ever reached. So on any day after the first person (any user, anywhere) has already viewed a given symbol, every subsequent click of "Get AI insight" for that same symbol — by that same user or any other — returns the byte-identical cached payload, `generatedAt` included; zero new Claude work happens. **Conclusion: the timestamp was never lying. The surrounding copy is what misleads.** The pre-click tagline ("generated *on demand* and refreshed daily") and the post-load footer ("refreshes daily") both read as "your interaction produces something fresh, and it'll refresh again for you tomorrow" — when the true mechanism is "the FIRST view of the day, by anyone, globally, produces one Claude call; every other view that day, by anyone, sees that exact frozen result, correctly time-stamped to when it was actually made." This is precisely the failure mode the user hypothesized in option (a): an honest cache behind a control whose copy implies more than it does. **Recommendation: (a) — make the control honestly reflect what it does, not (b).** Concretely, not yet shipped: drop "generated on demand" from the pre-click tagline (misleading given the shared-cache reality); replace the vague "refreshes daily" footer line with a concrete "Next refresh at {time}" computed from the next UTC-midnight cache-key rollover and rendered in the viewer's own local zone via the `formatInstant` utility built in the previous entry — telling the user precisely when new content will exist instead of implying their own click controls it. This is a pure copy/label change: the underlying cache-then-freeze mechanism is untouched, so it carries no new Claude-call cost and needs no new rate limit. **Did not implement this — explicitly holding per instruction ("tell me your recommendation before changing behaviour") until the user confirms (a) over (b).** For the record, in case (b) — genuine on-demand regeneration — is preferred instead: current config is `agentModel()` (`AGENT_MODEL` env var, defaults `claude-sonnet-4-6`), `max_tokens: 1500`, one cached (`ephemeral`) system prompt plus per-call user content of one symbol's quote/metrics, up to 8 recent news headlines, and optional measured-history stats — on the order of a few hundred input tokens beyond the cached system prompt. `RATE_LIMITS.insight` (burst 10/5min, 50/day per user — `lib/rateLimit/check.server.ts`) was explicitly designed around "at most one real Claude call per symbol per day, globally," i.e. it assumes cache hits are free; genuine on-demand regeneration breaks that assumption and could multiply real spend by however many times a popular symbol gets manually refreshed in a day. Deliberately not quoting a specific dollar figure — didn't want to guess at currently-billed per-token rates for the exact configured model rather than confirm them first. Not proceeding with (b) without that cost confirmed and a rate limit agreed, per instruction.)

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-17 (Quote-failure follow-ups + timestamp diagnosis/sweep + orphan-arrow fix, BUILT, VERIFIED. Issues #44/#45 filed and In Progress. **PUSHED to origin/main (`7cc088d`).** **Follow-up A — explicit `ok` field replaces the `price>0` heuristic.** `isLoadedQuote()` on Markets inferred "did this quote load?" from `price > 0` — the same class of bug as the numeric-input fix above (a value standing in for a state), and wrong for a halted/delisted/pre-IPO symbol that can legitimately fetch successfully and report a real $0. Added an explicit `Quote.ok: boolean`, set at both construction sites (`providerQuotes()`'s post-`Promise.allSettled` failure placeholder, and `getQuotesFn`'s guaranteed-entry-for-every-symbol fallback) — a failed fetch is now self-describing instead of inferred. `isLoadedQuote()` now just reads `q.ok`. Deliberately did NOT keep `price>0` as a secondary check, since re-adding it would reintroduce the exact misclassification the fix exists to prevent. **Follow-up B — audited every consumer of `isLikelyFund()`/`displaySector()`.** Found three, not the two originally suspected: Markets chips, Portfolio donut/table (the one the user was worried about), and a previously-unnoticed third — Stock Detail's `AboutCard`, which also gates its fund-vs-stock branch on the same helper and would have shown the same wrong "Exchange-traded fund" UI for a failed quote. Centralized the `ok`-gate in `sector.ts` (`isLikelyFund`/`displaySector` both return the honest "Unavailable"/false path when `ok === false`) so all three consumers got the fix without individual patches. **Live-queried real production holdings** (read-only, service-role) to answer the user's direct question: 4 distinct real symbols currently held across all users (AAPL, VOO, NVDA, AMZN) — **0 of 4 currently fail to load**, so no real user's portfolio chart is showing a distorted breakdown as of this check. Caveat stated explicitly: this can't retroactively prove no *past* distortion occurred, since no `ok`-equivalent signal existed before this fix. **Issue 1 — AI insight timestamp diagnosis (issue #44).** Reported symptom: `/app/stock/NVDA`'s insight footer looked several hours off. Checked all four user-specified hypotheses with concrete evidence, not assumption: (a) naive-column coercion — ruled out, `generatedAt` lives inside a `jsonb` payload column, immune to column-level tz coercion; (b) missing-`Z` local-parse footgun — ruled out, `generatedAt` is always written via `toISOString()`, confirmed against real `insights` rows; (c) SSR-vs-client mismatch — ruled out empirically, `curl` of the raw SSR HTML for `/app/stock/NVDA` shows this app's authenticated routes don't meaningfully SSR business content (generic loading shell only, real content mounts client-side post-auth); (d) stale timestamp on regeneration — ruled out, traced `writeInsightRow`/`getStockInsight` and confirmed `generatedAt` is freshly written on every regeneration. **Could not reproduce the reported offset** — a live test (sign in as a throwaway user with a confirmed non-UTC zone, view a real generated insight, compare rendered text to the raw DB value) showed the current code renders correctly. Reporting this honestly rather than force-fitting an explanation. **The sweep, regardless**: no single canonical formatting utility existed, so every call site was free to make its own (potentially wrong) instant-vs-calendar-date choice. Added `lib/format/datetime.ts` distinguishing **INSTANT** (real UTC timestamp — always render in the viewer's local zone via `toLocaleString(undefined,...)`) from **CALENDAR DATE** (bare day with no real time component — option expiry, portfolio snapshot day, scenario dates, market brief's covered day — always pinned to UTC so the date never shifts a day for viewers west of UTC). Migrated ~20 files: insight footer, transaction history (Portfolio, Stock Detail), agent decision log, margin event log, admin console (users list/detail, audit log), portfolio value chart, scenario dates, option expiry (chain/order panel/positions list), What-If simulator chart, market brief date, journal entries, mock-data sparkline fallback. Market-hours logic (`isUsMarketOpen`, always America/New_York via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`) deliberately untouched — confirmed already correct, explicitly out of scope per instruction. **Verified**: a deterministic test drove the same UTC instant through `TZ=America/Chicago`, `TZ=Asia/Kolkata`, `TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati` (UTC+14), and `TZ=Etc/GMT+12` in one process — correct local-shifted output every time; calendar dates rendered identically regardless of viewer offset. **Issue 2 — orphan arrow (issue #45).** The Reset-paper-account modal's "Cash balance" row read `→ $25,000.00` while every sibling row (`flex justify-between` pattern) had no arrow and no "current" value was ever shown anywhere in the list — confirmed genuine leftover, not an intentional "current → new" design. Fixed by removing the arrow. **Verified**: screenshotted the modal at 375px in both light and dark theme — plain `$25,000.00`, consistent with every other row. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite green.)

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-16 (Markets follow-ups + numeric-input leading-zero bug app-wide, BUILT, VERIFIED. Issue #43 filed and In Progress. Committed locally — **NOT pushed, reporting first per instruction.** **Follow-ups on the search/sector work (edab0df)**: (1) the sector-chip row's `!active &&` block previously rendered only the "All" chip while `quotes` was still loading, then popped in 5 more chips once quotes resolved — a real, structural reflow risk (was directly observed earlier in this session's own screenshots: "All" alone, then a 1.5s-later jump). Fixed by always rendering "All" plus a fixed-width `animate-pulse` skeleton row (`SECTOR_CHIP_SKELETON_WIDTHS`) reserving the row's final shape until quotes load, so the swap-in no longer changes the row's width/wrap under the user's cursor. (2) Confirmed the actual mechanism for a symbol whose quote fails to load: `getQuotesFn` (functions.ts) guarantees a Map entry for every requested symbol even on failure — a zeroed placeholder (`price: 0, sector: "—"`), never an absent key — so `quotes.has(sym)` is always true post-load regardless of success. Combined with `displaySector()`'s own blank-profile heuristic, that placeholder was silently mislabeled "ETFs & funds" (worse than disappearing — an actively wrong label, verified with a deterministic pure-logic script: `displaySector(zeroedQuote) === "ETFs & funds"` was `true`). Chose **honest degradation over a curated-taxonomy fallback**: added `isLoadedQuote(q) = q.price > 0` (real market prices are never exactly $0) as the true "did this load" signal, gating both sector-chip generation (a failed fetch never contributes a chip) and the row's Sector/Price cells (show "…", matching the existing loading-cell convention, instead of a wrong or blank label) — explicitly rejected falling back to `lib/mockData`'s curated sector for a failed symbol because that reintroduces the exact taxonomy-mismatch class of bug (issue #42) this session already fixed once. (3) Got past the Options page's onboarding quiz gate (3 questions, answered correctly) on the throwaway account and confirmed the "Browse chains" search box: `boxShadow: none`, `border: 0px` — clean, matching the other 5 already-verified locations.
  - **Numeric input leading-zero bug, app-wide (issue #43)** — the exact mechanism the user hypothesized, confirmed: fields like the Simulator amount and agent funding held a NUMBER in state (`useState(5000)`) with `onChange={(e) => setAmount(Number(e.target.value) || 0)}` — clearing yields `""`, `Number("") === 0` (not `NaN`), so the fallback silently rewrote "0" back into the field on the next render; confirmed live that the app's own `onChange` genuinely could not drive the DOM value to `""` — only a raw bypassing `Event('input')` dispatch could. A second, related population of fields (main trade qty/amount, margin repay, scenario qty) already held STRING state (so they *could* clear) but never normalized "05" → "5", and disabled their submit button with no visible reason. **Audited every numeric input in `app/src`** (8 fields, 6 files): Simulator amount, agent fund/withdraw amount, stock trade quantity + dollar amount + limit price, margin repay, scenario trade quantity, option contract count stepper. Admin console audited and confirmed to have zero numeric inputs. **Fixed at a shared layer**, the same approach as `SearchInputBox`: extracted `NumberInput` (`components/ui/number-input.tsx`) — holds the raw string always (so `""` is representable), coerces to a number only at submit/validation time via a new `parseNumberInput()` (returns `null`, not `0`, for empty input — replacing every `Number(x) || 0` call site that conflated "nothing entered" with "entered zero"), normalizes leading zeros in real time while preserving a deliberate bare "0" and an in-progress "0.5"/"0.", tracks cursor position by *characters from the end* so normalization (which only ever strips from the front) never jumps the cursor, and renders `type="text" inputMode="decimal|numeric"` rather than native `type="number"` (sidesteps that control's own empty/scroll/`e`-key inconsistencies, and surfaces the correct iOS keypad — decimal vs. whole-number — per field). Per-field precision preserved/matched to existing intent: 0dp for option contracts and agent funding (unchanged), 2dp for dollar amounts/prices, 6dp for share quantities (matching the main trading engine's existing fractional-share precision). Migrated all 8 fields; upgraded every migrated Trade/Run/Repay button from silently-disabled to disabled **with a visible reason** ("Enter an amount.", "Enter a quantity.") computed live.
  - **Server-side confirmation, the more serious question — checked, no gap found.** `executeTradeFn` (`quantity`/`amount`: `z.number().positive()`), margin repay (`z.number().positive()`), scenario trade (`z.number().positive()`), option trade (`contracts: z.number().int().positive()`), and the Simulator (`z.number().positive().max(1e9)`) all reject zero/negative before their handlers run. `fundAgentFn`'s Zod schema only requires `.finite()` (negative is legitimate — it's how a withdrawal is represented) but the underlying `fund_agent` Postgres RPC explicitly `raise exception 'invalid_amount'` on `p_amount = 0` and bounds-checks the magnitude against the real balance. Every money-moving path was already safe against a malicious or buggy client independent of this fix.
  - **Verified with real evidence.** For the migrated fields: live-drove the DOM to a genuinely empty string and confirmed the app's own React state (not a bypass) kept it empty, showed "Enter an amount."/"Enter a quantity." and disabled the submit button; typed "0" then "5" and watched it normalize to "5" in real time (no page reload, no blur needed); typed "12.5" and confirmed no cursor jump; confirmed `inputMode="decimal"` renders correctly at 375px. Deterministic pure-logic script proved the pre-fix `displaySector()` mislabeling and its fix. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, full 35-script `verify-*.ts` suite green.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-16 (AI Portfolio Agent — Tier-1 audit fixes, BUILT, VERIFIED on throwaway accounts only. PUSHED to origin/main (`b658c54`). Consider funding at least $X") instead of the generic "no trades needed" a healthy at-target agent would ALSO show — plus a new amber banner on `/app/agent` surfacing the same signal, since a buried decision-log line wasn't the point; the whole bug was that "can't afford anything" and "healthy" looked identical. **2 — membership stickiness (issue #39)**: new `MIN_HOLDING_DAYS = 5` — a position the thinker opened can't be fully EXITED by its own "no longer in target" logic until held ≥5 days; scoped narrowly to full exits only (drift-band trims and watchdog protective sells are untouched — both are genuine risk-control paths, not membership noise).   - **Verified with real evidence, throwaway accounts only — no real user's agent touched.** Fractional trading: a throwaway agent funded at the EXACT real affected user's scenario ($1,000, balanced, quant-only for determinism) went from the historically-observed "0 trades, ever" to 4 real fractional-quantity trades (0.41/0.43/0.29/0.27 shares), $850 of $1,000 meaningfully deployed, every position confirmed within its 30% cap. Weighted-average cost re-verified exactly correct with a fractional add (`537.360667` computed by hand === `537.360667` returned by the RPC); a full fractional exit confirmed clearing the holdings row to exactly zero, no dust remainder. Underfunded signal: a $10 throwaway balanced agent correctly executed zero trades, logged the distinguishable message (not "no trades needed"), with `signals.underfunded=true` and a computed `suggested_min_funding=$25`. Membership stickiness: measured on a PURE, deterministic seeded simulation (`planRebalance` called directly, no database) modeled on the real observed AMZN oscillation pattern (real re-entry gaps: 1.00/1.02/1.03/2.00/3.00/5.00/6.02 days) — **before: 13 total transactions / 5 round trips; after: 9 total transactions / 3 round trips — a 30.8% reduction in total trades and 40.0% reduction in round trips**, both measured, not asserted. Cost tracking: the real cron batch path (`runThinkerForAllAgents({onlyUserId})`, scoped to a throwaway agent — the identical function the daily Vercel cron calls in production) was confirmed to write a real `agent_run` event with `source:"cron"`, and the admin dashboard's own aggregation query, replicated verbatim, now correctly counts it (found via a real, if brief, race — `track()` is deliberately fire-and-forget everywhere in this codebase, including the pre-existing manual-trigger call site, so the verification script polls rather than reading immediately, matching the established design rather than changing it).
  - **Explicitly untouched, per instruction**: risk-level guardrails (`GUARDRAILS` constants), trailing-stop ratchet logic (`stopPct`/watchdog), and all AI reasoning/scoring (quant weights, Claude prompts) — this pass is about correctness and honesty in the execution layer, not strategy.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-16 (AI Portfolio Agent — first production audit since the epic went fully autonomous, `AGENT-AUDIT.md` PUSHED to origin/main (`4e7b6cc`) — read-only, reported first, pushed on explicit follow-up instruction. Full report at the repo root; summary here.) **Method**: read-only real-data queries (Postgres + `gh` GitHub Actions run history) against the 3 real users who currently run an enabled agent — no trades, resets, or agent modifications made to any real account. **Headline findings**: (1) both cron loops ARE running correctly and on schedule for every real agent — proven via real `agent_decisions` rows, not the heartbeat table, because (2) **a real bug was found and fixed**: `cron_heartbeats` for `agent-thinker` sat stale for 5 days while the job ran correctly every single day underneath it — root-caused to `void recordHeartbeat(...)` racing serverless teardown right before `return` (classic fire-and-forget bug), fixed by awaiting it in both `lib/agent/cron.server.ts` and the identically-patterned `lib/snapshots/endpoint.server.ts` — the only code changes made during this audit, both trivially safe, pure-observability, zero trading impact. (3) **Decision quality is genuinely good** — rationales cite real signals/news, risk level is honestly reflected in what got bought — but (4) **confirmed a known, previously-deferred gap is now a real, active problem**: severe membership churn (AMZN alone: 12 buys/8 sells for one real agent, 7/7 for another, often re-entering within 1 day of exiting) that the existing `COOLDOWN_DAYS` protection structurally cannot cover, since it only guards watchdog stop-sells, never thinker exits. (5) **Found a real, unnoticed bug**: a real $1,000 balanced agent has run its daily cycle 26 times and bought literally nothing, ever — every AGENT_UNIVERSE symbol costs more than its per-position dollar budget under whole-shares-only buying (`Math.floor(buyDollars/price) < 1 → silently continue`, `rebalance.ts:134`), and the decision log shows the identical "no trades needed" message a genuinely healthy at-target agent would show. (6) Trailing-stop ratchets are real and firing (185 ratchets, 282 real watchdog runs) but **zero real protective stop-sells have ever fired** for any real user in ~8 weeks — the single most safety-critical code path is unproven in production. (7) Cost is cheap and correctly-paced (133 real Claude calls, 100% success, ~$6.65 estimated total spend for 3 users over 8 weeks) but **the admin cost dashboard structurally undercounts it** — its `agent_run` tracking event fires only from the manual "Run agent now" button, never from the cron path that produced all 133 real calls. (8) **Performance stated honestly, no spin**: all 3 real agents underperformed SPY over their own active windows (−0.02%/+3.85%/0.00% vs. SPY's +4.1–4.8% over the same windows) — sample size (3 agents, ~8 weeks) is too small to mean anything about skill, but the number is what it is. Full prioritized recommendations (broken/high-value/later/not-worth-it) in `AGENT-AUDIT.md` Part 7. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, full verify-*.ts suite run.)

Live-tested against a real 1-symbol account: Claude did NOT include the market-wide item even though the stated threshold ("fewer than 2") should have triggered it — a soft LLM instruction-following gap, not a hallucination, but still wrong. **Fixed by moving the decision out of the model into deterministic code**: Claude now just treats market-wide symbols exactly like any other (write an item if real news exists, skip if not); a NEW code-level filter (`MIN_OWN_ITEMS_BEFORE_MARKET_WIDE = 2`) decides AFTER the fact, from the real count of the user's own items Claude actually wrote, whether to keep the market-wide items in the final payload. Hover-pause and resume-on-leave were instead verified by dispatching real `MouseEvent('mouseover'/'mouseout', {bubbles:true})` — the same event React's synthetic system actually listens for — which correctly held the position static for 8+ seconds (past the 6.5s interval) and correctly resumed after a dispatched leave. This is a tooling characteristic of this session, not a claim about real users' browsers, where genuine mouse events always fire normally.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-15 (Sector allocation bare-dash bug — BUILT, VERIFIED live, PUSHED to origin/main (`dd335d8`). Issue #34 filed, verified, and closed. Symptom: Portfolio's "Allocation by sector" chart showed a bare `"—"` legend entry for a VOO holding. **Diagnosed against live Finnhub first, not assumed**: ran a spread check via `providerQuotes()` across 5 ETFs (VOO/QQQ/SPY/ARKK/IWM) and 10 ordinary stocks spanning 8 distinct real sectors (Energy, Pharmaceuticals, Retail, Consumer products, Beverages, Machinery, Aerospace & Defense, Media — all deliberately OUTSIDE the app's curated mock table, to hit real Finnhub data unmasked by any client-side fallback). Result: **5/5 ETFs** had blank sector AND `undefined` marketCap simultaneously; **0/10 ordinary stocks** had a blank sector. Conclusion: this is exclusively an ETF/fund data gap in Finnhub's `/stock/profile2`, not a general data-quality issue — confirming the Stock Detail "About" tab's existing `isLikelyFund` heuristic (`!marketCap && (!sector || sector === "—")`) is well-founded, since every ETF checked hit both conditions together. **Root cause**: `app.portfolio.tsx`'s `bySector` memo grouped holdings directly by the raw `Quote.sector` field with zero fallback, so an ETF's `"—"` sentinel became a chart bucket name verbatim — and the About tab had its OWN separate inline copy of the fund-detection heuristic, a second mechanism the task explicitly said not to invent. **Fix**: new shared `lib/marketData/sector.ts` — `isLikelyFund(q)` (the exact About-tab heuristic, now the ONE copy) and `displaySector(q)` (`"ETFs & funds"` for a detected fund, the real sector string when one exists, `"Unclassified"` for any other genuinely-unknown case — never the raw `"—"` sentinel). Portfolio's `bySector` chart grouping and the Holdings-detail table's Sector column both now route through `displaySector`; the About tab was migrated to import the shared `isLikelyFund` instead of its own inline copy (kept its own more-specific "Exchange-traded fund" wording for that single-stat context, appropriate since "ETFs & funds" is a plural chart-bucket label — only the DETECTION boolean is shared, not forced-identical display text everywhere) and its own dead `"—"` fallback for the true-unknown case was fixed to `"Unclassified"` too. **Audited every other place sector is displayed, reported not just fixed**: Stock Detail header badge (already safe — conditionally hides itself when sector is missing, left unchanged); Markets page filter pills/rows (sourced from the curated `getStock()` mock table, which currently has zero ETFs in `MARKET_UNIVERSE` — confirmed inert today, not touched, since fixing a code path that can't currently render a dash would be speculative rather than a real fix); Dashboard and Watchlist (grepped, zero sector references, not applicable). **Verified live**: a mixed real portfolio (VOO + AAPL + JPM, bought via real `execute_trade` RPC calls on a throwaway account) showed "Technology" / "Banking" / "ETFs & funds" correctly labeled in both the chart legend and the holdings table, at both 1366×768 desktop and 375px mobile — no overlap, no truncation. A second, ETF-only throwaway account (VOO + QQQ) confirmed the two ETF holdings correctly MERGE into one single "ETFs & funds" segment (not two separate dash-like buckets) — proving the `Map`-keyed grouping logic works correctly with the new label. The donut tooltip's percentage math was confirmed unchanged by inspection (the fix only changes what populates the `name` field passed into the existing, untouched `{name, value}` aggregation — the percent-of-total formula never moved). VOO's About card re-confirmed showing "Exchange-traded fund" post-migration to the shared heuristic. Both throwaway accounts and all one-off diagnostic/seed/cleanup scripts deleted after — no test artifacts left behind. **No new dependencies.** Full 31-script `verify-*.ts` suite: 30/31 clean on the full run, the one non-pass (`verify-hardening-pass.ts`, a `STEP TIMEOUT` on `runMarginMonitor` — unrelated, this diff touches zero server/margin/options code) re-ran clean in isolation immediately after. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean. Committed as one chunk (`sector.ts` + `app.portfolio.tsx` + `app.stock.$symbol.tsx`, `dd335d8`). **PUSHED to origin/main** — reported first, then pushed on explicit follow-up instruction. Issue #34 filed, verified, and closed; moved to Done on the project board.)

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-15 (Password visibility toggles — BUILT, VERIFIED live, PUSHED to origin/main (`2ef902c`). Issue #33 filed, verified, and closed. Every `type="password"` field in the app audited and covered, not just sign-in — 7 fields across 3 files: `auth.tsx` (Sign in, Create account), `reset-password.tsx` (New password, Confirm new password), `app.settings.tsx` (Current password, New password, Confirm new password). New shared `PasswordInput` (`components/ui/password-input.tsx`): wraps the existing shadcn `Input` with a trailing Eye/EyeOff toggle (lucide-react — already the app's icon set, no new dependency), positioned via a `relative` wrapper + `absolute inset-y-0 right-0` button rather than the codebase's existing leading-icon wrapper-div convention (audited first — that pattern is for a LEADING icon; a trailing toggle button is the standard, correct shape for this specific job, and no existing precedent needed inventing around). Toggles the real `type` attribute (`password`↔`text`) rather than overlaying fake dots — the standard technique, since the underlying value never changes, only how it renders, so password managers/autofill are unaffected. **Always defaults to hidden**: `visible` is local `useState` inside each `PasswordInput` instance, never persisted, so it resets on every mount (page load, route change, form resubmit) and each field's toggle is fully independent of every other field on the page — proven live in Settings (revealing "New password" left "Current password" and "Confirm new password" both still masked) and in Reset Password (revealing "New password" left "Confirm new password" masked). `auth.tsx`'s existing `Field` helper was extended (destructure `type`, branch to `PasswordInput` when `type === "password"`, `Input` otherwise) rather than duplicated, so both its password fields inherited the toggle with a 3-line change; the other two files had no shared helper, so each `<Input type="password">` was swapped 1:1 for `<PasswordInput>` directly.
  - **Accessibility, built in from the start, not retrofitted**: a real `<button type="button">` (can never submit the form — confirmed live: clicking the toggle changed nothing else on the page, no navigation, no toast), `aria-label` that flips with state ("Show password"/"Hide password"), `aria-pressed` matching, keyboard-focusable (confirmed via `.focus()`+`document.activeElement`), and a `focus-visible:ring-ring` matching the rest of the app's inputs. **One honest caveat, verified not assumed**: this session's Browser-pane tool could not mechanically exercise Enter/Space-triggered activation — its synthetic key dispatch produces a real `keydown` reaching the focused button (confirmed via an event-listener trap) but the browser's native "Enter on a focused `<button>` synthesizes a click" behavior never fires, reproduced identically on a pre-existing, completely unrelated button (the top-bar theme toggle) with zero code from this diff — ruling out a component bug and pointing at the same category of tool-level limitation this session had already hit once before (CSS animations not starting while the tab's `document.hidden` stays `true`). The toggle is a genuine native `<button>` with no custom key handling that could suppress the browser's default behavior, so Enter/Space activation is guaranteed by the HTML spec independent of this tool's ability to prove it.
  - **Tap target**: button is `w-10` (40px) × the input's own `h-9` (36px) — clears the ~40px+ ask on width, the dimension that matters most for a right-edge icon; height is capped by the input's own fixed height, which nothing in this change alters. Confirmed via live measurement at 375px (`getBoundingClientRect()`: 40×36) and via a real successful tap-driven reveal on that exact viewport.
  - **Verification, real evidence across all 7 fields**: toggle confirmed working in Settings (all 3 fields, independent state) and Reset Password (both fields, independent state) via direct typed values becoming legible/masked on toggle; Sign In and Create Account confirmed via visual screenshots showing the revealed plaintext with zero icon/text overlap. Defaults-hidden re-confirmed on fresh page loads and after client-side route changes. **Two full real sign-ins** with a throwaway harness account — once with the password field left hidden through submit, once with it revealed through submit — both landed correctly on the Dashboard. **A full real password-reset round trip**, not simulated: a genuine Supabase recovery link generated via the admin API for a second throwaway account, the resulting session tokens carried over to the local dev server, the New Password field revealed and Confirm field left masked, and submission correctly showed "Password updated." 375px screenshots confirm no overlap in Settings (both masked and revealed) and Sign In; desktop screenshots confirm Create Account's password field and toggle. All throwaway accounts and one-off seed/cleanup scripts deleted after — no test artifacts left behind.

**Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-15 (Auth page showcase — graphic pass: ambient backdrop, per-slide diagrams, dynamic transitions — BUILT, VERIFIED live, PUSHED to origin/main (`4a5850e`). Issue #32 filed, verified, and closed. Follow-up to #31. Design brief was explicit: "futuristic" means precise/technical like a trading terminal, NOT sci-fi — no particle fields, no neon, restraint over spectacle. **Ambient backdrop** (`ShowcaseGraphics.tsx`, new file): a literal-pixel 44px grid (no `viewBox`, so tiles stay crisp squares at any panel aspect ratio) at 5% opacity + one large soft chart-arc path at 8% opacity, both `absolute inset-0` behind the slide content, layered with the pre-existing green ambient glow. **A real abstract diagram per slide, not a lucide icon**: connected-nodes network (AI agent), a timeline with a shock spike (scenario challenges), a diverging branch (what-if simulator), candlesticks + a smooth trend overlay (AI insights), a strike-ladder grid with one emphasized rung (options), an annotated checklist with one completed/checked row (journal & coaching) — all inline SVG, geometric, `currentColor`/primary-green strokes, zero image assets. **Dynamic transitions**: replaced the pure crossfade with a rise-and-fade on the whole slide block (`animate-in fade-in slide-in-from-bottom-3`, via `tw-animate-css` — already a dependency, no new package) plus a brief "draws itself in" stroke animation on each graphic's one or two emphasis strokes only (`pathLength={1}` SVG-length-normalization trick + a new `.svg-draw-in`/`@keyframes showcase-draw-in` pair in `styles.css`) — deliberately not every stroke, so six slides' worth of motion stays quick and restrained rather than a multi-part show. **Composition**: content width `480px`→`520px`, heading `text-3xl`→`text-4xl`, and a deliberate `-translate-y-[6%]` bias off dead-centre (judgment call, justified in-code: with a graphic now sitting above the heading, true dead-centre crowded the dots against the panel's vertical middle in a way that read as merely centred rather than composed; a slight upward anchor with room left underneath is the more usual "designed hero" convention). Rotation unchanged at 4000ms.
  - **A real, production-affecting bug was found and fixed during verification, not just a cosmetic pass.** Browser testing surfaced the auth page's logo intermittently rendering off-screen at the top-left after interacting with a dot indicator. Root-caused, not papered over: the panel's decorative gradient glow (pre-existing, unchanged — `absolute -bottom-32 -right-32`) spills 128px past the panel's own box on purpose; with `overflow-hidden`, that still makes the panel a genuine scroll container (`scrollHeight` 1328 vs `clientHeight` 1200 measured live), and focusing a dot button — a real mouse click OR keyboard Tab, not a testing artifact — triggers the browser's default scroll-into-view on that container, silently setting `scrollTop` and clipping the logo out of the visible (clipped) region. Confirmed by direct measurement (`panel.scrollTop` went 0→109 after a click; resetting it to 0 immediately restored the correct render) and confirmed this is a real user-facing risk, not tooling noise, since a keyboard user tabbing through the dots would hit the identical browser behavior. **Fixed at the root in `auth.tsx`**: `overflow-hidden` → `overflow-clip` on the panel — clips identically for the decorative glow but never creates a scroll container in the first place, so no future content change here can trigger this class of bug again. Re-verified with real clicks at both 1366×768 and 1920×1200 after the fix: `scrollTop` stays 0, logo stays at the correct `(40, 40)`, in every case.
  - **Verification, real evidence, not assumed:** desktop screenshots at 1366×768 and 1920×1200 across 3+ distinct slides each (network/candlestick/checklist/strike-ladder graphics all visually confirmed crisp and legible), backdrop confirmed to read as texture rather than competing with the foreground text at the tuned 5%/8% opacities. `prefers-reduced-motion` re-confirmed via a `window.matchMedia` patch + forced SPA-internal remount: 0 dot indicators, no `animate-in`/`svg-draw-in` classes present, held static across a full 5-second window (>1 rotation interval), and the patch confirmed not to leak into a genuine subsequent reload. Hover-pause re-confirmed held for 5+ real seconds; resume-on-mouse-leave re-confirmed via an advance within 5s of the mouse leaving. Mobile (375px) re-confirmed unaffected (`offsetParent: null`, screenshot matches the standard mobile auth layout). Sign in / Create account (Radix Tabs — again needs a real pointer-event click, not a synthetic `.click()`) / Forgot password → Back to sign in all re-confirmed via real interaction, plus one full real sign-in with a throwaway harness account (created and deleted via `verify-harness.ts`, along with the one-off seed/cleanup scripts used — no test artifacts left behind), landing correctly on the Dashboard with the real $25,000 starting balance.

Confirmed live, not hypothetically — an unscoped call during the fix touched 6 distinct real user IDs with open positions. **Fixed at the right layer per the instructions' own ranking (lighter/faster beats a bigger timeout)**: added `onlyUserId?: string` scoping to `getOptionsValueByUser`, threaded through from `runSnapshots`, unscoped (real cron) path unchanged. Verified properly: **full 31-script suite run THREE consecutive times — 31/31 green on all three**, zero footnotes, with the previously-failing step's elapsed time reported each run (1938ms/1628ms/2121ms against its 25000ms budget — 91–93.5% headroom, consistent margin, not a fluke). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean. **Last updated (previous):** 2026-08-14 (`verify-hardening-pass.ts` flake FIXED at the root, not tolerated blind — it had failed twice on a ~$2 live-price mismatch and passed clean on isolated re-run both times, exactly the "intermittently-red teaches you to ignore failures" pattern that lets a real regression slip through later. Root cause: the test's own "Dashboard total" reconstruction fetched each holding's price through `getServerQuote`'s 30-second-TTL cache, then compared it against `getPositionsValue()`'s (and `runSnapshots()`'s) internal fetch, which is DELIBERATELY always-live/never-cached for a money-critical number (`valuation.server.ts`'s own doc comment: "Never a cached/stale number for a money decision"). Comparing a value up to 30s stale against an always-fresh one is real, unavoidable drift on an active trading day, not a bug in either code path. **Fixed by eliminating the avoidable half of the gap** (candidate (a), preferred per instruction): switched the test's own holdings-value calc from a per-symbol loop through the cache to ONE batched, uncached `providerQuotes()` call — the exact same mechanism `getPositionsValue()` uses internally — shrinking the residual race window from "up to 30 real seconds" to "the network gap between two back-to-back live fetches." Added a small, explicitly-justified tolerance (`closeToLive`, 5bps of the reference value or 2¢, whichever is larger) for the 3 assertions that legitimately still span two independent live fetches to two separate real production functions (not mockable without defeating the point of a zero-mocks integration suite) — calibrated to be ~1000× tighter than the smallest real bug this test exists to catch (the original missing-loan-subtraction bug it's named for). Verified properly, not with one lucky run: ran the full 30-script suite **three consecutive times — 30/30 green on all three**, zero `❌` markers anywhere, and the specific reconciliation numbers matched EXACTLY to the cent in all three runs (the tolerance never had to do any work — it's a documented safety margin, not a crutch papering over a real mismatch). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean. **PUSHED to origin/main (`dbca9aa`).** Full detail in the changelog entry below.)

**🤖 Phase 10: AI Portfolio Agent — FULLY SHIPPED & LIVE ON AUTOPILOT (10.1–10.5).** Autopilot wired & confirmed by Venky: GitHub Actions `agent-watchdog` workflow + repo secrets set + tested; Vercel `agent-thinker` daily cron confirmed. The agent funds, thinks (~daily), protects via trailing stops (intraday), shows its work, and runs itself. Token-protected batch endpoints `/api/cron/agent-thinker` (Vercel cron `30 21 * * *`) + `/api/cron/agent-watchdog` (GitHub Actions every 30m market hours, DST-aware gate). Vercel Hobby = exactly 2 daily crons used (thinker + snapshot). **HARDENING PASS (CTO order): 1 gentler rebalance ✅ → 2 scale/rate-limit smoothing ✅ → 3 SPY benchmark line ✅ → 4 approve-mode + cosmetics ✅.**
- ✅ H1 gentler rebalance DONE + verified, committed `3d5a8cd`, pending push. New `lib/agent/rebalance.ts` `planRebalance()` (no migration): drift band ±5pp hysteresis (within band → no trade), prefer deploying agent_cash over selling winners, only trim if >5pp over target or dropped from target set, re-entry COOLDOWN 4 days (don't rebuy a symbol the watchdog stop-sold; inferred from agent_decisions action='sell'; thinker trims/exits now log action='trim' to disambiguate). Guardrails intact. Dashboard got trim/hold badges. Verified 20 checks: run1=4 trades→run2=0 trades (hysteresis), cooldown skip works, new cash deployed w/o churn, caps/buffer hold. Open follow-up (logged, not built): drift band handles weight noise but not MEMBERSHIP noise (name oscillating in/out of target set) — add membership-stickiness if it shows in practice.
- ✅ H2 scale/rate-limit smoothing DONE + verified, committed `7868576`, pending push. New `lib/marketData/ratelimit.server.ts` (module-level RateLimiter 50/min sliding + 6 concurrent, 8s per-call timeout, withRetry 3× exp backoff on 429/5xx/network only) wired into `fhGet`. Per-run DEDUP: thinker batch prefetches universe once (`prefetchUniverse()` → `runThinker(uid,{prefetch})` → `scoreCandidates(risk, prefetched)`); watchdog batch fetches union of held symbols once + shares price/beta. Added `onlyUserIds[]` scoping seam to cron batches (verification-only). Verified 17 checks: 25 agents×11 holdings → 11 fetches (not 275); 5 reuse runs add 0 fetches; bad symbol → placeholder, no abort/hang; throttle/backoff/timeout all proven via mock-clock. Behavior identical, just fewer calls. Next lever if needed (not built): durable Postgres price_cache across serverless invocations.
- ✅ H3 SPY benchmark line DONE + verified, committed `90188c7`, pending push. Agent perf chart overlays dashed S&P 500 line + beat/lag readout ("Agent +X% · S&P +Y% · Beating/Lagging by Z%"). Reuses `getCandles("SPY","1Y")` (Twelve Data, 1 fetch/load cached, sliced locally per range). **📐 CTO LESSON (2026-08-14) — DON'T DELEGATE RULES TO THE MODEL; DELEGATE JUDGMENT.** Two instances from the market-brief work, both worth generalising. (1) **Structural prevention beats instruction:** rather than telling Claude "never invent article URLs," the brief's JSON schema simply has NO url field — so fabrication is impossible, not merely discouraged. The real link is matched in code afterward from the Finnhub payload we already fetched. (2) **Deterministic rules belong in code:** Claude did NOT reliably honour "only include market-wide items if the brief is thin," even given an explicit numeric threshold — so that decision was moved out of the prompt into code gated on the real item count. **Rule of thumb going forward: if a constraint can be enforced deterministically, enforce it in code or in the schema. Reserve the model for judgment (what matters, how to phrase it), never for arithmetic, thresholds, or factual references it could invent.**

**📐 CTO LESSON (2026-08-09) — THE GRANT/SILENT-WRITE PATTERN HAS NOW BITTEN US 3×. Make it structural.** Instances: `transactions` missing service_role SELECT (found in M1) · `insights` missing DELETE (found repairing verify-insights) · `analytics_events` missing DELETE (found because the CTO asked whether the privacy policy's retention promise was actually honoured — the prune had been silently failing since it shipped). Same shape every time: a new table is created, the code performs an operation nobody granted, and it fails SILENTLY because the return value isn't checked. **STANDING RULE for every new table from now on: (1) enumerate EVERY operation the code will perform on it (select/insert/update/delete, by which role) and grant exactly those in the same migration; (2) route the write through `mustSucceed()`/`logIfFailed()` so a permission failure can never be silent; (3) if the table has a retention/prune path, the DELETE grant is part of the feature, not an afterthought.** Corollary: any user-facing PROMISE (privacy retention, "we delete your data") must have a test that proves it, or it's aspirational.

**📐 CTO LESSON (2026-08-04, from the epic hardening pass): specify DERIVED numbers, not just the obvious ones.** The equity bug (dashboard/portfolio-%/snapshots showed gross assets, not equity, for margin users — overstating net worth by the exact loan amount) existed because the M1/M2 specs said "make BUYING POWER margin-aware" and never said "net the loan out of TOTAL PORTFOLIO VALUE." CC built exactly what was asked. **Rule going forward: when a new liability/asset class is introduced, enumerate EVERY derived figure it touches (totals, %s, charts, snapshots, exports) in the spec — don't assume the obvious surface is the only one.** The hardening phase caught it, which is why cross-feature seam audits are non-negotiable at epic close.

**🚨 VERIFICATION-HARNESS RULE (learned the hard way 5×: 10.4 snapshots, Insights brief, M1 margin, 2026-08-11's scenario-challenges debug script (~2h silent hang), and 2026-08-12's admin-console browser-verification seed script (~4h24m silent hang, killed by CTO order — see the dedicated bullet below).** ROOT CAUSE finally identified during M1: an uncaught top-level `throw` in a `vite-node` script does NOT exit the process (unlike plain Node) — the SSR module runtime stays resident forever, printing nothing. Every "hang" was likely this, not slow APIs and not stuck DB locks. **MANDATORY for every verification script from now on:** (1) wrap the whole body in try/catch with an explicit `process.exit(1)`; (2) wrap EVERY await in a hard per-step timeout that throws with the step name; (3) print a timestamped line BEFORE and AFTER every step; (4) run in the foreground under a pty. Also: if a verification run goes quiet for >10 min, KILL IT — do not wait on a notification. **This is now STRUCTURAL, not just a convention to remember:** `app/verify-harness.ts` exports `step`/`assert`/`approx`/`deepEqual`/`sleep`/`createTestUser`/`runVerification` — every verify-*.ts script (including ad hoc throwaway debug scripts written under time pressure) MUST import from it rather than hand-rolling these primitives. `runVerification(main, opts)` wraps the ENTIRE script body in one hard global-ceiling timeout (default 8 min) as a backstop independent of any individual step's own timeout, so even a script that forgets to wrap one particular await still can't hang past the ceiling.
- **A second, compounding incident on the SAME 2026-08-11 debug script, root-caused separately:** the 2-hour hang's real trigger was `admin.auth.admin.createUser(...)` called WITHOUT `user_metadata: { terms_accepted_version: ... }` — `handle_new_user()` (migration `0022_terms_acceptance.sql`) REQUIRES that field and `RAISE EXCEPTION`s mid-transaction if it's absent (by design — a real signup-form bug should fail loudly, not silently create an unconsented account). For an `admin.auth.admin.createUser` CALLER, that trigger exception surfaces as an OPAQUE Postgres/GoTrue `500` (`AuthRetryableFetchError`, empty message) — nothing about it says "you forgot the metadata," and it was first misdiagnosed as a Supabase platform outage before the Auth error logs (`Failed to close prepared statement: ERROR: current transaction is aborted...`) made the real cause legible. Audited EVERY `createUser` call site in the repo (not just the ones from memory): all ~20 pre-existing verify-*.ts call sites already had the field correctly — the gap was entirely in throwaway scratch scripts that didn't copy that pattern. **Fixed structurally, not just for this one incident:** `createTestUser(admin, email, password)` in `verify-harness.ts` is now the ONLY way any script creates a test user — it always attaches the required metadata, so forgetting it is no longer possible. Every existing verify-*.ts call site was retrofitted to use it (mechanical swap, no behavior change, confirmed via typecheck).
- **Production implication worth knowing (not a bug to fix, just a sharp edge):** because `handle_new_user()` raises on missing metadata, a REAL caller who somehow hit this path (impossible via our own signup form, which always sends `terms_accepted_version` — see A4/`0022`) would see an opaque `500`/`AuthRetryableFetchError`, not our friendly "terms not accepted" message. The clear error only exists at the Postgres/trigger level, invisible to the HTTP caller. Low risk (our own client can't trigger it), but worth remembering if a future integration ever calls `auth.admin.createUser`/`auth.signUp` without going through the app's own signup form.
- **Supabase project limits worth remembering for future test-user seeding:** this project's Supabase instance caps outbound auth emails at **2/hour** (the cause of earlier email-send failures during testing — mitigated by always passing `email_confirm: true` in test scripts, which skips the confirmation email entirely) and caps signups at **30 per 5 minutes**. Pace any script that creates more than a handful of throwaway users in a tight loop.
- **🚨 2026-08-12 — a THIRD hang, ~4h24m silent, killed by explicit CTO order.** A one-off seed script for admin-console browser verification (creating a throwaway user + a journal entry, run via `runVerification` with a 30s global ceiling) never printed even its first `console.log` and never exited despite that ceiling — ps showed 4h24m wall time against only ~21min CPU time (blocked on I/O, not spinning). **ROOT CAUSE, confirmed by direct inspection: `lib/supabase/admin.server.ts`'s `getServiceClient()` had ZERO timeout on ANY call** — auth admin API, postgrest, rpc, all of it, via the plain `createClient(...)` with no `global.fetch` override. This is the EXACT gap the 2026-08-11 incident already identified and explicitly deferred ("a residual risk, deliberately not touched … to avoid regressions across every feature using `getServiceClient()`") — that deferral is what let this recur. **Fixed at the source, not just for this one script:** `getServiceClient()` now passes a custom `global.fetch` (20s `AbortController` timeout) into `createClient()` — since supabase-js routes `auth.*`/`.from()`/`.rpc()` ALL through this one function (confirmed by reading `SupabaseClient.ts`'s `_initSupabaseAuthClient` call), this is a single unavoidable choke point that protects every current and future caller, not a per-call-site fix. `createTestUser()` in `verify-harness.ts` also got its OWN redundant 25s `withTimeout` wrap (belt-and-suspenders — it can't hang past 25s even if some future `admin` client it's handed lacks a timeout of its own). **The browser-side client (`lib/supabase/client.ts`) had the identical gap** (zero fetch timeout, meaning a stalled connection could spin a real signed-in user's tab forever) — fixed the same way, 30s (vs. 20s server-side, since real user network conditions vary more).
  - **NEW STANDING RULE, no exceptions: there are no "quick throwaway scripts."** Both multi-hour losses (2026-08-11 and this one) came from exactly that exception — a script written "just this once" to skip the harness because it seemed simple. ANY script that touches the network or the database — seeds, debug scripts, one-offs, ad hoc checks, everything — goes through `verify-harness.ts` (`step`/`withTimeout`/`runVerification`) from the first line, full stop. If a throwaway script feels too trivial to justify the harness's ceremony, that feeling is the failure mode, not a reason to skip it.
  - **Audit performed 2026-08-12 for any other unbounded network/DB call, production paths included:** every `createClient()` call site in the repo (`admin.server.ts`, `client.ts` — confirmed via grep, nothing else calls it per the CLAUDE.md rule) now has a fetch timeout. `provider.server.ts` (Twelve Data) already fixed 2026-08-11. `finnhub.server.ts`'s fetch is already routed through `ratelimit.server.ts`'s `withHardTimeout` (8s, from the H2 hardening pass) — already bounded. The two `new Anthropic(...)` client sites (`insights.server.ts`, `agent/anthropic.server.ts`) rely on the SDK's own default 10-minute timeout — bounded, not the same unbounded-hang class, left as-is. No `axios`/`XMLHttpRequest`/other HTTP client usage found anywhere in the app. **Conclusion: every network call in this codebase now has SOME finite ceiling** — the last remaining gap (this incident's actual trigger) is closed.
**🔐 Related discovery (M1):** `service_role` has NEVER had table-level UPDATE on `profiles` (0001 only granted it to `authenticated`) — all legitimate writes go through SECURITY DEFINER functions which run as the function owner. This is GOOD defense-in-depth (even the service key can't clobber money columns outside a vetted, event-logged function) and is deliberately preserved. Test-only seam added instead: `0013_margin_test_seed.sql` → `admin_seed_margin_state(user_id, margin_loan?, last_interest_accrued_at?)`, service_role-only, scoped to ONLY those 2 columns (never cash_balance, never margin_enabled/status). CTO-approved as not widening the attack surface (service_role can already move money via execute_trade); noted as a test seam living in prod. **Same root cause bit again inside product code, not just the verify script:** `lib/margin/monitor.server.ts` wrote `margin_status` via the same kind of raw, unchecked `profiles` write and had been silently failing since M1 shipped — fixed via a dedicated `set_margin_status` RPC (`0014_margin_set_status.sql`). That, plus the CTO-ordered codebase-wide audit for the whole unchecked-Supabase-write bug class, is written up in full in the Changelog below.

**⚙️ PROCESS: Venky added a `cto-rigor` skill (2026-06-26) — adopted as the operating standard.** Key upgrades to enforce: preflight env checks before builds; definition-of-done with proof on diverse/adversarial inputs (never single happy-path); no patch jobs (general fixes only, no hardcoded defaults); serverless-awareness (in-memory state doesn't survive Vercel invocations); verify deployed, not just local; never rubber-stamp CC's "done" — check every requirement individually; keep this HANDOFF living. First catch under the new standard: V1 Insights' per-stock day-cache was spec'd in-memory → would leak paid Claude calls on serverless; amended to persist in the 0009 `insights` table (kind='stock', symbol, unique per symbol+day, authenticated-readable since no user data).

**📉 EPIC FULLY COMPLETE + HARDENED — Options & Margin paper trading (kicked off 2026-08-01, closed 2026-08-08, hardening pass done 2026-08-08). Status: O1–O4 ✅ · M1 ✅ · M2 ✅ · hardening pass ✅ — all committed locally, NOT yet pushed (see CURRENT STATE at top).** Phase plan (CTO): **O1** pricing engine — pure Black-Scholes module + generated option chains (deterministic, unit-tested math; no DB/UI) → **O2** DB model + server-side buy/sell engine (long calls/puts, sell-to-close, premium×100 contract mechanics) → **O3** options UI (chain browser on Stock Detail, order flow, positions w/ P&L) → **O4** expiration/exercise processing (fold into daily cron) → **M1** margin engine (opt-in, 2× buying power, daily interest, maintenance + simulated margin calls) → **M2** margin UI + education → hardening/QA. Design locks from roadmap: model-derived premiums (disclosed), v1 = long calls/puts + sell-to-close only, educational explainers everywhere. Venky's ask 2026-07-23.
- ✅ **O1 (pricing engine) DONE + verified, committed locally — NOT pushed. No DB, no UI, no trading — pure foundations, per plan.** `lib/options/blackscholes.ts` (pure European call/put pricing + delta/gamma/theta/vega), `lib/options/volatility.server.ts` (realized-vol estimator, clamped 10%-150%), `lib/options/chain.server.ts` (generated expiries + strike ladder + priced contracts), `lib/options/functions.ts` (`getOptionChainFn`, JWT-verified, 1hr day-cached). Full detail + real numbers in the changelog entry below.
- ✅ **O2 (trade engine) DONE + verified against the REAL applied migration, committed locally — NOT pushed.** Migration `0010_options.sql` applied by Venky: `option_positions` (owner-only RLS select; writes exclusively via the SECURITY DEFINER function) + `option_transactions` (append-only ledger, same pattern as equities' `transactions`) + `execute_option_trade` (atomic, row-locked, `service_role`-only EXECUTE — the exact Phase 6 `execute_trade` pattern). `executeOptionTradeFn` (JWT-verified): the client sends ONLY `{contractId, side, contracts}` — the server re-derives symbol/type/strike/expiry from the contract id itself via a new `parseContractId`, rejects expired contracts (checked BOTH server-side and again in the SQL function, a deliberate double backstop), and computes the premium itself (live spot + realized vol → Black-Scholes via a new `priceParsedContract`) — there's no `premium` field anywhere in the input schema, so a client-doctored one gets silently stripped before the handler runs.
- ✅ **O3 (options UI) DONE + verified live end-to-end in the real running app, committed locally — NOT pushed.** New Options tab on Stock Detail (chain browser: expiry pills, strike-centered calls/puts table, ATM auto-highlighted, ITM shading, disclaimer + collapsible "What are options?" explainer), a responsive buy/sell order dialog (bottom-sheet on phone / centered dialog on desktop, one component via Tailwind breakpoints), positions shown identically on both the stock page and Portfolio (one shared `["optionPositions"]` React Query, server-computed via new `lib/options/valuation.server.ts` — true single source of truth), and option positions now fold into Dashboard's total portfolio value + today's-change AND the daily snapshot writer (closing both `TODO(O3)` markers from O2). Full real numbers + methodology notes in the changelog entry below.
- ✅ **O4 (expiration processing) DONE + verified against the REAL applied migration, committed locally — NOT pushed. The Robinhood-parity-style "options lifecycle" is now fully closed** — positions can no longer be left dangling past expiry with nothing happening. v1 = CASH SETTLEMENT only (no share delivery/exercise), disclosed in the explainer. Migration `0011_option_expiry.sql`: widened `option_transactions.side` to add `'expired'`/`'settled'` (two distinct values, not one side with total=0-vs->0, so the ledger self-documents which happened) + `settle_expired_option(p_user_id, p_contract_id, p_settle_per_share)` — same SECURITY DEFINER/service_role-only lockdown as every other money-moving function in this schema, reads `contracts` from the LOCKED position row itself (never a caller-supplied count), and rejects any position whose `expiry >= current_date` — structurally unable to touch a live position no matter what the caller's own query filtered for. New `lib/options/expiry.server.ts` `runExpiryProcessing()`: finds every position with `expiry < today` (the exact same boundary `execute_option_trade` already uses for "expired" — the two functions can never disagree about a contract's status; a contract expiring TODAY stays tradeable all day and settles on TOMORROW's run), batches one historical-candle fetch per distinct underlying (shared `getDailyHistory`), settles each via the underlying's close ON the expiry date (falls back to the latest available close if that exact date's candle is missing — documented, not silent), per-position error isolation, `onlyUserId` verification seam. Folded into the EXISTING `/api/cron/snapshot` endpoint (Vercel Hobby's 2-cron cap stays at 2 — same "fold in" pattern already used for the AI daily brief), running BEFORE the snapshot write so settlement cash lands in the SAME day's snapshot. UI: `OptionsExplainer` gained a 4th paragraph disclosing cash settlement; Portfolio's Options card gained a small "Recent option activity" list (buy/sell/settled/expired, clearly labeled — "Settled $478.00" vs "Expired worthless"); positions list needed no change (a settled position just disappears, same as a sell). Full real numbers in the changelog entry below.

  - **Order panel (`app.stock.$symbol.tsx`):** new Shares/Dollars segmented toggle (Robinhood-style, same visual pattern as the buy/sell toggle). Shares mode now allows fractional input (`step="any"`, no more `Math.floor`). Dollars mode shows a live "≈ N.NNNN shares @ $price" estimate as the user types, computed from the CLIENT's displayed price for UX only — the actual quantity executed is always resolved server-side from a fresh price, per rule #2. Client-side validation (`validateSharesQty`/`validateDollarAmount`) rejects zero/negative/absurd precision (>6dp for shares, >2dp for dollars) and enforces sensible minimums (≥0.0001 shares, ≥$1) with friendly toasts, mirroring the existing `insufficient_funds`/`insufficient_shares` error style.
  - **`executeTradeFn` (`lib/trading/functions.ts`):** input is now `quantity` XOR `amount` XOR `sellAll` (zod `.refine` enforces exactly one). For `amount` orders the SERVER computes `qty = amount / serverPrice`, rounded to 6dp — the client's estimate is never trusted for execution, only display. For a `sellAll` order the server reads the EXACT stored `holdings.quantity` (no rounding at all) and sells precisely that — zero dust, because it's literally the same DB value, not a recomputation. For a dollar-based SELL, the computed qty is capped at the held quantity (`Math.min(rawQty, held)`) so "sell $X worth" of a smaller position just closes it instead of erroring.
  - **"Sell all" affordance:** a toggleable button (shown only when `side==="sell"` and a position exists) reading "Sell all — closes your entire position (N shares ≈ $X)"; when active it hides the Shares/Dollars section entirely and the confirm button reads "Confirm sell · all SYMBOL (N.NNNNNN)".
  - **Display formatting:** new `fmtQty(n, maxDp=6)` in `lib/mockData.ts` (locale-formatted, trims trailing zeros — "1" not "1.000000", "0.241512" not truncated). Audited and fixed every raw `{h.quantity}`/`{t.quantity}` render app-wide: Stock Detail (position stat + recent trades), Dashboard holdings table, Portfolio holdings + transactions tables, Agent holdings table + decision-log trade lines. Confirmation toast now reads "Bought ≈0.2555 shares of NVDA for $50.00" in Dollars/sell-all mode, or "Bought 0.5 NVDA @ $195.47" in Shares mode.
  - **Rounding policy (stated, not hidden):** dollar-order quantities are rounded to 6 decimal places before execution, so the realized cost can differ from the requested dollar amount by a sub-cent fraction (observed: $50 requested → cost off by ~$0.0000093, invisible at the 2dp currency display everyone sees). Sell-all has NO such rounding — it uses the exact stored quantity, so a full-position close is always exactly zero dust.
  - **VERIFIED end-to-end on the real running app** (throwaway test user `pt-r2-verify-…@example.org`, real Finnhub price + real Supabase execute_trade, cleaned up after): Buy $50 of NVDA (Dollars mode) → "≈0.2555 shares @ $195.66" preview, executed 0.255545 shares, buying power $100,000.00 → $99,950.00 EXACTLY. Buy 0.5 shares (Shares mode, server re-fetched a fresh price of $195.83988802 at execution, different from the momentary display price — expected and correct) → position 0.755545 shares, weighted avg cost $195.77911931122568 — independently recomputed by hand from the two real fills and matched to 11 significant figures. Sell $25 worth → shares reduced to 0.628228, avg cost unchanged (correct — sells don't alter cost basis), buying power +$25.00 exactly. **Sell all** → position fully closed, "You don't own any NVDA yet" shown, **`holdings` table confirmed to have ZERO rows for that user+symbol** (not a zeroed row — actually deleted, true zero dust), cash reconciled to the cent ($100,000.4388815, matching a $0.44 net gain from real intra-test price movement, nothing unaccounted for). Rejections, all with NO balance change (confirmed via direct DB read after): overspend $500,000 → "Not enough buying power for this order."; $0.50 → "Minimum order is $1.00."; 0 shares → "Enter a quantity greater than zero."; −5 shares → same friendly message (not a crash); selling with a zero position → "You don't have enough shares to sell." `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, client-bundle grep → 0 matches for secrets + `getServiceClient`/`execute_trade`/`verifyUser`.

Noted alongside the existing `insights`-DELETE and `holdings`-INSERT gaps for a future cleanup migration.
- ✅ **R3 (stock-page story) DONE + verified, SHIPPED to `origin/main` (part of the R4 push, `a3b4b93`). No migration/new provider** — everything reuses data already being fetched (Finnhub profile2, `fhCompanyNews`, existing holdings/quotes).

Also added `url` to `NewsItem` (Finnhub's article link was fetched but previously discarded) and moved `NewsItem` into the shared `types.ts` (matching how `Quote`/`SymbolMatch` are already organized there, rather than living only in `finnhub.server.ts`). **Behavior change, deliberate:** `fhCompanyNews` used to swallow ALL errors internally and always return `[]` — changed so errors now PROPAGATE, so the News tab (and only the News tab, via its own try/catch in the new server fn) can tell "genuinely no news" apart from "provider error" and show a different message for each. The two EXISTING callers (agent thinker, AI Insights) already wrapped every call in `.catch(() => [])` defensively, so this is backward-compatible — verified by reading both call sites before changing the shared function. Cache key now includes the requested `limit` (`fh:news:{sym}:{limit}`) since different callers request different limits (5/8/10) and were previously able to silently share/clobber each other's cached list.

Extended Finnhub's `/stock/profile2` parsing (`fhProfile`) to also capture `weburl`/`country`/`ipo` — same API call already being made for name/sector/logo/market cap, zero extra cost. ETF degrade: when the profile is empty (no sector, no market cap — Finnhub's ETF behavior), shows "Exchange-traded fund" instead of a blank/NaN sector, and the IPO-date/website rows are simply omitted (not shown as dangling "—") when the data doesn't exist for a fund.

The portfolio-total denominator reuses the Dashboard's EXACT formula (`cash + Σ qty×price` across all holdings) — Stock Detail already had the current symbol's quote loaded, so it only fetches quotes for the user's OTHER held symbols (via the existing `useQuotes` hook) rather than refetching everything. Non-holders see a plain nudge card ("You don't own SYMBOL yet... place a paper order from the panel on the right").
  - **Tabs, not new page sections:** kept the existing tab-based design language (Your position / News / About / Recent trades — News and About inserted, trades moved last) rather than stacking new full-width cards, per the prompt's "your call, keep consistent" — chart and order panel untouched.
  - 🐛 **Real bug found + fixed during verification (my own new code, not a testing artifact):** `NewsTab` originally checked `newsQ.isLoading` to decide when to show the spinner. In React Query v5, `isLoading` = `isPending && isFetching`, which goes FALSE during the gap between a failed attempt and its retry (still pending, but not actively fetching) — during that gap, with `retry:1` on a forced-error test, the code fell through past both the loading and error checks straight to `items ?? []` and rendered "No recent news" for a symbol that was actually mid-retry, not actually empty. Fixed by checking `isPending` instead (stays true across retries, only clears once truly settled). Root-caused via a temporary forced-error edit to `getCompanyNewsFn` (reverted immediately after) rather than guessing — confirmed the fix by observing "Loading news…" persist correctly through a live retry cycle before reverting.
  - **VERIFIED end-to-end on the real running app** (throwaway test user `pt-r3-verify-…@example.org`, cleaned up after): AAPL — 10 real headlines with real sources (ChartMill, Yahoo) and relative times ("35m ago" → "2h ago"), links confirmed via DOM inspection to be real `finnhub.io/api/news?id=...` URLs with `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`; About card showed "Apple Inc · AAPL · NASDAQ NMS - GLOBAL MARKET", sector "Technology", market cap "$4.9T", country "US", IPO "1980-12-12", "Visit website" → confirmed `href="https://www.apple.com/"` with correct target/rel. Bought $50 of AAPL (0.165651 fractional shares) — position card math verified by hand from a single snapshot (price $301.31: market value $49.91, total return −$0.09/-0.18%, today's change −$5.32, all recomputed independently and matched exactly) — then cross-checked against the Dashboard loaded a few seconds later at the SAME live price: holdings-table market value $49.91, P&L −$0.09 (-0.18%) — **identical to the cent** on both pages. VOO — ETF degrade confirmed clean: "VANGUARD S&P 500 ETF", "Exchange-traded fund", market cap/country both "—" with no NaN or blank gaps, no dangling IPO/website rows; real news also present (SeekingAlpha, Yahoo sources). No-position nudge state confirmed on AAPL/VOO/TSLA/AMD before any purchase. Rate-limit/error path confirmed by a temporary forced-throw test (reverted): renders "Couldn't load this" + the exact friendly rate-limit message + a working "Try again" button. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, client-bundle grep → 0 matches for all 5 secrets + `fhCompanyNews`/`fhGet`/`getServiceClient`.
