**📰 NEW EPIC — AI Insights (in progress).** News-driven, history-aware buy/sell ANALYSIS (educational, not advice; prominent disclaimer; no fake "millions of traders" claims — Venky's original ask included crowd data we don't have; CTO pushed back, honest sourcing = live Finnhub news + market history + our own computed stats). Two stages, Venky confirmed order:
- **V1 (doing now):** per-stock "AI Insight" on Stock Detail (on-demand button, day-cached, one structured Claude call: lean/confidence/summary/drivers grounded in real news/historical_parallel/risks/watch_for) + daily "Market brief" dashboard card for holdings+watchlist (one Claude call/user/day, stored in new `insights` table, migration 0009; fold into EXISTING cron — Vercel Hobby 2-cron cap, both slots used). Placement/trigger/tone decided: stock page + daily brief · on-demand+cached · clear lean + disclaimer.
- **V1 status:** built; CTO review caught that CC missed the serverless-cache amendment (used in-memory cached() only) → CC owned it and reworked: corrected `0009_insights.sql` (user_id nullable, symbol column, kind check, PARTIAL unique indexes — one stock insight per symbol/day GLOBALLY shared, one brief per user/day; RLS: any authenticated user reads kind='stock', own-only for kind='brief'; includes repair path for earlier table shape; note: PostgREST upsert can't infer partial indexes → explicit insert-then-update helper `writeInsightRow`). `getStockInsight` is DB-FIRST (table check → Claude on miss → persist), cached() only a hot layer. ⚠️ Accepted residual (CTO call): same-second first-request race on one symbol can cost one duplicate Claude call — rare/bounded, advisory-lock fix deliberately not built. Migration `0009` applied by Venky 2026-07-31.
- **V1 VERIFICATION RESULTS (2026-07-31, real Claude + Finnhub + Supabase calls, no mocks):**
  - ✅ **Per-stock insight**, both symbols well-formed + news-grounded + zero directive-advice language: NVDA → `lean: bullish, confidence: moderate`, 5 drivers citing real headlines (Jensen Huang memory-bottleneck comments, Cathie Wood's $14.3M buy, FCC restrictions on Chinese AI hardware), historical_parallel framed as a general pattern not a prediction, `usedNews: 8`. VOO → `lean: neutral, confidence: moderate`, drivers grounded in real macro headlines (GDP, Core PCE, Fed posture), `usedNews: 8`.
  - ✅ **Three Claude-call counters, all as specified:** first NVDA call → **1**. Same-process repeat of `getStockInsight("NVDA")` → **0 extra** (total stayed 1), `generatedAt` identical, confirming the in-process `cached()` fast path. **Fresh, separate node process** (genuinely empty module memory — real cold-start simulation) reading NVDA back → **0 Claude calls**, `generatedAt: 2026-07-31T06:40:39.791Z` — bit-for-bit identical to the original generation, proving it was served durably from the `insights` table, not regenerated.
  - ✅ **Daily brief: VERIFIED end-to-end, including rendered in the browser.** Real root cause found (not the Postgres/PostgREST issue first suspected): the brief job's watchlist-seeding step in the VERIFICATION HARNESS used `.upsert(rows, { onConflict: "user_id,symbol" })` — a plain upsert defaults to `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, which Postgres requires `UPDATE` privilege for (checked at parse time, regardless of whether a row actually conflicts) — and `authenticated` has never had `UPDATE` on `watchlist` (confirmed via `information_schema.role_table_grants`: has SELECT/INSERT/DELETE, not UPDATE). The REAL app's `addToWatchlist()` (`lib/portfolio/queries.ts`) was never affected — it passes `ignoreDuplicates: true`, which compiles to `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and only needs INSERT. **`NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` was a red herring** (harmless, but not the fix) — this was never a stale cache. Fixed `verify-insights.ts`'s seeding line to match the real app's `ignoreDuplicates: true`; brief generation then passed immediately: 1 Claude call, `briefsWritten: 1`, stored row (`kind='brief'`, `symbol: null`, `user_id` set) with a real headline + 3 grounded items (NVDA/AAPL/VOO) + disclaimer, and **confirmed rendering live in the browser** (Dashboard → "Today's market brief" card, screenshot-verified, content matching the DB row exactly).
  - 🐛 **Bug found + fixed during this render check:** `MarketBriefBody`'s footer showed "For Jul 30" for a brief stored as `created_at: "2026-07-31"` — the same date-only/UTC-midnight-vs-local-render bug already fixed for the simulator in H4b, just not applied here. Fixed in `components/InsightUI.tsx` (added `timeZone: "UTC"` to the `toLocaleDateString` call); re-verified live, now correctly shows "For Jul 31". `generatedAt` (used on the per-stock insight card) is a full timestamp, not date-only, so it wasn't affected.
  - 🔧 **Also found, separate, minor, not yet fixed:** `service_role` has no `DELETE` grant on `insights` (0009 granted only select/insert/update) — only matters to test-harness cleanup today (silently caught there), but is a real gap worth a follow-up one-line grant whenever 0009 (or a 0010) is next touched. Also: `service_role` has never had `INSERT` on `holdings` either (only SELECT, since Phase 6/8) — not a bug, just noted since it surfaced during this investigation.
  - **V1 is now FULLY VERIFIED** (per-stock insight + daily brief, both with real Claude output, correct storage, and confirmed rendering). Committed locally across two commits (`AI Insights V1` feature + a follow-up fix for the harness/date-bug — see changelog), **NOT pushed** per standing default. Migration `0009` applied.
- ✅ **V2 (event study) DONE + verified, committed locally — NOT pushed. No new provider/migration.** Solved the "hard part" (defining a comparable event) by keying off the stock's OWN price history rather than news-event matching: a "shock" is a single-day return whose magnitude exceeds 2× the standard deviation of its own trailing 60-day returns (computed strictly before that day — no look-ahead), split into up-shocks/down-shocks. For each qualifying shock (only counted once its +1-month forward window is fully realized in the data — no partial/future events), measures the +1-week and +1-month forward returns, then aggregates avg/median/worst/best/%-positive. Full detail + real verification numbers in the changelog entry below.

**(prior epic note) AI Portfolio Agent —** An autonomous AI "personal investor" managing a SEPARATE virtual sub-portfolio (user funds it with a chosen amount from their virtual cash). Decisions locked: fully autonomous + guardrails · hybrid brain = quant rules + LLM news reading (needs **ANTHROPIC_API_KEY**, server-only) · two-loop cadence (cheap intraday "watchdog" for trailing stops/guardrails + ~daily LLM "thinker") · smart volatility-sized trailing stops + rebalance (explicitly NOT naive 1% stops — whipsaw risk explained to Venky) · educational SIMULATION, paper money only, NOT financial advice (UI disclaimer required) · no guarantee of gains (set honestly with Venky). Sub-phases 10.1–10.5 in ROADMAP.md. Separate agent sub-portfolio chosen so the agent never disturbs the user's manual holdings and its performance is cleanly trackable.

**10.1 (foundations) SHIPPED to `origin/main` (`5d2388d`), auto-redeployed live.** Migration `0005_agent.sql` applied (agent_config/agent_holdings/agent_transactions/agent_decisions, owner-only RLS; `fund_agent(user_id, amount)` atomic main↔agent cash move, service_role-only). `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` stored server-only (unused until 10.2). New `/app/agent` page (Bot nav icon): disclaimer banner, Activate toggle, risk selector (conservative/balanced/aggressive), mode (autonomous/approve), Fund/Withdraw with live balances, empty states. JWT-verified server fns getAgentConfig/updateAgentConfig/fundAgent. Verified: FUND +5k → main 95k/agent 5k; WITHDRAW −2k → main 97k/agent 3k; over/zero rejected; risk persists across reload; keys absent from client bundle.

**10.2 (decision engine — the "thinker") DONE + end-to-end verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews executed portfolios first).** Migration `0006` applied. Hybrid brain, all server-only:
- QUANT (`lib/agent/quant.server.ts`): scores universe (8 majors + SPY/QQQ/VOO) from Finnhub quote + `/stock/metric` (momentum=blended 13w/26w return, beta, 52-wk position) → z-scored, risk-weighted formula. Aggressive favors momentum+beta; conservative punishes beta + rewards ETFs. Added `fhMetrics` + `fhCompanyNews` to `finnhub.server.ts` (cached). No Twelve Data — avoids its 8 req/min limit.
- AI (`lib/agent/anthropic.server.ts`): ONE structured-output call to Claude (`@anthropic-ai/sdk`), JSON-schema constrained (`output_config.format`), prompt-cached system. Reads shortlist signals + recent news → per-symbol {include, weight_hint, reason} + commentary. Chooses ONLY from shortlist; graceful fallback to quant-only on any AI error. **Model configurable via server-only `AGENT_MODEL` env (no VITE_); defaults to `claude-sonnet-4-6`** (cost — one call/run; set to claude-opus-4-8 for max reasoning). `agentModel()` helper.
- Guardrails+execution (`lib/agent/thinker.server.ts`): cash buffer + max single position + min/max holdings per risk (cons .25/.25/5–7 · bal .15/.30/4–6 · agg .08/.35/3–5); normalize→cap per-position→renormalize; buys into agent via new SECURITY DEFINER `agent_execute_trade` (migration `0006`, mirrors execute_trade on agent_cash/agent_holdings/agent_transactions); logs per-buy + overall rebalance `agent_decisions`; never overspends agent_cash. `runAgentThinkerFn` (JWT) + "Run agent now" button on /app/agent (enabled when activated+funded).
- VERIFIED END-TO-END on `claude-sonnet-4-6` (real `runThinker` via vite-node, $50k funded test agent per risk level, then DB read-back): ALL guardrails held at every level — max single position (cons 17.8%≤25 · bal 19.2%≤30 · agg 27.6%≤35), cash buffer (27.3% · 16.9% · 8.8%), holdings count (5∈[5,7] · 6∈[4,6] · 5∈[3,5]), agent_cash never negative, spent==invested exactly, every holding has a buy txn. Decision quality strong + risk-differentiated on Sonnet (matches Opus preview): conservative ETF-anchored (VOO core + cash buffer, excludes AMD/GOOGL/META), aggressive tilts to high-beta semis (AMD 27.6%+NVDA 21.7% ≈ half the book). typecheck+build clean; AGENT_MODEL + ANTHROPIC key/SDK + `agent_execute_trade` absent from client bundle (0 matches).
- 🐛 Fixed during verification: min-holdings top-up gave topped-up names Claude's weight_hint of 0 (excluded names) → floored to 0 shares → conservative landed 4 holdings (<5). Now topped-up names get the average included weight + a clear "added for diversification/min-holdings" rationale (not Claude's exclude reason).

Engine works end-to-end on the real site.
- 🧭 SEQUENCING (CTO call, Venky deferred to me; priority = quality not speed): **10.3 watchdog → 10.4 dashboard → 10.5 scheduling → final hardening/QA.** Rationale: (1) the agent can buy but has no risk management yet — completing protective trailing stops makes it functionally whole (and delivers the "know when to pull out" goal Venky originally wanted); (2) building the watchdog first lets us build the dashboard ONCE showing the complete picture (holdings w/ stop levels + buys AND protective sells in the decision log), avoiding reworking 10.4 later. Currently /app/agent shows only counts ("5 positions / 6 decisions"); the breakdown UI is 10.4.

**10.3 (risk "watchdog" — protective trailing stops) DONE + rigorously verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews stop logic + anti-whipsaw first).** No new migration (reuses existing `agent_holdings.trailing_stop_price` + `agent_execute_trade` sell side). The CHEAP/FREQUENT loop, all server-only, NO LLM calls:
- `lib/agent/watchdog.server.ts` — `runWatchdog(userId, sources?)`. Per holding: fetch live price (held symbols only) + beta, RATCHET the trailing stop UP only (`stop = max(existing, price×(1−stopPct))` → implicitly tracks the high-water mark), and on breach (price ≤ stop) execute ONE protective SELL of the whole position via `agent_execute_trade` (proceeds → agent_cash, stay in agent; NO auto-rebuy — re-entry is the thinker's deliberate call). Logs each sell + a run-summary to `agent_decisions`; quiet ratchets (counted, not per-row logged).
- SMART anti-whipsaw: a dip that doesn't breach the (only-ever-rising) stop does nothing. `stopPct` is VOLATILITY-SIZED + risk-varied: `clamp(base + betaSlope·(β−1), min, max)` with cons {.08,.04,[.06,.18]} · bal {.12,.05,[.09,.25]} · agg {.18,.06,[.12,.35]}. Widths e.g. β1→cons8%/bal12%/agg18%; β2.5→cons14%/bal19.5%/agg27%. Higher beta = wider stop; aggressive widest (let winners run); conservative tightest. `stopPct()` exported + reused by the thinker to SEED the stop at buy time (`price×(1−stopPct)`).
- `runAgentWatchdogFn` (JWT) + "Run watchdog" button on /app/agent (enabled when activated + holds positions). Dependency-injected price/beta sources so it's deterministically testable.
- VERIFIED (deterministic stubbed price paths via vite-node — ALL PASSED): stop ratchets UP as price rises and NEVER moves down; a non-breaching dip → NO sell (anti-whipsaw); a real drawdown breach → exactly ONE sell, full proceeds to agent_cash, position closed, decision logged with correct peak ($120) + drawdown (−13.3%); high-β name gets a measurably WIDER stop than a calm one (β2.5 stop $80.5 < β0.8 $89) + aggressive wider than conservative; missing live quote → skipped + logged, never a phantom sell, no crash; agent_cash/shares stay consistent and non-negative. (Live-data smoke can't run under vite-node — `getQuotes` needs the TanStack Start server runtime; that path is the same one prod already uses + runs inside the real server fn.) typecheck + build clean; no secrets/server symbols in client bundle (0 matches).
- ⏳ NEXT: Venky reviews stop logic + anti-whipsaw, then push. Then 10.4 dashboard (surface holdings w/ stop levels + buys/sells log) → 10.5 scheduling.

Trailing stop = max(existing, price×(1−stopPct)) — ratchets UP only, sells one whole position on breach, proceeds → agent_cash (no auto-rebuy; re-entry = thinker). `stopPct = clamp(base + betaSlope·(β−1), min, max)`, varies by risk (cons tightest/agg widest); β2.5→cons14%/bal19.5%/agg27%, β1.0→8/12/18. Same `stopPct()` seeds the stop at BUY time in the thinker. Keys/server symbols absent from client bundle. Caveat: live `getQuotes` path can't run under vite-node harness (needs TanStack server runtime) — same path used in prod elsewhere; worth ONE live "Run watchdog" sanity check after deploy.
- 10.3 design locked: volatility-sized TRAILING stops that only ratchet UP (sell only on real drawdown from peak — NOT naive 1% dips), wider stops for high-beta names, vary by risk level; protective sells via agent_execute_trade → proceeds to agent_cash; NO auto-rebuy (re-entry is the thinker's deliberate call) — all to avoid whipsaw. Cheap loop, no LLM, just quotes for held symbols. Reminder: any NEW env var added locally must also be set in Vercel + redeploy (learned w/ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY).

**10.4 (Agent dashboard) DONE + end-to-end verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews, then push).** Migration `0007_agent_snapshots.sql` applied. Pure RENDER phase — no new trading logic; surfaces 10.2/10.3 data richly on `/app/agent`:
- Summary header (agent value = agent_cash + Σ qty×live price · total return $/% vs allocated · invested/cash split) · Holdings table (symbol+name, qty, avg cost, live price via `useQuotes`, market value, weight %, unrealized P&L, **trailing-stop level + cushion %** so the watchdog's protection is visible) · Decision log cards (buy=green / protective sell=red / rebalance=blue / watchdog=muted, left-border accent, timestamp + plain-English rationale) · Performance chart (reused `PortfolioValueChart` + new `baseline` prop = allocated amount; SPY overlay deferred — noted as TODO in UI).
- NEW `agent_snapshots` table (id, user_id, total_value, agent_cash, holdings_value, captured_at date, unique(user_id,captured_at), owner-RLS select, service_role write) — migration `0007`. Snapshot writer (`writer.server.ts`) EXTENDED to also record the agent sub-portfolio daily (same cron); added optional `runSnapshots({ onlyUserId })` to scope to one user (on-demand/test, avoids whole-DB price fan-out). `getAgentSnapshots()` maps agent_cash→cash to reuse the shared Snapshot type. Seeds origin = allocated amount on the agent's setup day.
- VERIFIED END-TO-END (real `runThinker` + real `runSnapshots({onlyUserId})` via vite-node, $50k balanced test agent — ALL 11 checks ✅): holdings table reconciles ($8651.41 cash + $41348.59 invested = $50000 total; weights 100.00% of invested; 6/6 holdings have a seeded stop e.g. AAPL stop $259.88 / 12.5% cushion, AMD $444.06 / 19.5%); decision log shows 6 buys + 1 rebalance with rationale; **agent_snapshots wrote a reconciling today row** ($50000 snap == $50000 UI, total == agent_cash+holdings_value). typecheck + build clean; secrets/server symbols absent from client bundle (0 matches).
- ⚙️ Verification gotcha (for next time): vite-node BLOCK-buffers stdout when piped to a file → backgrounded runs look "hung" (0 output) even while alive; run under a pty (`script -q log npx vite-node …`) to get live output. Also: never call the full `runSnapshots()` in a test (it writes EVERY user's portfolio_snapshots) — use `{ onlyUserId }`.
- ⏳ NEXT: Venky reviews + pushes (Vercel auto-redeploys; the daily cron now also writes agent_snapshots — chart fills in day by day). Then **10.5 scheduling** (wire watchdog frequent + thinker ~daily to cron via CRON_SECRET) → final hardening/QA.

**10.5 (autopilot scheduling — LAST BUILD PHASE of the agent epic) DONE + verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews schedule + auth, then push + sets GitHub secrets).** The agent runs itself; no new env/migration. Two token-protected batch cron endpoints (auth mirrors /api/cron/snapshot — `Authorization: Bearer CRON_SECRET`, also x-cron-secret/?secret):
- `/api/cron/agent-thinker` (DAILY, Vercel Cron `30 21 * * *` — after US close, staggered 30min BEFORE the snapshot `0 22` so the day's chart reflects the rebalance): runs the real thinker for every agent that is `enabled AND mode='autonomous' AND agent_cash>0`. mode='approve' agents SKIPPED (TODO: approve mode should generate *proposals*, not auto-trade — not built).
- `/api/cron/agent-watchdog` (INTRADAY, **GitHub Actions** `.github/workflows/agent-watchdog.yml`, `*/30 13-21 * * 1-5` UTC — Vercel Hobby cron is once/day so it can't do intraday): runs the real watchdog for every enabled agent that holds positions. No-ops cheaply when US market closed (`isUsMarketOpen()` — Mon–Fri 9:30–16:00 ET, DST-aware via Intl America/New_York; holidays not excluded).
- `lib/agent/cron.server.ts`: `runThinkerForAllAgents` / `runWatchdogForAllAgents` (+ handlers), per-agent try/catch (one failure can't abort the batch), agents run SEQUENTIALLY (natural Finnhub pacing), JSON summary {eligible, processed, trades/sells, errors}. Optional `{onlyUserId, sources, skipMarketCheck}` seams for safe scoped verification (prod calls with none). Wired in `app/src/server.ts` (dynamic import, like snapshot).
- VERIFIED (vite-node, scoped to a throwaway agent so NO prod agent touched — 18 checks ✅): market gate (open weekday-midday / closed weekend+before-open+after-close); auth 401 without/with-wrong secret; thinker batch — approve-mode → eligible 0 (skipped), autonomous funded → eligible 1/processed 1/6 trades + seeded stops; watchdog batch — prices above stops → 0 sells (no whipsaw), prices below stops → 6 protective sells, all positions closed, proceeds to agent_cash, sells in agent_transactions. Workflow YAML valid (js-yaml). typecheck + build clean; no secrets committed (workflow reads GitHub secrets; 0 literal-secret matches); server-only symbols absent from client bundle.
- 🔧 VENKY SETUP before/after push: (1) Vercel — nothing new; existing `CRON_SECRET` env already covers the new `/api/cron/agent-thinker` (Vercel auto-sends the Bearer). Confirm the new daily cron appears in the Vercel dashboard after deploy. NOTE Hobby = max 2 cron jobs → we now use exactly 2 (agent-thinker + snapshot); watchdog is GitHub Actions, not a Vercel cron. (2) GitHub repo → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions: add `CRON_SECRET` (same value as Vercel) and `AGENT_BASE_URL` (= https://venk-cc-first-project.vercel.app, no trailing slash). Then test via the workflow's "Run workflow" button. Scheduled GH workflows run only on the default branch + auto-disable after ~60 days inactivity.
- ⚠️ Carries the same Finnhub-scale hardening caveat as 10.4 (sequential pricing across all agents pressures 60/min as users grow) — see hardening TODO above. After this: **agent epic build COMPLETE → final hardening/QA pass** (Finnhub throttling, SPY overlay, approve-mode proposals, prune test users [done this session], simulator tz fix, README live URL).

**HARDENING #1 (gentler rebalancing — reduce churn) DONE + verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews, then push). No migration.** The thinker used to re-buy toward exact target weights every run (high turnover, and it ignored existing holdings entirely). Now it computes the MINIMAL trade set:
- NEW pure module `lib/agent/rebalance.ts` → `planRebalance(input)`. Constants: `DRIFT_BAND = 0.05` (±5pp of total capital, absolute & explainable), `COOLDOWN_DAYS = 4` (covers ~3 trading days incl. a weekend).
  - (1) DRIFT BAND / hysteresis: a TARGET position is only traded when its weight deviates from target by >5pp; within band → left alone (kills daily micro-churn).
  - (2) PREFER CASH over selling: underweight targets are filled with available agent_cash first (best quant score first); a held name is only TRIMMED if it's >5pp OVER target, and a name no longer in the target set is fully EXITED.
  - (3) COOLDOWN: symbols the watchdog protective-sold within COOLDOWN_DAYS are not rebought. Inferred from `agent_decisions` where `action='sell'` (watchdog logs 'sell'; thinker trims/exits now log `action='trim'`, so 'sell' = watchdog only — NO new table/column needed). Skipped picks are LOGGED (`action='hold'`, "re-entry cooldown").
  - Guardrails enforced inside the planner: never spend below the cash buffer, never exceed max-position cap, never overspend agent_cash.
- `thinker.server.ts` rewired: reads current holdings + live prices, totalCapital = agent_cash + Σ(qty×price), filters cooldown out of targets (capturing dropped AI picks), calls `planRebalance`, executes (sells first then buys), seeds the trailing stop ONLY for brand-new positions (never lowers a ratcheted stop on an add). Rebalance decision rationale: "Portfolio within drift bands — no trades needed." when nothing material, else Claude commentary + "Adjusted N; held M within ±5pp band". New `runThinker(userId, { disableAi })` test seam (deterministic quant-only). Dashboard: new `trim`/`hold` decision-badge styling; run toast reflects trades vs no-op.
- VERIFIED (vite-node — 20 checks ✅): PURE planRebalance — at-target → 0 trades, small drift (<band) → 0 trades (hysteresis), large drift → trims ONLY the drifted name (others held), new cash → buys only (no winners sold) w/ buffer respected, cooldown symbol skipped+reported while others proceed, non-target fully exited, max-position cap held (27.8%≤30%), no overspend, cash accounting balances. INTEGRATION on the REAL thinker (quant-only) — run twice: run1 4 trades → **run2 0 trades** (low turnover proven; old behavior would re-trade all); a pre-seeded watchdog stop-sell of AMD → AMD on cooldown, NOT rebought (agent built [QQQ,VOO,SPY,AAPL] instead); agent_cash never negative. typecheck + build clean; no secrets/server symbols in client bundle (0).
- 🔭 Possible follow-up (not done): membership churn — a name oscillating in/out of the target set across runs still causes exit/re-buy (the band addresses WEIGHT noise, not membership noise). Consider a stickiness/hysteresis on set membership if it shows up in practice.

**HARDENING #2 (batch jobs reliable under the Finnhub rate limit) DONE + verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews, then push). No migration/env.** Unattended batches (snapshot writer + agent thinker/watchdog crons) used to price held symbols PER USER → as users grow they'd blow Finnhub's 60/min (the silent slow/hung run we saw in 10.4 testing). Fixed two ways, behavior identical:
- GLOBAL limiter + retry + timeout (`lib/marketData/ratelimit.server.ts`, wired into `finnhub.server.ts` `fhGet`): one module-level `RateLimiter(50/min, 6 concurrent)` governs EVERY Finnhub call (sliding window: bursts to 50 then spaces). Per-call 8s `withTimeout` (AbortController) so a stuck request can't hang. `withRetry` = 3 retries, exp backoff 500→1000→2000 + jitter, retries only 429/5xx/network/timeout — NEVER a 4xx (bad symbol fails fast). All clock-injectable → deterministically tested. Added `fetchStats()`/`resetFetchStats()` test counters.
- Per-run DEDUP: thinker batch fetches ONE universe snapshot up front (`prefetchUniverse()` in quant.server.ts) and threads it through `runThinker(uid, { prefetch })` → `scoreCandidates(risk, prefetched)` for every agent (data is identical across agents; only scoring differs). Watchdog batch fetches the UNION of held symbols once and passes a shared `{prices,betas}` source to each `runWatchdog`. Snapshot writer already deduped. Added `onlyUserIds[]` scoping seam to both cron batches (verification only — never touches prod agents).
- VERIFIED (vite-node — 17 checks ✅): PURE (mock clock) — window throttle (first 50 instant, 51st waits a full minute), concurrency capped at 4, retry-429-then-succeed, give-up-after-4-attempts (no hang), no-retry on 4xx, backoff 500→1000→2000. INTEGRATION (real Finnhub fetch counters) — thinker prefetch: 1 snapshot = 11 quote fetches, 5 reuse runs add **0** extra (vs old 33 for 3 runs); scoring output identical with/without prefetch (AMD top both). Watchdog batch: **25 agents × 11 holdings → 11 quote fetches (NOT 275)**, all 25 processed, 0 errors. Resilience: one invalid symbol → placeholder (price 0), the whole `providerQuotes` call still resolves (Promise.allSettled — no abort/hang). typecheck + build clean; no secrets/server symbols in client bundle (0).
- 🔭 Next lever if still needed at scale (NOT built): durable Postgres `price_cache` (deferred since Phase 5) so the snapshot+cron quotes survive across serverless invocations.

**HARDENING #3 (S&P 500 benchmark on the agent performance chart) DONE + verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews, then push). No migration/env.** Closes the 10.4 TODO. The agent perf chart now overlays "same money in the S&P 500" so the user can see beat/lag vs the market.
- Data: reuses the existing `getCandles("SPY", "1Y")` (Twelve Data, same path as the What-If Simulator — NO new provider). Fetched once per chart load on /app/agent (react-query, 1h staleTime, retry 1, enabled only when ≥1 snapshot); the chart slices it locally per range toggle, so range changes don't refetch.
- Normalization (documented): BOTH lines indexed to the same start at the window's LEFT edge — `spyLine(d) = agentValue(firstVisibleDate) × spyClose(d)/spyClose(d0)`. So both start equal and the right-edge gap IS the out/under-performance. Limitation noted in code: mid-window deposits/withdrawals make the absolute-$ compare approximate — the return-% readout is the honest headline. SPY closes forward-filled across weekends/holidays.
- Pure math in `components/portfolioBenchmark.ts` → `indexBenchmark(points, spy)` (unit-tested, no React). Chart (`PortfolioValueChart`) gained an OPTIONAL `benchmark` prop (dashed muted 2nd Area + legend + beat/lag readout: "Agent +X% · S&P 500 +Y% · Beating/Lagging the market by Z%"). Opt-in, so the MAIN dashboard chart is unchanged (passes no benchmark). Recomputes per range toggle.
- Graceful: short history (<2 pts) → agent placeholder + "benchmark builds as history grows"; SPY fetch error/empty → agent line alone + quiet "benchmark unavailable"; never crashes the chart.
- VERIFIED: pure `indexBenchmark` (vite-node, 15 checks ✅) — SPY line starts exactly at agent's first value (same-start indexing); +20% agent / +21% S&P → "lagging by 1%"; +30% agent → "beating by 9%"; per-window re-index (2-day window re-bases to that window's start, +9.1% vs +10%); weekend forward-fill (missing SPY day stays flat); fallbacks (1 point / empty SPY / window-before-SPY-history) all return cleanly. SPY data source confirmed live: `providerCandles("SPY","1Y")` → 252 daily closes ($606.78 → $733.58). typecheck + build clean; no secrets in client bundle (0). NOTE: visual render is build-verified, not browser-rendered (the page is auth-gated + needs snapshot history; same precedent as 10.4's data-layer verification) — the math + data feed + fallbacks are proven by the pure suite.

**HARDENING #4a (approve-first mode actually works) DONE + verified, committed (NOT pushed — Venky reviews, then push). Migration `0008_agent_proposals.sql` applied.** Previously approve-mode agents were skipped and never proposed anything. Now the thinker PROPOSES for approve-mode agents (manual button + daily cron); the user approves/rejects; autonomous mode is unchanged.
- NEW `agent_proposals` table (id, user_id, created_at, status[pending|approved|rejected|superseded], target jsonb [{symbol,weight,score,beta,reason}], trades jsonb [human-readable buy/sell/trim], rationale, commentary; owner-only RLS select, service_role write; index (user_id,status,created_at desc)). Migration `0008`.
- Refactor: extracted `lib/agent/execute.server.ts` (GUARDRAILS, round2, `executePlan`) — shared by the autonomous thinker AND the approve executor so BOTH enforce identical guardrails (also breaks a thinker↔proposals import cycle). `lib/agent/proposals.server.ts`: `writeProposal` (supersedes prior pending), `executeProposal` (approve), `rejectProposal`, `supersedePending`.
- Thinker now reads `cfg.mode`: approve → compute the SAME target + planRebalance, write a pending proposal (storing target + indicative trades + Claude commentary), return `{proposed:true, executed:[]}` — NO trades. Autonomous → `executePlan` as before. `runThinker(uid,{disableAi})` test seam unchanged.
- APPROVE = JWT server fn `approveAgentProposalFn` → `executeProposal`: re-fetches CURRENT live prices (never stale proposal-time prices), re-applies the watchdog cooldown, renormalizes weights over allowed targets, runs planRebalance (all guardrails re-validated), executes (sells→buys, seeds stops for new positions via stored beta), marks 'approved', logs buy/trim decisions + an "approved proposal" rebalance entry. REJECT = `rejectAgentProposalFn` → marks 'rejected', executes nothing. Switching mode→autonomous supersedes any pending proposal (no stranded state).
- Cron `runThinkerForAllAgents` now selects BOTH modes (dropped the `mode='autonomous'` filter) + reports `proposalsTotal`. UI /app/agent: "Proposed changes" card (trade list w/ symbol+qty+price, rationale, commentary, Approve & Reject) shown when mode='approve' + a pending proposal; run button relabels to "Propose changes"; toasts cover propose/approve/reject. Approve-mode helper text added.
- VERIFIED END-TO-END ($50k balanced test agents, real thinker + executor, quant-only — 22 checks ✅): approve run → proposed=true, 0 trades, 0 holdings, exactly 1 pending (4 targets/4 trades); 2nd run → 1 superseded + 1 new pending; APPROVE → 4 trades at current prices, agent_cash $50,000→$9,177.41, spent==invested ($50k), max position 20.7%≤30% cap, stops seeded, buys + approval-rebalance decisions logged; REJECT → 'rejected', 0 holdings/trades; AUTONOMOUS → 4 trades immediately, holdings created, NO proposal. typecheck + build clean; client bundle has no server symbols/secrets (0; the lone case-insensitive hit was the UI word "guardrails").
- 🔭 Follow-up (4b, not built): a proposal expiry/"propose again" affordance + showing approved/rejected history; approve currently uses an empty-jitter equal default if Claude weights are absent (quant-only) — fine.

**Phase 9 build/deploy facts (important):** Lovable's vite config defaulted to a NON-Vercel build (Nitro→Cloudflare) which would break on Vercel — FIXED by pinning `nitro: { preset: "vercel" }` in `app/vite.config.ts` (build now emits `.vercel/output` Build Output API v3; SSR + server fns + `/api/cron/snapshot` all run as the `__server` function). **`vercel.json` lives at `app/vercel.json`** (NOT repo root) because Vercel Root Directory = `app/` — a root-level vercel.json would be ignored and the cron would never register. Cron: `{ "crons": [{ "path": "/api/cron/snapshot", "schedule": "0 22 * * *" }] }`. `.vercel/` gitignored. `DEPLOYMENT.md` runbook at repo root has the full click-by-click. Vercel auto-sends `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` to cron when `CRON_SECRET` env is set (matches our endpoint).

(2) Free Vercel URL (e.g. `*.vercel.app`); custom domain later. (3) GitHub repo stays PRIVATE. Hosting: Vercel (frontend + cron) + existing Supabase project as prod (it's already cloud). Vercel Cron hits `/api/cron/snapshot` daily — Vercel auto-sends `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` when `CRON_SECRET` env is set, which matches our endpoint. Must add the Vercel URL to Supabase Auth Site URL/redirects, and turn email confirmation back ON for launch.

**Money formulas (single source of truth, in dashboard code):** total_value = cash + Σ(qty×live price); today's Δ$ = Σ(qty×dayChange) [Finnhub change-vs-prior-close]; today's Δ% = todayΔ$/(total_value−todayΔ$); total return $ = total_value − 100000; total return % = (total_value−100000)/100000×100.

**Phase 8 scheduling decision (CTO, 2026-06-16):** daily `portfolio_snapshots` writer runs as a server function triggered by **Vercel Cron** (built-in once deployed in Phase 9) — fits the "server functions + server-only keys" pattern, zero extra infra. In Phase 8 we build the writer + migration + a token-protected manual trigger and test it by hand; the automatic daily schedule gets wired at deploy (Phase 9). Initial baseline seeded so the dashboard value chart isn't empty; reconstructing historical value from the transaction ledger + historical prices noted as an optional later enhancement.

Flow: browser → index.ts → server fn → cache → provider → Twelve Data. Phase 4 call sites unchanged (same signatures, now async).

**✅ RESOLVED architecture decision (2026-06-16): server-side logic = TanStack Start server functions** (not Supabase Edge Functions). Applies to market-data fetching (Phase 5) and trade execution (Phase 6). Reason: simpler for a solo founder — same codebase, shared TS types, one deploy, no Deno/separate function pipeline. Market-data API key lives in a SERVER-ONLY env var (never `VITE_`/public, never in repo). Supabase stays the DB/auth/RLS layer. CLAUDE.md rule #1 and ARCHITECTURE.md updated to match.

**GitHub auth:** Venky's machine is authenticated via `gh` CLI (logged in as Venkat100, `repo`/`read:org`/`gist` scopes) — future pushes work without re-auth. ⚠️ **Gotcha fixed 2026-07-31:** the original `gh` install (Phase 2) had landed in `/tmp/gh_2.94.0_macOS_arm64/bin/gh`, and `~/.gitconfig`'s `credential.https://github.com.helper` pointed straight at that path. `/tmp` gets cleared (reboot/cleanup), which silently broke `git push` with `fatal: could not read Username for 'https://github.com'` — no working credential fallback, since the github.com-specific helper entry overrides the global `osxkeychain` one. **Fixed:** `gh` reinstalled at the PERMANENT `~/.local/bin/gh` (v2.97.0, added to PATH via `~/.bash_profile`), re-authenticated (device/web flow), and `~/.gitconfig`'s stale `credential.https://github.com.helper` / `credential.https://gist.github.com.helper` entries replaced with `!/Users/venkatpraveen/.local/bin/gh auth git-credential` (via `gh auth setup-git`, run by Venky himself — Claude Code doesn't edit git config, even authorized, per its own hard rule). Verified: `git push origin main` succeeded, local HEAD == `origin/main` HEAD (`b553782`), `git push --dry-run` → "Everything up-to-date". If this breaks again, check `~/.gitconfig`'s credential helper path first before assuming a real auth/permissions problem.
**Supabase project:** `papertrader` is live. Security: Data API ON, auto-expose-new-tables OFF, automatic RLS ON. `profiles` table + `handle_new_user` trigger applied via SQL Editor (migration `supabase/migrations/0001_init_profiles.sql`). Email confirmation turned OFF for dev testing (turn back ON before launch). URL + publishable key live in `app/.env` (gitignored, never pushed).
**Auth implementation notes:** single Supabase client at `app/src/lib/supabase/client.ts`; `AuthProvider`/`useAuth` at `app/src/lib/auth/auth-context.tsx`; route guard `AuthGate` in `routes/app.tsx` is CLIENT-side on purpose (session lives in browser, not SSR server). DB types must be `type` aliases not `interface` or supabase-js generics collapse to `never`.
**Loose ends:** test user `papertrader-verify+...@example.com` still in Supabase Auth→Users (deletable). Landing page "Try simulator/Explore demo" buttons redirect to login since `/app/*` is gated — see backlog "public simulator preview".

**⚠️ Stack correction:** The Lovable frontend is a **TanStack Start** app (full-stack React with TanStack Router + a built-in server layer), NOT a plain Vite SPA as the docs originally assumed. Docs updated. Implication: backend/server logic could live in TanStack Start server functions OR Supabase Edge Functions — decision deferred to backend phase (leaning TanStack server functions for market data, Supabase for DB/auth/RLS).

## 1. The people & the working model

- **Venky** (venkatpraveen1@gmail.com) — product owner / founder. Driving the project, makes final calls. Prefers concise, direct communication. Is not coding much himself by choice — wants the agent to do most of the coding.
- **Claude in Cowork** — acting **CTO + product manager**. Plans architecture, makes technical decisions, and writes precise prompts for Venky to run elsewhere.
- **Claude Code** — the **implementation agent**, connected to this folder (`Paper Trading - Venk` on Venky's desktop). Does the actual coding.
- **Lovable** — AI app builder generating the React frontend design.

**The core workflow loop:**
1. Cowork (CTO) and Venky plan together.
2. Cowork writes a precise, copy-paste-ready prompt.
3. Venky pastes it into **Lovable** (for frontend/design) or **Claude Code** (for implementation).
4. Venky brings the result back (screenshot, error, "it worked," or "this looks thin").
5. We iterate in small, tight loops.

Venky explicitly asked: "be my CTO and my product person."

**Standing communication rule (always follow this):** Venky is a product person, NOT technical. Every time he pastes back output from Claude Code, the CTO (Cowork) must respond in two clearly separated parts:
1. **"What just happened"** — plain English, product perspective. What was built/changed, what it means for the app and the user, any problems or decisions needed, whether we're on track. No technical jargon.
2. **"Your next prompt"** — the fully technical, specific, copy-paste-ready prompt for Claude Code.
Never make Venky parse raw technical output himself.

---

## 2. What we're building (one paragraph)

**PaperTrader** — a full-stack paper-trading web app. Users get $100,000 of virtual money, trade real stock tickers at live market prices (no real money), track a live portfolio with profit/loss, and run **"what if I had invested in this stock back then"** simulations against real historical data. It's a simulation / education tool — no real brokerage, no real money movement. The **What-If Simulator** is the flagship differentiator (most paper-trading apps only trade forward; ours looks backward too).

Full detail in `README.md`.

---

## 3. Decisions locked so far (with reasoning)

| Decision | Choice | Why |
|----------|--------|-----|
| Scope of v1 | Comprehensive full platform (not just a calculator) | Venky wants something "comprehensive" / full-fledged. |
| Data needs | Live **+** historical prices | Venky chose this explicitly. Needs a feed with both. |
| Platform | Web app | CTO pick; most flexible, easy to share/deploy. |
| Venky's involvement | Agent does most coding | Venky's choice; keep prompts explicit and beginner-safe. |
| Frontend stack | React + **TanStack Start** + TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Recharts | What Lovable actually generated (full-stack React w/ a server layer, NOT a plain Vite SPA). |
| Backend | **Supabase** (Postgres + Auth + RLS) for DB/auth; **TanStack Start server functions** for server-side logic | Supabase one-click from Lovable. Server logic (market data, trades) runs in TanStack server functions, not Supabase Edge Functions — simpler for a solo founder (one codebase, shared types, one deploy). |
| Market data | **Twelve Data primary** (key in use), **Finnhub** fallback (thin) | Both free tiers have live + historical. Isolated behind `lib/marketData/`. Twelve Data free ≈ 8 credits/min → small universe + TTL cache. |
| Source control | GitHub (`venk-cc-first-project`, monorepo) | Repo of record. Lovable sync is dead post-import; Claude Code is source of truth. |
| Hosting (planned) | Vercel (frontend) + Supabase cloud (backend) | Cheap/free to start. |

**Hard architectural rules (non-negotiable):**
1. Never call the market API from the browser — all market data via **TanStack Start server functions**; API key in a SERVER-ONLY env var (no `VITE_`), never in repo.
2. Never trust client-supplied prices/balances — trades execute server-side against a server-fetched price; user identity from the verified JWT, never a client-sent id.
3. Row-Level Security on every user table.
4. Isolate the data provider — only `app/src/lib/marketData/` may import Twelve Data/Finnhub.
5. It's a simulation — no real brokerage, no real money.

Full data model and logic in `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## 4. The plan / phases (summary — full version in ROADMAP.md)

- **Phase 0 — Planning** ✅ done.
- **Phase 1 — Frontend design in Lovable** ✅ done (full UI, all 8 screens, imported into `app/`).
- **Phase 2 — Repo & GitHub** ✅ done (monorepo on `origin/main`).
- **Phase 3 — Auth & accounts** ✅ done (Supabase auth, `profiles`, $100k seed).
- **Phase 4 — Database & portfolio plumbing** ✅ done (real tables + RLS, mock data replaced).
- **Phase 5 — Live market data** ✅ done (marketData adapter + server functions, real prices).
- **Phase 6 — Trading engine** ✅ implemented + verified, pending push (server-side buy/sell).
- **Phase 7 — What-If Simulator** ⬅️ NEXT. The flagship feature, real history + SPY comparison.
- **Phase 8 — Live tracking & polish.** Snapshots, polling, states, mobile.
- **Phase 9 — Launch.** Deploy, secrets, public repo.

**Sequencing note (resolved by momentum):** we built in the original order rather than Simulator-first; no objection raised. Simulator is Phase 7, next up.

---

## 5. The 8 frontend screens (what Lovable is building)

1. Landing page (marketing).
2. Auth (login + signup).
3. Dashboard — stat cards (portfolio value, buying power, today's change, total return), big value chart with range toggles, holdings table, watchlist + top movers sidebar.
4. Stock detail — price chart, key stats, buy/sell order panel (market + limit), tabs.
5. **What-If Simulator** — pick stock + past date + amount → value today, charted vs. S&P 500. The hero screen.
6. Markets — searchable/filterable stock list with sparklines.
7. Portfolio / Activity — allocation donut + transaction history.
8. Settings — profile, theme toggle, reset paper account to $100k.

Plus global shell: left sidebar nav, top bar with global search + avatar. Dark mode default. Every user starts with $100,000 virtual cash.

> Note: Lovable often "soft-builds" later screens — nails the first few, stubs the rest. Expect 1–3 follow-up prompts to flesh out thin screens (especially the Simulator). This is normal.

## 7. The exact Lovable design prompt we used (for reference / reuse)

> Build a polished, modern paper trading platform called **PaperTrader** — a web app where users practice investing with virtual money and run "what if I had invested" simulations. No real money. Build the full UI with realistic mock data; backend + market-data API connected later.
>
> Design system: clean fintech aesthetic (Robinhood meets Bloomberg-lite), dark mode default + light toggle, green gains / red losses, shadcn/ui + Recharts, Inter font, responsive.
>
> Screens: (1) Landing, (2) Auth login/signup, (3) Dashboard with stat cards + portfolio value chart with 1D/1W/1M/3M/1Y/ALL toggles + holdings table + watchlist/top-movers sidebar, (4) Stock detail with price chart + key stats + buy/sell panel (market & limit) + tabs, (5) What-If Simulator (stock + date + amount → value today, charted vs S&P 500 — make it impressive), (6) Markets searchable list with sparklines, (7) Portfolio/Activity with allocation donut + transaction history, (8) Settings with theme toggle + reset to $100k.
>
> Global: left sidebar nav, top bar with global search + avatar. Every user starts with $100,000 virtual cash. Mock data using AAPL/TSLA/NVDA/MSFT/AMZN/GOOGL with realistic histories. Structure so mock data is easy to swap for real API calls. Prioritize beautiful, cohesive, production-quality design; charts and What-If simulator are the visual highlights.

- **2026-08-15 (`verify-hardening-pass.ts`'s last intermittent-failure source fixed at the root — a real production bug found via instrumentation, not a timeout enlarged to make the flake go away — BUILT, VERIFIED with 3 consecutive full-suite runs, PUSHED to origin/main (`a44fc00`)) — Issue #29 filed, verified, and closed.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** this script has now failed at least four times across sessions — twice on live-price drift (already root-caused and fixed) and twice on step timeouts — and passes clean in isolation every single time. Diagnose first, don't guess; instrument if needed; report the specific call and its actual elapsed time vs. budget. Test the RateLimiter-queueing hypothesis specifically (does a per-step timeout measure queue wait, not the call itself?) and confirm or rule it out with evidence. Fix at the right layer, ranked: lighter/faster script beats a timeout fix; a timeout measuring the wrong thing beats simply enlarging it; moving the step earlier in run order is a dodge, not a fix, unless proven the sole issue. Do NOT just enlarge the timeout — if the budget genuinely needs raising, justify the number against measured data. Proof standard: 3 consecutive full-suite runs, 31/31 on all three, reporting the previously-failing step's elapsed time each run.
  - **Diagnosis, with real instrumentation:** temporarily added `rateLimiterStats()` (a read-only passthrough to the module-level `RateLimiter`'s `windowCount` getter) to `finnhub.server.ts`, plus `fetchStats()`-bracketed logging around the two most Finnhub-call-heavy steps in the script (the agent thinker's cold universe scan, and the `runSnapshots` step itself), then ran the script repeatedly in isolation. **The RateLimiter-queueing hypothesis was tested directly and RULED OUT**: `RateLimiter` is a plain in-memory class (`const limiter = new RateLimiter(50, 6)` at module scope in `finnhub.server.ts`) with zero Postgres/Redis backing — confirmed by reading its full implementation. Each `npx vite-node <script>.ts` invocation in the regression-suite loop is a genuinely separate OS process, so this module-level state cannot carry over from one script to the next; there is no cross-script queue for a later script to be "waiting in line" behind. Measured `windowCount` across three isolated diagnostic runs stayed at 23–33 out of the 50 cap by the time `runSnapshots` was reached — nowhere near queue-forcing territory. This class of failure needed a different explanation.
  - **The real root cause, found by reading — not assuming — what `runSnapshots` actually calls:** `getOptionsValueByUser()` in `lib/options/valuation.server.ts` took NO parameter and unconditionally ran `admin.from("option_positions").select("*")` — no `user_id` filter, ever — then live-priced (Finnhub quote + Twelve Data realized-vol, PER DISTINCT UNDERLYING SYMBOL) the ENTIRE resulting set. `writer.server.ts`'s own header comment states the exact opposite intent for this parameter ("Optionally scope to a single user ... so we don't fan out a price fetch across the whole user base"), and every OTHER query in the same function (`profiles`, `holdings`, `agent_config`, `agent_holdings`) correctly honors `onlyUserId` via the established `(one ? q.eq(...) : q)` pattern — only the options leg, added later (O3), was never wired into that scoping. This is why the failure is genuinely intermittent and irreproducible in a clean isolated run: elapsed time and provider-call volume for this ONE call scale with however many DISTINCT option symbols are open across EVERY real and leftover-test account in the database at that exact moment — a number this script has zero control over and that varies run to run — not with the one throwaway test user's own single NVDA position. A live check during the fix confirmed this wasn't hypothetical: an unscoped call touched **6 distinct real user IDs** with genuinely open option positions at that moment.
  - **Fixed at the correct layer, per the kickoff's own ranking:** `getOptionsValueByUser(onlyUserId?: string)` now filters by `user_id` when a scope is given (same `(onlyUserId ? q.eq(...) : q)` idiom the rest of the file already uses), and `writer.server.ts` threads its own `one` through (`getOptionsValueByUser(one)`). This is "make the script's dependency lighter/faster," the top-ranked option — a real production correctness fix (any genuine on-demand/admin-triggered single-user snapshot in production was previously incurring a full-database options fan-out it never needed or asked for), not merely something invented to make a test pass. The unscoped path (the real daily cron, which legitimately needs the whole book) is byte-identical to before — `onlyUserId ? ... : q` falls through to the exact same unfiltered query when omitted. No timeout was enlarged anywhere as part of this fix.
  - **New assertions in `verify-hardening-pass.ts` prove the fix, not just assert the number moved:** seeds a SECOND throwaway user with a real `option_positions` row on a different underlying (MSFT, direct insert — `option_positions` has full service_role CRUD grants, unlike the append-only `transactions` ledger), confirms via a direct count query that both throwaway users genuinely have an open position (so the test isn't vacuous), then calls `getOptionsValueByUser(uid)` (scoped) and confirms its returned map has NO entry for the other user and made exactly 1 provider fetch (only NVDA, never MSFT) — then calls `getOptionsValueByUser()` (unscoped) and confirms it STILL includes both users, proving the real production cron path is unaffected. The second throwaway user is deleted immediately after (cascades its option_positions row).
  - **Verified properly — 3 consecutive full-suite runs, not one lucky pass, per explicit instruction:** the full 31-script `verify-*.ts` suite was run three times in a row: **31/31 exit 0, zero `❌` markers, on all three runs.** The previously-failing step's elapsed time (against its unchanged 25,000ms budget) was measured each run: **1,938ms (run 1), 1,628ms (run 2), 2,121ms (run 3)** — 91–93.5% headroom every time, a consistent, comfortable margin rather than three narrow escapes. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean.
  - **Commit:** `lib/options/valuation.server.ts` (the scoping fix) + `lib/snapshots/writer.server.ts` (threading `onlyUserId` through) + `verify-hardening-pass.ts` (the new scoping-proof assertions) + this HANDOFF update, one commit (`a44fc00`) — the fix, its one call site, and the test that proves it are a single indivisible change. **PUSHED to origin/main** — reported first, then pushed on explicit follow-up instruction.

- **2026-08-15 (Auth page left panel — rotating feature showcase — BUILT, VERIFIED with in-page precision logging + a genuine reduced-motion emulation, PUSHED to origin/main (`498edf1`)) — Issue #28 filed, verified, and closed.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** the left brand panel on `/auth` is nearly empty (a testimonial at the bottom, nothing else), desktop-only so zero mobile risk. Build a 4-6 slide rotating showcase using the app's own visual language, no product screenshots (would go stale immediately, as just proven by the stock-page layout change the same day). Lead with differentiators (AI agent, scenario challenges, simulator, AI insights), then table stakes. Honest copy, reused from the landing page where it fits. Motion: ~5-6s/slide, pause on hover, MUST respect prefers-reduced-motion (single static slide, no animation at all) as a non-negotiable accessibility requirement. CSS/JS only, no new dependencies, must not delay or interfere with the auth form. Also: the existing testimonial reads as fabricated — remove or transparently reframe it, CTO's call which.
  - **The testimonial: removed, not reframed.** "I tested 14 strategies before risking a dollar... — a curious investor" has no real attribution and reads as invented. This product has been careful about honesty everywhere else this session alone — real Top Movers data instead of stale mock prices, admin cost figures always labeled as estimates never measured, analyst counts explicitly captioned as "not a prediction or a recommendation from us," no claim of price targets since that endpoint is genuinely paywalled — a fabricated quote was the one remaining inconsistency. A "transparently labeled as illustrative" fake quote was considered and rejected: it would still occupy the exact space a real, honest feature tour could fill instead, for zero benefit over just removing it.
  - **Built:** `hooks/use-reduced-motion.tsx` (new) — `usePrefersReducedMotion()`, deliberately mirroring `use-mobile.tsx`'s existing `matchMedia` + `addEventListener("change", ...)` pattern rather than inventing a different shape for the same kind of check. `components/AuthFeatureShowcase.tsx` (new) — `SLIDES` array of 6 `{icon, title, body}` entries, every `title`/`body` string copy-pasted verbatim from `routes/index.tsx`'s own landing-page feature grid (never re-worded), ordered: AI portfolio agent → Scenario challenges → What-if simulator → AI stock insights → Options trading → Journal & coaching (the last one merges two landing-page sentences into one honest combined sentence rather than spending a 7th slide on it, keeping the total inside the requested 4-6). `SLIDE_MS = 5500`. Auto-advance via a single `setInterval` in a `useEffect` keyed on `[reducedMotion, paused]` — the effect's own early-return (`if (reducedMotion || paused) return;`) is the ENTIRE pause/reduced-motion mechanism, no separate code paths to keep in sync. Crossfade transition reuses `tailwindcss-animate`'s `animate-in fade-in duration-700` utility classes (already a real dependency — confirmed via grep, already used by `components/ui/dialog.tsx`'s own open/close animation — so this adds zero new packages), restarted on every slide change via `key={activeIndex}` forcing a DOM remount rather than any animation library. Dot indicators are real `<button>`s (clickable to jump to a slide directly, `aria-label`/`aria-current` set) — hidden entirely under reduced motion rather than left as dead, non-advancing controls.
  - **`routes/auth.tsx`**: removed the testimonial `<div>`, added `import { AuthFeatureShowcase } from "@/components/AuthFeatureShowcase"`, rendered in its place inside the existing `hidden lg:flex` panel — no other structural change to the page, the panel's logo/decorative-blob/layout are all untouched.
  - **Verification, real evidence not eyeballed screenshots (a screenshot mid-fade genuinely IS ambiguous — caught one early on that looked like a "jump" until precision-logged evidence explained it as normal tool-round-trip latency, not a bug):**
    - **Rotation timing**: an in-page `setInterval`-based logger (`window.__showcaseLog`, sampling the rendered `<h3>` title once per second into a plain array, read back afterward) proved the exact cadence — held one title for ~4 real seconds, transitioned, held the next for ~5 real seconds — matching `SLIDE_MS=5500` — and proved correct forward-only progression with modular wraparound (index 5 → 0 → 1 observed cleanly, never backward, never skipping).
    - **Hover-pause**: hovered the card, ran the same style of in-page logger for **30+ real seconds** (over 5x one slide interval) — title stayed on the exact same slide for all 21 samples, zero advancement. Moved the mouse away and confirmed the title changed on the next check, proving resume-on-leave.
    - **prefers-reduced-motion — the non-negotiable requirement, tested for real:** this environment has no direct "emulate reduced motion" control, so `window.matchMedia` was monkey-patched in-page to return `matches: true` specifically for the reduced-motion query (other queries passed through to the real implementation unchanged), then an SPA-internal remount was forced by clicking the real in-app `Link`s (logo → landing page → "Sign in" → back to `/auth`) — client-side routing keeps the same JS realm/`window` object, so the patch survived the remount and the hook's `useEffect` re-evaluated against it fresh. Confirmed simultaneously in one query: `title: "AI portfolio agent"` (slide 0, the fixed static slide), `dotsPresent: 0`, and `cardClasses` containing NO `animate-in`/`fade-in` — then confirmed genuinely static by sampling every 2s for 10+ seconds (11 samples, one single unique title the entire time). A subsequent real full-page reload (clearing the patch) confirmed normal rotation resumed correctly — the patch doesn't leak or break anything once removed.
    - **Auth form unaffected**: real sign-in via a throwaway `pt-authpanel-verify-…@example.org` account through the actual form (not a direct Supabase call) succeeded and landed on the Dashboard; "Forgot password?" correctly switches to the reset-link form and "Back to sign in" correctly restores it; "Create account" tab renders the full signup form (name/email/password/terms checkbox/$25,000 messaging) correctly. Throwaway account deleted after.
    - **Mobile (375px)**: confirmed the showcase's `<h3>` has `offsetParent === null` (genuinely not rendered visually, not just scrolled off) — same `hidden lg:flex` mechanism the original testimonial always used, untouched by this diff, so this is a property this page already had, not a new risk. Screenshot confirms the mobile auth view is visually identical to before the change.
    - **No new dependencies**: `git diff --stat` on `package.json`/`package-lock.json` shows zero changes.
  - **Full regression suite (31 `verify-*.ts` scripts): 31/31 exit 0, zero `❌` markers.** `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean.
  - **Commit:** `hooks/use-reduced-motion.tsx` + `components/AuthFeatureShowcase.tsx` + `routes/auth.tsx` (testimonial removal + wiring) + this HANDOFF update, one commit (`498edf1`) — the hook, the component, and its one call site are a single indivisible feature with no meaningful split point. **PUSHED to origin/main** — reported first, then pushed on explicit follow-up instruction.

- **2026-08-15 (Stock Detail layout — move News/About into the right column, below the order panel — BUILT, VERIFIED with real DOM measurement + screenshots, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Issue #27 filed and In progress.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** Venky's request — News and About are too hidden behind the tab bar; move both into the right column, below the order panel, which is currently empty there on tall screens. MOVE don't duplicate (tab bar drops to 4: Your position / Options / Recent trades / Journal). Right column order: About first, then News, redesigned for the ~350px width (2-line headline, source, relative time, still `target="_blank"`). Mobile ordering is the risk — decide and justify the stack deliberately (chart → order panel → key stats → earnings/analysts → AI insight → About → News → tabs), right-rail content must not jump ahead of left-column content on small screens. Earnings/analyst card and Key Stats stay put (need width). Empty cases hidden entirely, same rule as elsewhere.
  - **The `display:contents` technique, chosen deliberately over the alternatives:** the two-column desktop layout (`grid lg:grid-cols-[1fr_360px]`) needed the right column's content (order panel/About/News) to INTERLEAVE with the left column's content (chart/Key Stats/earnings/insight/tabs) on mobile, not simply stack after it — a plain single-column fallback (today's pre-existing behavior for the two column-wrapper divs) would have put the ENTIRE left column before the ENTIRE right column, burying the order panel below the tabs card on mobile. CSS Grid `grid-template-areas` was considered and rejected — precise row-by-row alignment between two independent-height columns is fragile and unnecessary (the columns are ALREADY independent-height today, that's not a new property this change introduces). The chosen fix: both column wrapper divs get `contents lg:flex lg:flex-col lg:gap-6` — `display:contents` below `lg:` removes the wrapper from the box model entirely, promoting its own children to be direct siblings in the OUTER grid, where each card's own `order-N` class (1 through 8, matching the kickoff's exact specified sequence) determines mobile position and the outer grid's own `gap-6` spaces everything uniformly (no need for the wrappers' own `space-y-6` at that breakpoint). At `lg:` and up, the wrappers switch to real `flex flex-col` containers, and every card's `lg:order-none` hands control back to plain DOM/JSX order within its own column — which is why the JSX itself only needed to declare each card ONCE, in one place, wrapped in a small `order-N lg:order-none` div; no duplicated markup anywhere.
  - **`NewsTab`→`NewsCard`, redesigned for the narrow column**: previously just `TabsContent` body sharing the wide tab card's own chrome; now a genuine standalone `Card` with its own header. Headline goes from a single unclamped line (fine at full tab width, would have overflowed or wrapped awkwardly at 350px) to `line-clamp-2`, source+relative-time unchanged, external link icon/behavior unchanged (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, confirmed via a live DOM query on real rendered anchors). Genuinely empty (or a rate-limited/error state, unchanged from before) still degrades exactly as before — the whole card renders `null` when there's truly no news, same "hide entirely, no empty heading" rule as the Earnings & analysts card and Key Stats.
  - **`AboutTab`→`AboutCard`**: no content redesign needed — already label/value pairs, confirmed to read fine at the new width. The one real cleanup: its old `sm:grid-cols-3` (from the wide-tab-content layout) never actually triggers inside a ~350px column (640px breakpoint), so it was DEAD styling that would have misled a future reader into thinking a 3-column layout was live here — replaced with an explicit `grid-cols-1` stack that matches its actual always-narrow rendering context. No genuine "fully empty" case exists for About once a quote has loaded (even the ETF degradation path always shows a real name/logo/exchange plus a meaningful "Exchange-traded fund" label, never a blank card) — considered adding a hard hide-if-ETF condition per a literal reading of the kickoff's "hide if no company profile" phrasing, but concluded (and documented in a code comment) that the EXISTING graceful degradation already satisfies the real intent (never an empty heading), so no artificial new hide condition was invented where the design was already correct.
  - **Verification, real evidence not assumption:**
    - **Desktop (AAPL):** DOM query on every `[class*="text-base"]` CardTitle in the page confirms the exact sequence `Key stats, Earnings & analysts, AI Insight, Place paper order, [empty], Confirm buy · 1 AAPL, About, News` — left column then right column, About immediately after the order panel, News after About, nothing duplicated (each title string appears exactly once in the whole DOM). Precise `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement: order panel bottom at y=705, About top at y=729 — a 24px gap, exactly the grid's own `gap-6`, proving genuinely no dead space rather than just "less" dead space. All three (order panel/About/News) share the identical x=913 left offset, confirming they're really in the same column.
    - **Tab bar:** `[role="tab"]` query returns exactly `["Your position", "Options", "Recent trades", "Journal"]` — 4 items, neither News nor About present.
    - **ETF (VOO):** Earnings & analysts card and the "Related stocks" section both confirmed absent from the DOM via direct query (not just visually collapsed). About shows the real graceful-degradation path (name "Vanguard S&P 500 ETF", "Exchange-traded fund" label, Market cap/Country as "—"). News is NOT hidden for VOO — it genuinely has market-relevant news items, correctly rendered; confirms the hide rule is keyed on actual emptiness, not on "is this an ETF."
    - **Mobile (375px, AAPL):** exact section order proven via `getBoundingClientRect()` on all 8 sections in one pass: chart(142) → order panel "Place paper order"(679) → Key stats(1228) → Earnings & analysts(1766) → AI Insight(2321) → About(2563) → News(3076) → tabs(3809) — byte-for-byte the sequence specified in the kickoff. Screenshots at 4 scroll positions visually confirm the same sequence (chart+order-panel-start; Key-stats+Earnings-start; AI-Insight+About+Related-stocks+News-start; News-rows+tabs-row) — a session-specific Browser-pane quirk reported the viewport as 375px while the real CSS engine rendered at 429px (same class of artifact documented in the prior enrichment-phase-2 entry, at a different width this session) but caused zero horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth === innerWidth` confirmed) and didn't prevent clean screenshots this time.
    - Throwaway verification account (`pt-layout-verify-…@example.org`) created via the harness, used for the login-gated views above, fully deleted after — nothing test-only left behind.
  - **Full regression suite (31 `verify-*.ts` scripts): 30/31 clean on the full run.** The one non-pass, `verify-hardening-pass.ts` (a `STEP TIMEOUT` on `runSnapshots`), re-ran clean in isolation immediately after — confirmed transient/unrelated, since this entire change is `app.stock.$symbol.tsx` markup/CSS only, touching zero server functions, zero database code, zero anything `verify-hardening-pass.ts` could plausibly be sensitive to. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean.
  - **Commit:** `app.stock.$symbol.tsx` (the full layout restructure) + this HANDOFF update, one commit (`03d3219`) — kept as one rather than split further, since the grid restructuring, the two component renames, and the narrow-width News redesign are all one inseparable change to the same render tree (splitting them would leave intermediate commits in a broken/half-migrated state). **PUSHED to origin/main** — reported first, then pushed on explicit follow-up instruction.

- **2026-08-14 (Stock page enrichment, phase 2 — free Finnhub endpoints — BUILT, VERIFIED with real evidence + 2 real bugs found and fixed along the way, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Issue #26 filed and In progress.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** run after the Tier-2 pass; the earlier probe found 6 accessible free Finnhub endpoints, build 4 (next earnings date, EPS surprise history, analyst recommendation trend, peer tickers), deliberately skip insider sentiment/transactions with the reasoning documented in code (novices misread them; an insider sale is usually scheduled vesting, not bearish — revisit later with education attached). All four cached 24h+, ETFs must degrade cleanly (hide the section, no dash-grids, no empty headings), educational framing throughout (context, not advice — no price-target implication since that endpoint is paywalled and we don't have it). Verify against mega-cap/ETF/mid-cap, prove the cache is doing its job (repeat loads don't re-fetch), report the rate-limit arithmetic, screenshots 375px+desktop, full suite green, typecheck+build clean.
  - **Built in `finnhub.server.ts`** (extended the existing single Finnhub-adapter file rather than fragmenting into a new one — keeps "isolate the data provider" meaning ONE file, matching how `fhProfile`/`fhFundamentals`/`fhCompanyNews`/`fhMetrics` already live together): `fhNextEarnings` (`/calendar/earnings`, queries a ~240-day forward window and takes the soonest date — comfortably spans one quarterly cycle even right after a print), `fhEarningsSurprises` (`/stock/earnings`, last 4 quarters, sorted newest-first defensively rather than trusting provider order), `fhRecommendationTrend` (`/stock/recommendation`, last 4 months, same defensive sort), `fhPeers` (`/stock/peers`, excludes the requested symbol itself — Finnhub includes it in the raw response — dedupes, caps at 6). A combined `fhStockEnrichment(symbol)` fetches all four in parallel, each independently best-effort so one failing/empty field never blocks the other three. New `ENRICHMENT_TTL = 24h`. New types in `types.ts`: `NextEarnings`/`EarningsSurprise`/`RecommendationTrendPoint`/`StockEnrichment`. One new server function (`getStockEnrichmentFn` in `functions.ts`) and client wrapper (`getStockEnrichment` in `index.ts`) — no-auth, same pattern as quotes/candles/search/news, since none of this is user-specific.
  - **🐛 Real bug #1, found DURING the cache-hit verification the kickoff itself demanded, not before:** `fhNextEarnings` returning bare `undefined` for the genuine "ETF, no earnings" case failed `durableCached`'s L2 write — Postgres `price_cache.payload` is `jsonb NOT NULL`, and both `undefined` and (the first attempted fix) a bare `null` map to SQL NULL on the Supabase client's insert, not the valid JSON scalar `null` — confirmed live both times via the actual `"[price_cache] L2 write failed ... null value in column payload"` error. The durable half of the two-tier cache was silently never persisting the ETF case; only the in-process L1 tier ever "worked," meaning every cold serverless invocation would re-hit Finnhub for every ETF's earnings calendar, forever, undetected by anything short of actually running the cache-proof test this pass's own instructions required. Fixed by wrapping the cached value in a real, always-non-null object (`{ earnings: NextEarnings | null }`) — the OUTER value is never null, only the INNER field is, which is a completely different (and valid) thing from the column itself being SQL NULL — unwrapped back to plain `NextEarnings | undefined` at the public return. Cache kind renamed `"earningsCalendar"` → `"nextEarnings"` so this session's own stale rows (written under the broken pre-fix shape while chasing the bug) couldn't shadow the fix for the rest of the 24h TTL.
  - **🐛 Real bug #2, found on the FIRST full-suite re-run after building the fix above:** the new `verify-stock-enrichment.ts`'s own cache-proof section used a hardcoded test symbol (`"CRM"`) — correct on a script's first-ever run, but a second run of the SAME script within the same 24h TTL hits its own prior run's now-warm cache and fails the "1st call makes exactly 4 requests" assertion, the identical non-idempotent-test failure class already root-caused once THIS SAME DAY in `verify-hardening-pass.ts` (see the entry above). Fixed the same way `verify-live-prices.ts` already solves it: an explicit "clean slate" step (`__clearL1ForTest()` + a scoped `price_cache` delete for that symbol across all 4 new cache kinds) before the cache-proof assertions, plus an end-of-run cleanup delete so the NEXT run starts clean too. Proven fixed by running the script twice back-to-back, not just once — both runs show the identical exactly-4-then-0 result.
  - **UI, `app.stock.$symbol.tsx`**: a new "Earnings & analysts" card between Key Stats and AI Insight — `NextEarningsRow` (date + before/after-market badge + consensus EPS/revenue), `EarningsSurpriseChart` (a diverging bar per quarter, green=beat/red=miss, width scaled to the largest surprise in the set — visualized, not a raw table, per the kickoff), `AnalystRecommendationBar` (a single proportional segmented bar for the latest month, zero-count segments hidden from both the bar and its legend). The WHOLE card returns `null` (not an empty shell) when all three are absent — the ETF case. `AboutTab` gained a `peers` prop and a "Related stocks" section (a row of symbol pills, each a real `Link` to that peer's own Stock Detail page) — same hide-entirely-if-empty rule. Every new section carries the same educational-framing disclaimer sentence, worded to make zero advice/prediction/recommendation implication and to never imply PaperTrader has price targets (the one endpoint that's genuinely paywalled on this tier).
  - **Live verification, real Finnhub, zero mocks:** AAPL (mega-cap) — real Oct 28 2026 earnings date w/ EPS $2.02/Revenue $115.06B estimate, 4 real quarterly surprises (+2.4%/+4.2%/+1.1%/−0.9%), 54 real analysts (13/24/14/3/0 across the 5 tiers, `strongSell=0` correctly hidden from both bar and legend), 6 real peers (DELL/SNDK/WDC/HPE/NTAP/P). VOO (ETF) — confirmed the ENTIRE "Earnings & analysts" card is absent from the DOM (not hidden via CSS, genuinely not rendered), and the About tab has no "Related stocks" section at all — both proven via direct DOM/text inspection, not assumed from the empty-array contract. SOFI (mid-cap, unprofitable — already known from the Tier-1 Key Stats fix to show a real negative net margin) — real Oct 26 2026 earnings date, 4 real surprises including a genuine miss (−1.2%), 30 real analysts across all 5 tiers INCLUDING a nonzero `strongSell=1` (confirming the zero-count-hides / nonzero-count-shows logic works both directions), 6 real peers (AXP/COF/SYF/ALLY/FCFS/OMF).
  - **Cache-hit proof (the kickoff's explicit "prove it, don't just claim it" ask) — `verify-stock-enrichment.ts`, new, added to the permanent suite:** a genuinely cold symbol (clean-slated first, per the idempotency fix above) makes EXACTLY 4 real Finnhub requests on its first call and ZERO additional requests on a second call in the same process — proven, not assumed, and proven twice (the idempotency re-run) to rule out the fix itself being a fluke.
  - **Rate-limit arithmetic, reported per instruction:** all 4 endpoints cached 24h per symbol — steady-state cost is per DISTINCT symbol viewed per DAY, not per page view, since every viewer of an already-warm symbol within 24h is a cache hit (L1 in-process, L2 durable Postgres survives cold starts). Worst case for a burst of N never-before-seen symbols in one minute is 4×N Finnhub calls, sharing the SAME module-level `RateLimiter(50,6)` every other Finnhub call already goes through — not a new risk, self-throttled by existing infrastructure. At this app's real scale (a handful of confirmed real accounts, per the Tier-1 test-account audit), even an implausible 15 brand-new symbols in one minute is 60 calls/min, still inside the free-tier cap.
  - **375px mobile screenshots:** a session-specific Browser-pane artifact — the mobile viewport preset reports 375px (`outerWidth`/`screenWidth`/`visualViewport.width` all correctly say 375) but the real CSS layout engine renders at 555px (`window.innerWidth`), reproduced on a completely fresh tab and with explicit `width:375` dimensions, ruling out stale per-tab state — meant every screenshot showed content cropped at 375px against a 555px-wide real render, an artifact of the capture pipeline, not the product. Verified instead via direct DOM measurement: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth` (zero horizontal overflow at whatever width the engine actually used) and an explicit `anyElementWiderThanViewport` check across every element in `<main>` returning `false`. Consistent with this project's established practice of accepting DOM evidence in place of a screenshot once a Browser-pane capture artifact is confirmed unrelated to the product (same standard applied to several blank-screenshot-at-scroll-position incidents earlier this same day).
  - **Full regression suite (31 `verify-*.ts` scripts, including the new one): 31/31 exit 0, zero `❌` markers** — genuinely green on the SECOND full run (the first run correctly caught the non-idempotent-test bug described above; that's the regression suite doing its job, not a flake to explain away). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean, secret grep clean.
