- **2026-08-08 (Options & Margin epic — FINAL HARDENING PASS — audited every cross-feature seam, found + fixed ONE real bug, re-ran the full regression suite, committed locally in 2 chunks) — NOT pushed. No new features, per instruction.**
  - **1. CROSS-FEATURE INTERACTION AUDIT — 5 seams, each reasoned through by reading the actual code, then proven live:**
    - **Options + margin (can you buy options on borrowed money, and does it count toward equity/maintenance?):** YES, intentionally, and consistently with the stock path. `execute_option_trade` (redefined in `0012_margin.sql`, not left at its O2-era shape) threads `p_positions_value` into `margin_buying_power()` exactly like `execute_trade`, borrows the shortfall, and logs a `'borrow'` margin_event — verified live: buying 1 NVDA call while cash was $0 (fully margin-committed) correctly borrowed money and logged a second `'borrow'` event alongside the stock one. `lib/margin/valuation.server.ts`'s `getPositionsValue()` — the SINGLE function both `execute_trade`/`execute_option_trade`/the monitor all call — sums stock holdings AND `getEnrichedOptionPositions()`, so option value has been correctly counted toward equity/maintenance since M1 shipped. No bug: an options-only margin account CAN be called, was never silently exempt.
    - **Expiry settlement + margin:** `settle_expired_option` (also redefined in `0012_margin.sql`) pays settlement proceeds toward the outstanding loan FIRST (`v_loan_repaid := least(v_total, v_margin_loan)`), remainder to cash, logs a `'repay'` event with `detail.source:'settlement'` — the exact same pattern as a voluntary `sell_to_close`. Live-verified: seeded an expired ITM NVDA call (same test seam O4's own verify script established — a direct `option_positions` insert with a past expiry, since there's no normal way to end up holding one), ran `runExpiryProcessing`, settlement credited $1,996.00 and the loan dropped by exactly $1,996.00, repay event logged correctly.
    - **Margin call liquidation + options:** the liquidator (`monitor.server.ts`'s `listPositionCandidates`) pools stocks AND option positions together by live market value and sells the single largest ENTIRELY, repeating until cleared — options are NOT exempt, so an options-heavy account is NOT un-rescuable. Live-verified by deliberately growing an option position until it was the account's single largest holding, then forcing a call: the liquidator correctly sold BOTH the stock and the option position (`liquidated: [{kind:"stock",...}, {kind:"option",...}]`).
    - **AI agent + margin:** fully isolated, confirmed by CODE (agent trades exclusively via `agent_execute_trade`, which never references `profiles.cash_balance`/`margin_loan`/`margin_enabled` anywhere in `0006_agent_execute.sql`, nor does any file under `lib/agent/`) AND live (ran the real thinker — quant-only, AI disabled to avoid burning API calls in a regression test — producing 4 real trades into `agent_holdings`, then confirmed `getPositionsValue` for the SAME account was unchanged by those trades, i.e. it only ever reads `holdings`+`option_positions`, never `agent_holdings`). The agent can never borrow (no code path exists for it to) and never distorts main-account equity math.
    - **Reset + everything:** re-ran C1b's reset scenario now that M2 exists, with stocks + options + an outstanding margin loan + active agent holdings ALL simultaneously live on one account — `reset_paper_account` still atomically clears all four (holdings/option_positions/agent_holdings/margin state), forgives the loan (documented), keeps every ledger, and writes exactly one `account_events` marker. No regression from M2 (M2 was UI-only, never touched the reset path).
  - **2. THE REAL BUG — Dashboard/snapshot showed gross assets, not equity.** Cross-checking Dashboard total, the Margin page's own equity, and the daily snapshot for one account holding everything at once (stocks + options, both partly bought on margin, + a margin loan + an active agent) found they DISAGREED. Root cause: `app.dashboard.tsx`'s `total` (Portfolio value / Total return), `app.stock.$symbol.tsx`'s `totalPortfolio` (the "% of portfolio" stat), and `lib/snapshots/writer.server.ts`'s `total` (the value chart's own source of truth) all computed `cash + holdings + options` — this formula predates margin (M1) and was never updated when margin landed, so it silently kept showing GROSS ASSETS while the Margin page (M2) correctly computes EQUITY (`cash + positions − loan`). For any margin account with an outstanding loan, Dashboard/the value chart overstated net worth by exactly the borrowed amount — live-proven with a real number: a genuine $61,062.66 loan caused the old formula to show $151,062.66 where the correct equity was $90,000.00, an overstatement of $61,062.66 to the cent. **Fixed** by subtracting `margin_loan` in all three places (`app.dashboard.tsx`, `app.stock.$symbol.tsx`, `lib/snapshots/writer.server.ts`, which also needed `margin_loan` added to its `profiles` select) — post-fix, Dashboard total / Margin page equity / snapshot `total_value` all agreed to the cent in the same live test, through expiry settlement, a forced call+liquidation, and the daily cron chain, and a fresh reset back to a clean $100,000 correctly showed $100,000 exactly (confirming the fix is a no-op at zero loan, i.e. no regression for the vast majority of non-margin users). Also fixed, found in passing: the stock-detail page's "% of portfolio" denominator silently excluded option value even before margin existed (comment literally said "same formula as the Dashboard" — it was faithfully copying the OLD, now-fixed bug) — now includes it.
  - **3. SNAPSHOT/VALUATION CONSISTENCY** — folded into the same live test as #2 above: Dashboard total, Margin page equity (`getPositionsValue` called directly, the exact function the monitor/trade functions use), and the daily snapshot's `total_value` (via a real `runSnapshots({onlyUserId})` call, not a re-derivation) all reconciled to the cent for the combined stocks+options+agent+margin account, both pre- and post-fix (pre-fix numbers computed inline for comparison, not by reverting code — see #2).
  - **4. CRON CHAIN ORDER** — confirmed the REAL order from `vercel.json` + the endpoint code (not assumed): `agent-thinker` cron fires `30 21 * * *` (21:30 UTC) running `runThinkerForAllAgents` → `runDailyBriefs`; `snapshot` cron fires `0 22 * * *` (22:00 UTC, 30 min later) running `runExpiryProcessing` → `runInterestAccrual` → `runMarginMonitor` → `runSnapshots`, each step in `endpoint.server.ts`/`cron.server.ts` wrapped in its OWN `try/catch` so a failure in any one can't block the ones after it — read directly from the shipped handler code, not reconstructed. This means the agent's daily trades land BEFORE expiry/interest/margin/snapshot each day, so by the time the snapshot captures "today," the agent's own moves are already reflected — logically sound, not a bug. Per-step isolation (a failure in expiry/interest/margin can't abort the snapshot write) is structurally guaranteed by that same try/catch-per-step shape in the real endpoint code; per-USER isolation within a batch (one bad account can't abort others) is likewise structurally guaranteed by a try/catch inside every batch loop (`monitor.server.ts`'s and `cron.server.ts`'s thinker/watchdog loops) — verified by reading those loops line-by-line rather than by deliberately corrupting a second account inside an otherwise-unscoped production batch call, which this pass deliberately avoided (see the safety note in `verify-hardening-pass.ts`'s own header) since `runMarginMonitor`/`runThinkerForAllAgents` only accept a SINGLE `onlyUserId`, not a list, and calling them unscoped would touch every real user in the live database. Ran the full real chain (both crons, real production composition and order) against one seeded account — full summary in the verification section below.
  - **5. FULL REGRESSION** — re-ran every `verify-*.ts` script in the repo. 11 of 12 real suites (`verify-blackscholes`, `verify-eventstudy`, `verify-eventstudy-live`, `verify-options-chain`, `verify-options-live`, `verify-options-trade-live`, `verify-margin-live`, `verify-options-expiry-live`, `verify-reset-account-live`, `verify-insights-fresh`, plus the new `verify-hardening-pass`) PASSED clean. One pre-existing flaky assertion was found and fixed (unrelated to any change in this pass): `verify-options-trade-live.ts` compared two independently-computed floats (`total` from Postgres numeric arithmetic vs. `premium×100×contracts` recomputed in JS) with strict `===`, which can differ by IEEE754 epsilon depending on the day's real live premium — switched to the file's own `closeTo()` tolerance helper. `verify-insights.ts` (a pre-M1-era script, predating the hardened-harness standing rule) HUNG with zero output and was killed after >10 minutes per the project's own standing rule — not investigated further (out of scope for an Options/Margin pass) and flagged as a residual risk below. `verify-margin-ui.ts` is a CLI control tool (create/state/force/setloan/monitor/delete), not a standalone pass/fail suite, and was exercised functionally throughout this pass rather than run bare.
  - **6. HOUSEKEEPING** — found and deleted 4 leftover throwaway test users (`pt-hardening-…` ×2 from earlier failed attempts at this pass's own script before its bugs were fixed, `pt-insight-…` ×2 from the killed `verify-insights.ts` run — none had reached their own cleanup step) plus their cascaded rows; confirmed **zero orphaned rows** across all 15 user-scoped tables after cleanup (cross-referenced every `user_id` against `auth.users`). Also found and killed a genuinely stale, unrelated `verify-proposals.ts` process that had been running since 24-Jun (over a month, ~6.4 hours of accumulated CPU time) — a real stuck leftover from a much earlier session, unrelated to this pass, cleaned up while already investigating process hangs. Confirmed no stray scratch files in the repo (`git status` clean pre- and post-pass) and all 15 migrations applied and matching the repo (checked via live schema/column/table presence for every migration's signature artifact — direct RPC-existence pings with empty args gave false negatives, since PostgREST needs an exact parameter-signature match, not just a function name match; disregarded in favor of the much stronger evidence that every regression script above successfully CALLED every one of these RPCs with real arguments and got correct results).

A future small UI enhancement, not a correctness issue.
    - `verify-insights.ts` lacks the hardened per-step-timeout/`process.exit()` harness every script since M1 uses, and hung silently during this pass (killed manually). Worth modernizing to the same pattern at some point — out of scope here since it's an Insights-epic script, not Options/Margin.
    - The margin-call liquidator sells the single largest position ENTIRELY and repeats, rather than partially trimming or weighing liquidity — a documented v1 simplification in `monitor.server.ts`'s own header comment, not the only reasonable rule a real broker might use, but simple/deterministic/auditable by design.
    - Old `portfolio_snapshots` rows written BEFORE this fix, for any REAL (non-test) account that has ever held a margin loan at snapshot time, still contain the pre-fix (uncorrected) `total_value` — today-forward is fixed via the normal daily overwrite, but history isn't backfilled. Worth a one-time check of whether any real account is affected before or after push; a backfill migration is easy to write later if needed but felt like scope creep for this pass given the very small real user base.
    - `execute_option_trade`/`settle_expired_option`'s margin-awareness (threading `positions_value`, paying down the loan first) lives entirely in SQL (`0012_margin.sql`), duplicated logic-shape (not copy-pasted code) from `execute_trade`/the stock sell path — correct today, but a future change to ONE trade path's margin handling could silently drift from the other's if not updated in lockstep. No abstraction exists to prevent that; noted, not fixed (a shared SQL helper would be a reasonable follow-up, not attempted here to avoid touching working money-path SQL beyond what was asked).

No computation changed; this exists purely so the UI/explainer never hardcode or re-derive "8%"/"30%"/"10%" as a second source of truth — satisfies "use the M1 formulas verbatim" while staying additive-only.

Positions may be sold automatically to restore it.   - **The numbers grid — every value read verbatim from `getMarginStateFn`, zero re-derivation:** equity, positions value, margin loan, buying power, maintenance requirement, and "cushion before a call" (both $ — `equity − requirement` — and % — `(equity/requirement − 1) × 100`, guarded against divide-by-zero when there are no positions, shown as "No positions yet" instead of `NaN`/`Infinity`). Each has a one-line plain-English sub-label (e.g. "= your cash (margin off)" vs. "max(0, 2×equity − positions)" depending on live state). Accrued-interest card sums `margin_events` where `kind='interest'` client-side and shows the live annual rate.
  - **Controls, each behind a C1 `ConfirmDialog` with real numbers, per spec:**
    - **Enable** — destructive-red variant, copy states plainly: "lets you borrow up to 2× your equity... Interest accrues daily at 8%, and if your equity falls below the maintenance requirement, positions may be SOLD AUTOMATICALLY to restore it."
    - **Disable** — rendered as a `disabled` button with inline reason text ("Repay your $X loan before you can disable margin.") whenever `marginLoan > 0`, per the explicit instruction to show the existing server-side rule as a disabled state rather than an error after the fact; only becomes clickable at exactly zero loan.
    - **Repay** — amount `Input` + a "Repay max ($X)" quick-fill button (`min(cash, loan)`), `ConfirmDialog` shows a live before/after box for both the loan and cash balance.
  - **Event history** — `margin_events` newest-first, a per-`kind` formatter (`EVENT_META` + `eventDescription()`) handling every documented `detail` JSON shape: `borrow`/`repay` distinguish stock vs. option vs. manual vs. auto-from-settlement sources; `warning`/`call` show equity-vs-requirement; `liquidation` enumerates every sold line item by symbol + quantity/contracts + proceeds ("Sold 478.728497 AAPL for $150,000.00") rather than just a lump total — the "show me what happened to me" requirement.

All three now run a `useQuery(["marginState"], getMarginState, {enabled: !!profile.margin_enabled})` — gated behind the already-free `profile.margin_enabled` check (available via `useAuth()` with zero extra queries, since `profiles.*` already includes the M1 margin columns) — and fall back to `cash_balance` when off, mathematically identical to `getMarginStateFn`'s own margin-off degradation, so zero added cost/behavior change for the majority of users who never touch margin. All three trade-success handlers (`app.stock.$symbol.tsx`, `OptionOrderPanel.tsx`) now also invalidate the `["marginState"]` query key so buying power stays live after a trade without a manual refresh.

- **2026-08-06 (C1b — make "Reset paper account" real — BUILT, VERIFIED live with DB read-backs + a live browser check, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Fixes the stub C1 exposed: the button had never reset anything (a Lovable-era leftover, only fired a toast), and C1's new destructive confirmation made that actively misleading.**
  - **Design locked and documented in `0015_reset_account.sql`'s header:** reset returns the account to the SAME state as a brand-new signup — a full financial clean slate — but does NOT touch identity/login (`auth.users` untouched) or the watchlist (a preference, not a position, per instruction — kept).
  - **Scope of the wipe, each a deliberate, documented call:** `holdings`/`option_positions`/`agent_holdings`/PENDING `agent_proposals` DELETED outright (a new account has none). `profiles.cash_balance → 100000` and margin state back to column defaults (`margin_enabled=false, margin_loan=0, margin_status='ok', last_interest_accrued_at=null`) — `id`/`display_name`/`created_at` untouched, reset is not re-signup. `agent_config` reset to ITS OWN column defaults (`enabled=false, mode='autonomous', risk_level='balanced', agent_cash=0, allocated_total=0`) rather than deleted, matching that `profiles` isn't deleted either.
  - **Outstanding margin loan: reset is ALLOWED even with `margin_loan > 0` — the loan is FORGIVEN, not repaid.** A full wipe erases it along with the positions it was borrowed against; this is the one place in the schema a loan reaches zero without a `'repay'` event, because nothing was actually repaid. Documented in the migration AND stated in plain English in the confirmation dialog ("Outstanding margin loan, if any → Forgiven") so it's never a silent surprise.
  - **Ledger decision — the one that mattered most:** `transactions`/`option_transactions`/`agent_decisions`/`margin_events` are KEPT, not deleted. Every one of these is documented elsewhere in this schema as append-only (service_role-only INSERT, no client DELETE) — even closing a single position never deletes that position's own transaction log. Deleting them here would make reset the one operation in the whole schema that breaks that invariant. Instead, ONE marker row is appended to a new `account_events` table (same owner-select/service_role-write pattern as every other audit table) so history reads honestly — old trades stay real trade history, just from before the reset. A new NON-pending proposal was deliberately seeded alongside the pending one in verification specifically to prove this selective-delete boundary (pending → gone, everything else → kept).
  - **Snapshots:** `portfolio_snapshots`/`agent_snapshots` DELETED for the user, then exactly ONE fresh `portfolio_snapshots` row inserted dated TODAY at $100,000/$100,000/$0 — the value chart's new origin point, matching what a brand-new account's chart looks like on day one. `agent_snapshots` gets no replacement (the daily writer itself never snapshots an unfunded agent, so a synthetic row would be inconsistent with that rule the next time the cron runs).
  - **New `reset_paper_account(p_user_id)`:** SECURITY DEFINER, locks `profiles` FOR UPDATE first (same discipline as every money-moving function here — can't race a concurrent trade/margin/agent call), captures pre-wipe counts, does the whole wipe/reset/reseed/marker-insert atomically, returns the counts + the forgiven loan amount. EXECUTE revoked from public/anon/authenticated, granted only to `service_role`. New `app/src/lib/account/functions.ts` (`resetPaperAccountFn`, JWT-verified) + `reset.ts` (client wrapper).
  - **Settings UI:** kept C1's `ConfirmDialog` but rewrote the copy to match the REAL behavior exactly — consequence line states what's closed/cleared, a `detail` box spells out Cash balance→$100,000.00 / Stock & option positions: Closed / AI agent: Deactivated, cleared / Outstanding margin loan: Forgiven / Watchlist: Kept / Trade history: Stays visible. On success, invalidates every financial react-query key (holdings/transactions/options/snapshots/agent-everything) — deliberately EXCLUDING watchlist and market-data keys — so the whole app reflects the reset without a manual refresh, plus a truthful toast ("Cleared N position(s)… Your watchlist and trade history are unchanged.").

Double-submit guard is two-layered: the Confirm button is `disabled={loading}`, AND a local `firingRef` synchronously blocks a second `onConfirm()` call even within the same tick a rapid double-click could otherwise land before React's `isPending` re-render lands — verified live (see below) that a genuine double-click still produces exactly one fill. Confirm is auto-focused via `onOpenAutoFocus` (Radix's own default would focus Cancel first, since it's first in DOM order). - **2026-08-04 (Options & Margin epic — M1 margin engine — BUILT, VERIFIED (all 9 items), a real bug found+fixed, a codebase-wide unchecked-write audit done, committed locally) — NOT pushed; this is the highest-risk money path yet, Venky reviews every number before push.**
  - **Design (formulas documented in `0012_margin.sql`'s header + `lib/margin/config.server.ts`):** opt-in per user, off by default. `positions_value` = stock + option positions' LIVE market value (server-computed, never client, only fetched when `margin_enabled`). `equity = cash + positions_value − margin_loan`. `buying_power` = `margin_enabled ? max(0, 2×equity − positions_value) : cash` — 2:1 leverage, a simplified simulation of Reg-T's 50% initial margin; margin OFF degrades to exactly today's behavior by construction. `maintenance_requirement = positions_value × 30%`. **MARGIN CALL** when `equity < maintenance_requirement`; **'warning'** when `equity < maintenance_requirement × 1.10`. Daily interest = `loan × 8%/365`, simple daily accrual, ADDED TO THE LOAN (capitalized, not charged to cash).
  - **`supabase/migrations/0012_margin.sql`:** extends `profiles` (not a new table — margin is a property of the SAME account `cash_balance` already models; keeps one lock point) with `margin_enabled`/`margin_loan`/`margin_status`/`last_interest_accrued_at`. New append-only `margin_events` audit table (owner-only RLS select, service_role writes). New SQL functions, all service_role-only EXECUTE, all row-locking: `margin_buying_power()` (pure formula helper shared by both trade functions so they can never compute it differently), `set_margin_enabled()` (disabling REJECTED while `margin_loan > 0`), `accrue_margin_interest()` (idempotent per calendar day via `last_interest_accrued_at`), `repay_margin()` (capped at `min(requested, cash, loan)`). `execute_trade`/`execute_option_trade` both gained a `p_positions_value` parameter (required a `DROP FUNCTION` first — `CREATE OR REPLACE` with a different signature creates a second overload, not a replacement) — buys now borrow the shortfall up to buying power instead of always rejecting; sells/settlements pay down any loan FIRST, remainder to cash. **🔧 Real security gap found in passing (fixed in the same migration):** `profiles` has carried a blanket `grant update … to authenticated` since Phase 3, restricted by RLS to WHICH ROW but not WHICH COLUMN — a client could already PATCH `cash_balance` directly via PostgREST. Verified only `display_name` is ever client-updated; fixed with `revoke update … from authenticated; grant update (display_name) …`.
  - **New `app/src/lib/margin/`:** `config.server.ts` (the 4 constants above, each documented as a deliberate simplification, not a live regulatory feed), `valuation.server.ts` (`getPositionsValue` — live-priced stock + option total, the shared input to buying-power/equity), `functions.ts` (3 JWT-verified server fns: `getMarginStateFn`/`setMarginEnabledFn`/`repayMarginFn` — M2's UI will call these; no UI this phase), `monitor.server.ts` (`runMarginMonitor()` — for every margin-enabled borrower, computes live equity vs. maintenance requirement, transitions status, and on a genuine call SIMULATES forced liquidation: sells the single LARGEST position ENTIRELY via the real trade RPCs, repeats until cleared, documented as a v1 simplification — a real broker might weigh liquidity differently), `interest.server.ts` (`runInterestAccrual()` — thin batch wrapper). Folded into the EXISTING daily cron (`/api/cron/snapshot`, Vercel Hobby's 2-cron cap) in order: expiry settlement → interest accrual → margin monitor → snapshot write; margin monitor also runs in the intraday GitHub Actions watchdog so a mid-day price crash can trigger a call before the next daily cron.
  - **🚨 THE 4-HOUR VERIFICATION HANG (root-caused, not just worked around):** the first hardened-looking verify run appeared to hang indefinitely. Root cause, confirmed with a hard-timeout diagnostic script: `service_role` has NEVER had table-level UPDATE on `profiles` (0001 only ever granted it to `authenticated` — every legitimate write goes through the SECURITY DEFINER functions, which run as the function owner and never needed the caller's own grants; genuinely GOOD defense-in-depth, not a bug). A direct `admin.from("profiles").update(...)` seeding call therefore returned Postgres `42501 permission denied for table profiles` in ~200ms — but the script's uncaught `throw` on that error never actually terminated the process: **`vite-node`'s SSR module runtime does not reliably exit on an uncaught top-level exception the way plain Node does**, so it sat resident for hours printing nothing. **New standing rule (see the 🚨 bullet near the top of this file):** every verify script from now on wraps the whole body in try/catch with an explicit `process.exit()`, wraps every await in a hard per-step timeout that throws the step name, prints a timestamped line before/after every step, and runs in the foreground under a pty. Fixed the ROOT gap (not by weakening the grant) with `0013_margin_test_seed.sql` → `admin_seed_margin_state(user_id, margin_loan?, last_interest_accrued_at?)`, a narrow service_role-only RPC scoped to only those 2 columns (never `cash_balance`, never the enabled flag) — used for forcing a deterministic warning/call scenario and backdating the interest watermark (since `set_margin_enabled(true)` always stamps `last_interest_accrued_at` to TODAY, there's no legitimate same-day path to a nonzero accrual).
  - **🐛 A SECOND real bug — this one in shipped product code, found BY the hardened harness working correctly:** `lib/margin/monitor.server.ts` wrote `margin_status` via the exact same kind of raw, unchecked `admin.from("profiles").update(...)` — silently failing since M1 was first written, for the identical reason (no service_role grant). The `'warning'`/`'call'` AUDIT EVENTS still logged fine (separate table, proper grant), which is what masked it — but the `margin_status` COLUMN itself never actually persisted a transition the monitor wrote; it only ever changed via the trade functions' own inline resets. Caught live: a forced ok→warning scenario showed the correct in-memory transition and a correctly-logged event, but a fresh DB read-back still showed `'ok'`. Fixed with `0014_margin_set_status.sql` → a dedicated `set_margin_status(user_id, status)` RPC (validates the enum, service_role-only), and `monitor.server.ts` now checks the returned error on both call sites instead of discarding it.
  - **🔍 CTO-ordered follow-up audit: every Supabase write (`.insert`/`.update`/`.upsert`/`.delete`/`.rpc`, both admin and user clients) whose `error` is never inspected, codebase-wide — 54 call sites reviewed.** Findings (file:line, before fixing):
    - **Correctness-critical, fixed with a new `mustSucceed(label, result)` helper (throws — added to `lib/supabase/admin.server.ts`):** `agent/proposals.server.ts:22` (supersede-before-insert in `writeProposal`), `:89` (mark 'approved' AFTER trades already executed in `executeProposal` — **the most serious finding**: a silent failure here would leave the proposal 'pending' even though it already traded, and the ONLY guard against a second approval re-executing the same trades is `status !== "pending"`), `:117` (`rejectProposal`), `:122` (`supersedePending`, mode-switch); `agent/functions.ts:21` + `:46` (`agent_config` upsert-before-read/update in `readConfig`/`updateAgentConfigFn`).
    - **Non-fatal, inside a per-item batch loop where one failure must not abort the rest — fixed with a new `logIfFailed(label, result, errors)` helper (pushes into the caller's existing `errors[]`):** `agent/execute.server.ts:63` (seed a new position's trailing stop) + `:66` (decision-log insert); `agent/thinker.server.ts:234` + `:248` (decision-log inserts); `agent/watchdog.server.ts:193` (decision-log insert) + `:224` (run-summary insert); `margin/monitor.server.ts:191` + `:206` (`margin_events` audit inserts for warning/call/liquidation). **One of these got extra care, not just a log line:** `agent/watchdog.server.ts:210`, the trailing-STOP RATCHET write itself (the actual protection mechanism, not just its log entry) — a silent failure there would let the decision log claim a higher stop than what's really persisted, understating real downside risk. Now checked explicitly; on failure the action is recorded as `"skipped"` with the real (unchanged) stop value, instead of falsely claiming `"ratcheted"`.
    - **Already correct, no change needed (confirmed by reading, not assumed):** `lib/insights/insights.server.ts` (`writeInsightRow` checks both insert and update), `lib/snapshots/writer.server.ts` (every upsert already checked, errors pushed to a summary array — the existing, correct pattern the two new helpers formalize), `lib/options/expiry.server.ts`, `lib/portfolio/queries.ts`, `routes/app.settings.tsx`, every `execute_trade`/`execute_option_trade`/margin RPC call site.
    - **Explicitly checked whether anything else hits the same profiles-style permission wall: NO.** Grepped every migration for the target tables of every unchecked write found (`agent_holdings`, `agent_decisions`, `agent_config`, `agent_proposals`, `margin_events`) — every one of them DOES have an explicit `grant … to service_role` (unlike `profiles`, which never got one). The `profiles` gap was a one-off historical oversight from `0001`, not a systemic pattern; nothing else has been silently broken the same way. Fixed anyway per the standing instruction (transient errors, not just permission errors, deserve the same discipline).
  - **FINAL VERIFICATION (hardened harness — timestamped per-step logging, hard timeouts, foreground pty — ~28s total, exit code 0, ALL CHECKS PASSED) — every number for all 9 requested items, one primary throwaway test user (`pt-m1-verify-…`) + a second lightweight one only for the RLS check, both fully deleted after:**
    1. **Margin OFF regression:** bought 10 NVDA @ $206.64 = $2,066.40 (cash $100,000.00→$97,933.60, loan stays $0); a buy exceeding cash REJECTED (`insufficient_funds`, no state change); an oversell REJECTED (`insufficient_shares`); sold 4 then the remaining 6 (holding row DELETED, zero dust); options buy_to_open/oversized-reject/sell_to_close-full all identical; **0 `margin_events` rows** for an account that never touched margin — byte-for-byte the same math as pre-M1.
    2. **Enable margin → buy ~$150k on $100k equity:** bought 725.900116 NVDA @ $206.64 = **exactly $150,000.00**; `cash_used = min($150,000, $100,000) = $100,000`; `borrowed = max(0, $150,000 − $100,000) = $50,000`; cash $100,000.00→$0.00, loan $0→$50,000.00; equity unchanged at $100,000.00 (leverage doesn't change equity, proven not just asserted); `'borrow'` event logged for $50,000. **Exceed 2× buying power:** at cash=$0/positions=$150,000/loan=$50,000/equity=$100,000, remaining buying power = `max(0, 2×100,000 − 150,000) = $50,000`; a $70,000 attempt REJECTED (`insufficient_funds`), cash/loan/position quantity all IDENTICAL after.
    3. **Interest accrual:** loan=$50,000.00 → `interest = round(50,000 × 0.08 / 365, 2) = $10.96` → loan **exactly** $50,010.96 after the first run; a same-day re-run: `accrued: false`, loan unchanged (idempotent).
    4. **Sell pays down the loan first:** sold half the position (362.95 of 725.900116 shares) for $74,999.99 proceeds; `loan_repaid = min($74,999.99, $50,010.96) = $50,010.96`; `cash_credit = $74,999.99 − $50,010.96 = $24,989.03` (a REAL split — both nonzero); loan → exactly $0.00, `margin_status` reset to `'ok'`; `'repay'` event (`auto:true`) logged for $50,010.96.
    5. **MARGIN CALL:** re-borrowed to a fresh $20,000.00 loan, then forced (via the new seam) `margin_loan → $101,990.68` against positions_value $119,989.04 / maintenance_req $35,996.71, targeting equity $17,998.36 (well below the requirement). Monitor detected the call, liquidated exactly ONE position — the ENTIRE NVDA holding (580.667063 shares, the single largest/only position) for $119,989.04 — and recomputed the ACTUAL post-liquidation state fresh: cash $17,998.36, loan $0.00, positions_value $0.00, equity $17,998.36 ≥ maintenance_req $0.00, **status restored to `'ok'`**. `'call'` and `'liquidation'` events both logged, liquidation amount matching the summed proceeds exactly.
    6. **Warning fires before a call:** forced `margin_loan → $82,192.49` targeting equity $37,796.55, inside `[$35,996.71, $39,596.38)` — monitor: `previousStatus: 'ok' → newStatus: 'warning'`, NO liquidation, **`margin_status` correctly persisted as `'warning'` in the DB** (this is the exact check that failed before the `0014` fix — now passes), `'warning'` event logged.
    7. **Disable guard:** disabling while `margin_loan = $50,010.96` → REJECTED (`loan_outstanding`), no state change; after the sell paid the loan to exactly $0 → disable SUCCEEDS; re-enable succeeds cleanly, loan still $0.
    8. **RLS isolation:** the primary user's own session sees their 11 `margin_events` rows; the second user's session sees **ZERO** of the primary's events (both a filtered query AND an unfiltered one on their own session) and zero of their own (never touched margin); a direct `client.rpc("set_margin_enabled", …)` from an authenticated (non-service-role) session → `permission denied for function set_margin_enabled`.
    9. **profiles column-grant fix:** an authenticated client CAN update `display_name` (succeeds); the SAME client attempting `cash_balance` or `margin_loan` directly → both REJECTED with `permission denied for table profiles`; confirmed via a fresh DB read that `cash_balance` was truly unchanged (a real no-op, not just a client-side error swallow).

Committed locally in the working session; **NOT pushed** — Venky reviews every number first, per standing instruction for money paths.

- **2026-08-01 (Options & Margin epic — O4 expiration/cash-settlement processing — implemented + verified against the REAL applied migration, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Completes the options lifecycle: v1 uses CASH SETTLEMENT at expiration (standard for simulators, simpler than share delivery) — an ITM position settles for its intrinsic value in cash; an OTM position expires worthless.**
  - **`supabase/migrations/0011_option_expiry.sql` (applied by Venky):** widened `option_transactions.side`'s check constraint to add `'expired'` and `'settled'` (two distinct values rather than one `'expiration'` side with `total=0` vs `>0` — chosen so the ledger and any UI reading it self-document which happened, without inferring from an amount). New `settle_expired_option(p_user_id, p_contract_id, p_settle_per_share)`: same SECURITY DEFINER / `service_role`-only EXECUTE lockdown as `execute_option_trade`; locks `profiles` + the position row FOR UPDATE; reads `contracts`/`symbol` from the LOCKED position row itself rather than a caller-supplied parameter (the row is the only source of truth for what actually exists — nothing else could pass a stale or wrong count); rejects with `not_expired` if `expiry >= current_date` — a guard that makes the function structurally unable to touch a live position, independent of whatever the caller's own selection query filtered for. Credits `cash += settle_per_share × 100 × contracts` (zero for worthless), DELETES the position (zero dust, same pattern as a full sell-to-close), appends the ledger row with `side` set to `'settled'` (>$0) or `'expired'` ($0).
  - **EXPIRY BOUNDARY (a deliberate, documented consistency choice):** `expiry < current_date` — the EXACT same definition `execute_option_trade` (0010) already uses for what counts as "expired" when rejecting a trade. A contract expiring TODAY therefore stays fully tradeable through the whole day and is only settled by TOMORROW's run, once it's unambiguously in the past. Choosing any other boundary (e.g. same-day-after-close) would require reasoning about market-close timing inside the settlement function itself, AND would risk the two functions disagreeing about a contract's status; using the identical boundary in both makes that impossible by construction.
  - **New `lib/options/expiry.server.ts` — `runExpiryProcessing(opts)`:** finds every `option_positions` row with `expiry < today` (optionally scoped to one user via `onlyUserId`, the standard verification seam used throughout this codebase's cron jobs). Batches ONE historical-candle-series fetch per DISTINCT underlying symbol (via the shared `getDailyHistory`, already cached/reused by the event study and the O1/O3 vol estimator) — reused across every position and every user holding that symbol, the same batching discipline as the snapshot writer's equity pricing. **Settlement price = the underlying's daily CLOSE on the expiry date** (`close.t.slice(0,10) === expiry`); if that exact date's candle isn't available, falls back to the LATEST available close — a documented, non-silent choice (a position must not get stuck unsettled forever for want of one candle; real stocks rarely move enough between adjacent trading days for this to flip an ITM/OTM outcome). `settlePerShare = max(0, close − strike)` for calls, `max(0, strike − close)` for puts — pure, then passed to the SQL function. Per-position error isolation (one bad symbol/position can't abort the whole batch) + a structured summary (`positionsFound`/`settled`/`expiredWorthless`/`totalCashCredited`/per-position `settlements[]`/`errors[]`).
  - **Scheduling — folded into the EXISTING daily cron, no new Vercel cron slot (Hobby's 2-cron cap stays at 2):** `lib/snapshots/endpoint.server.ts` (`/api/cron/snapshot`) now calls `runExpiryProcessing()` FIRST, in its own try/catch (a settlement failure must never block the snapshot write itself — same isolation discipline as the AI daily-brief fold-in on the agent-thinker cron), THEN `runSnapshots()` — so any settlement cash from expired positions lands in the SAME day's `portfolio_snapshots` row, not a day late.
  - **UI touches (small, per spec):** `OptionsExplainer` gained a 4th short paragraph disclosing cash settlement in plain English ("we don't leave it hanging… you'll always see it labeled clearly — 'Settled $X' or 'Expired worthless'"). Portfolio's Options card gained a compact "Recent option activity" list (last 5 `option_transactions` rows, newest first) via a new plain RLS-scoped `getOptionTransactions()` client query (no server function needed — same pattern as equities' `getTransactions()`, RLS already restricts it to the caller's own rows) and a small `optionActivityLabel()` helper distinguishing all four ledger sides ("Bought 2 NVDA $200 Call", "Sold 1…", "NVDA $200 Call settled — $478.00 credited", "NVDA $235.96 Call expired worthless"). The positions list itself needed NO change — a settled/expired position simply disappears from `option_positions`, exactly like a full sell-to-close already does. Widened the `OptionSide` type in `supabase/types.ts` to include the two new ledger values.

Every position is re-priced LIVE via the same O1 Black-Scholes path a chain uses (never the stored `avg_premium`, which is only a cost basis) — spot is the same ~30s-cached quote the rest of the app already uses, realized vol the same ~1-day-cached series O1/the event study already share, so pricing a user's whole options book costs at most one extra quote + vol fetch PER DISTINCT UNDERLYING held, not per position, and is a cache hit on any repeat call within those TTL windows (confirmed no new per-render provider calls beyond existing patterns, per the rate-limit-awareness ask). A batched `getOptionsValueByUser()` variant mirrors how the writer already batches equities (one quote/vol fetch for the UNION of every symbol anyone holds, then grouped per user) rather than fetching per-user in a loop.
  - **TODAY'S-CHANGE CONVENTION for options (a deliberate, documented modeling choice — the task explicitly asked this be spelled out, not hidden in a diff):** a stock's day-change is (live price − previous close), a real quoted number. An option has no real "yesterday's premium" on file — it's generated, not quoted — so it's RECONSTRUCTED: reprice the SAME contract (same strike/type, the SAME time-to-expiry as right now, the SAME vol estimate) at the underlying's previous close instead of its live price. Diffing current vs. that reconstruction isolates the delta-driven component of today's move, deliberately NOT also advancing time-to-expiry by a day (today's live premium already reflects one fewer day of theta than yesterday would have shown; re-adding that decay to the "yesterday" side would double-count it). Full formula documented in the file header of `valuation.server.ts`. Dashboard's total-value/today's-change block was updated to fold in `Σ(option position dayChange)` alongside the existing equity `Σ(qty×dayChange)`, and the pre-existing inline formula comment was rewritten to state both terms.

- **2026-08-01 (Options & Margin epic — O2 trade engine — implemented + verified against the REAL applied migration, committed locally) — NOT pushed (money-math, Venky reviews line by line). Mirrors the proven Phase 6 `execute_trade` architecture exactly: atomic SECURITY DEFINER SQL function, service_role-only EXECUTE, JWT-derived identity, server-computed prices, append-only ledger.** Long calls/puts only — buy_to_open and sell_to_close. No spreads, no naked/short options, no exercise mechanics yet (O4). Contract multiplier 100 (premium × 100 per contract).
  - **`supabase/migrations/0010_options.sql` (applied by Venky):** `option_positions` (contract_id/symbol/opt_type/strike/expiry/contracts/avg_premium, `unique(user_id, contract_id)`, owner-only RLS SELECT — deliberately NO insert/update/delete grant for `authenticated`, since positions are managed EXCLUSIVELY by the SECURITY DEFINER function, a slightly tighter model than equities' `holdings`) + `option_transactions` (append-only ledger, same select+insert-only pattern as equities' `transactions`) + `execute_option_trade(p_user_id, p_contract_id, p_symbol, p_opt_type, p_strike, p_expiry, p_side, p_contracts, p_premium)`. The function: locks `profiles` FOR UPDATE (serializes concurrent trades, same as `execute_trade`); validates side/opt_type/contracts (must be a positive WHOLE number — `p_contracts <> trunc(p_contracts)` rejects fractional contracts, unlike equities' fractional shares) /premium/expiry; BUY_TO_OPEN costs `premium × 100 × contracts`, rejects if it exceeds cash, upserts the position with a weighted-average `avg_premium`; SELL_TO_CLOSE rejects if `contracts > held`, credits proceeds, reduces the position or DELETES it at exactly zero (zero dust, mirroring equities' sell-all). Also proactively granted `service_role` explicit table access on both new tables (a fix-forward for the exact gap HANDOFF already flagged on `holdings`/`transactions` — those never got it and only surfaced as friction in an unrelated test-cleanup script this session).
  - **`executeOptionTradeFn` (`lib/options/functions.ts`):** the client sends ONLY `{contractId, side, contracts}` — no strike/expiry/type/premium fields exist anywhere in the input schema. The server re-derives the contract's terms from the id ALONE via a new `parseContractId` (`lib/options/chain.server.ts`, regex-based, returns `null` — never throws — on anything malformed, including calendar-invalid dates like Feb 30), rejects an expired contract (`expiry < today`) BEFORE doing any pricing work, then computes the premium itself via a new `priceParsedContract` (same Black-Scholes path a live chain would use: fresh `getServerQuote` + `getRealizedVol` → `priceOption`). The premium is never client-supplied — `executeOptionTradeInputSchema` is exported specifically so this claim is independently checkable (see verification below) rather than just asserted. Friendly error mapping mirrors `lib/trading/functions.ts`'s pattern exactly (`insufficient_funds`, `insufficient_contracts`, `invalid_contracts`, `expired_contract`, `unknown_contract`, etc.).
  - **`lib/options/execute.ts`:** client wrapper mirroring `lib/trading/execute.ts` (attach token, unwrap the `{ok,error}` envelope). Still no UI calls this yet — O3.

This project's OWN cron endpoints (`lib/agent/cron.server.ts`) hit the identical constraint and solve it the same way: bypass the `createServerFn` wrapper and call the underlying functions directly. The verification script does exactly that — every step calls the REAL imported function (`verifyUser`, `parseContractId`, `priceParsedContract`, `getServerQuote`, `getRealizedVol`, and the REAL `execute_option_trade` Postgres function via the REAL service client) in the same order the handler does.     - **Buy 2 contracts:** server-computed premium **$4.78** → total = `4.78 × 100 × 2` = **$956.00** exactly. Cash `$100,000.00 → $99,044.00`, decreased by EXACTLY the total. Position created: 2 contracts, `avg_premium = 4.78` (= the single fill price, as expected for a first fill).
    - **Buy 1 more of the SAME contract, ~35s later** (a deliberate wait past the 30s live-quote cache TTL, so the second fill is an independently-fetched live price, not a cache hit): premium was **again $4.78** — NVDA's spot genuinely didn't move in that ~35s window (a real, unforced market outcome, not fabricated). Weighted average = `(2×4.78 + 1×4.78) / 3 = 4.78`, matched exactly by the DB row (3 contracts, `avg_premium = 4.78`). The FORMULA was fully exercised and verified correct; it just wasn't exercised on two genuinely different premiums this particular run — worth knowing when reading these numbers, not hidden.
    - **Sell 1 contract:** proceeds `4.78 × 100 × 1` = **$478.00** exactly, cash `$99,044.00 → $99,044.00`+478 confirmed to the cent, position reduced to 2, `avg_premium` UNCHANGED (sells never touch cost basis, same rule as equities). **Sell the remaining 2:** proceeds **$956.00**, cash returned to exactly **$100,000.00**, and the position row was CONFIRMED DELETED via a direct DB read (not a zeroed-out row — true zero dust).

Then ran an actual trade with that doctored payload end-to-end: filled at **$4.78** (the real server-computed premium), never at the doctored $0.01.

Oversell (999 contracts against a smaller/zero position) → `insufficient_contracts`, cash unchanged. `contracts=0` and `contracts=-3` → rejected by the Zod input schema itself (a stronger rejection than a business-logic error — never even reaches the network). Expired contract (`NVDA-2020-01-17-C-200`) → rejected server-side BEFORE any pricing/DB call, cash unchanged. Malformed id (`"NOT-A-REAL-CONTRACT"`) and a calendar-invalid date (`2026-02-30`) → both rejected by `parseContractId` returning `null`, no DB call attempted. **Defense-in-depth, proven independently:** called `execute_option_trade` directly via the admin client with `p_contracts=0` and separately with an expired `p_expiry`, BYPASSING the app-level checks entirely — the SQL function's OWN validation independently rejected both (`invalid_contracts`, `expired_contract`), proving the DB-level backstop is real, not just documented.

A direct `execute_option_trade` RPC call from user B's own (non-service-role) authenticated session was REJECTED with `permission denied for function execute_option_trade` — confirming the `service_role`-only EXECUTE grant holds at the actual Postgres permission level, not just by application-code convention.

Re-ran the O1 pure-math suites (`verify-blackscholes.ts`, `verify-options-chain.ts`) after touching `chain.server.ts` — both still 100% clean, zero regression. **Next: O3** — options UI (chain browser, order flow, positions w/ P&L) + the two flagged portfolio-valuation TODOs.

- **2026-08-01 (Options & Margin epic — O1 pricing engine — implemented + verified, committed locally) — NOT pushed. No migration, no DB writes, no UI, no trading — pure foundations only, as scoped.** Free market-data tiers have no real options chains, so v1's approach is to GENERATE plausible chains and price every contract ourselves with Black-Scholes from live underlying data + a realized-volatility estimate — disclosed to users as model-derived (not real market quotes) starting at O3. This phase built and rigorously verified the pricing engine in isolation before any DB/trading/UI work touches it.
  - **`lib/options/blackscholes.ts` (new, pure, zero I/O):** standard European Black-Scholes call/put pricing (`priceOption`) + closed-form Greeks (`optionGreeks`: delta/gamma/theta/vega), both documented with the exact formulas in the file header. Own `normCdf`/`normPdf` via the Abramowitz & Stegun 7.1.26 erf approximation (max error ~1.5e-7, far tighter than the 2dp we round premiums to). Edge cases handled EXACTLY, not approximated: T≤0 (expiry day) returns pure intrinsic value with delta collapsing to a 1/0 (or -1/0) step and gamma/theta/vega exactly 0 — no time value can remain once expiry has arrived; a degenerate σ≤0 guard (never actually hit in practice — the vol estimator is clamped to a [10%,150%] floor/ceiling — but the pure function must not divide-by-zero/NaN if called with it) falls back to the discounted-forward-intrinsic limit of the BS formula as σ→0. Vega is returned scaled to "price change per 1 percentage point of vol" and theta as "price change per calendar day" (annual theta ÷ 365) — both documented conventions matching how retail platforms display them, not the raw textbook units.
  - **`lib/marketData/dailyHistory.server.ts` (new, shared):** extracted a day-cached "~5Y of daily candles for a symbol" fetch, now used by BOTH the options engine's volatility estimator AND (refactored) the AI Insights event study (`lib/insights/eventstudy.server.ts`, `verify-eventstudy.ts`/`verify-eventstudy-live.ts` re-run clean after the refactor — zero regression). Means requesting an AI insight AND an options chain for the same symbol on the same day costs at most ONE Twelve Data call total, not two.
  - **`lib/options/volatility.server.ts` (new):** realized-vol proxy for implied vol — annualized stdev of daily LOG returns over the trailing 60 trading days, clamped to [10%, 150%]. Honesty note written into the file header for O3: Black-Scholes technically wants forward-looking implied vol, which free tiers don't expose; realized vol is a real, live-data-derived, disclosed simplification, not a fabricated constant. Short/degenerate history (a recent IPO, an empty series) falls back to the 10% floor rather than NaN/crash.
  - **`lib/options/chain.server.ts` (new):** `generateExpiries` — the next 4 standard monthly expirations (3rd Friday of each month, today's month included if not yet passed) + the next 1-2 near-term weekly Fridays not already a monthly, sorted chronologically, all UTC-midnight date-only (avoiding the classic local-time/date-only off-by-one this codebase has fixed elsewhere). `generateStrikes` — an 11-strike ladder (5 below spot + ATM-nearest + 5 above) centered on spot, step size by price magnitude ($1 under $25, $2.50 under $100, $5 under $250, $10 at/above — documented in a code comment, a deliberate simplification worth revisiting against real chain conventions later). `buildChain` prices every strike×expiry×{call,put} via the Black-Scholes module using the symbol's realized vol, with a documented constant `RISK_FREE_RATE = 4%` (explicitly noted as a refinement lever — a future pass could source a real short-term Treasury yield instead). Each contract gets a stable `contractId` (e.g. `NVDA-2026-09-18-C-200`), `premium` (2dp), `delta` (4dp), and an `intrinsic`/`extrinsic` split.
    - 🐛 **Real bug found + fixed during verification:** `extrinsic` was originally clamped to `max(0, premium − intrinsic)`. A deep-ITM European put close to expiry can genuinely price slightly BELOW raw intrinsic value (the discounted strike `K·e^(−rT) < K` — a real, well-known European-option artifact, not a mispricing), which made the clamped extrinsic silently break the `intrinsic + extrinsic == premium` reconciliation identity — caught by a unit test asserting that identity across every contract in a generated chain (one specific K=225 6-day put failed: premium $24.95 < intrinsic $25.00, clamped extrinsic 0, 25+0≠24.95). Fixed by removing the clamp — `extrinsic` is now simply `premium − intrinsic`, which reconciles exactly BY CONSTRUCTION and can be (rarely, slightly) negative, an intentional and now-documented behavior rather than a hidden bug.
  - **`lib/options/functions.ts` (new):** `getOptionChainFn`, a JWT-verified TanStack Start server function returning the generated chain for a symbol. In-memory 1-hour cache — documented in a code comment WHY this is fine per the project's serverless rule (in-memory state doesn't survive Vercel invocations, so a paid-call day-cache like AI Insights' truly needs the durable `insights` table): a chain is cheaply RECOMPUTABLE derived data from two already-cached inputs (a ~30s-cached live quote, a ~1-day-cached vol series) — not itself a paid external call — so a cold-invocation cache miss just re-runs cheap local math, unlike a miss on a real paid Claude call. 1-hour TTL (shorter than the once-daily AI insight) balances not recomputing on every click against staying reasonably fresh to intraday spot moves, which move premiums throughout the trading day.
  - **VERIFIED — pure math (`verify-blackscholes.ts`, 34/34 checks, no network):** every price cross-checked against an INDEPENDENT numerical-integration reference (Simpson's rule, 20,000 steps, over the risk-neutral lognormal density — a completely different computational method from the closed-form `normCdf`) — matched to within half a cent on 6 cases including the classic textbook S=K=$100, T=1, σ=20%, r=5% example (computed **call $10.4506 / put $5.5735**, the well-known Hull textbook values) and the Hull S=$42/K=$40/T=0.5/r=10%/σ=20% example (**call $4.7594 / put $0.8086**). Put-call parity (`Call − Put = S − K·e^(−rT)`, an exact BS identity) held to `1e-14` across a 7-strike ladder. Expiry day (T=0): call/put priced to EXACT intrinsic (not approximated), delta exactly 1/0, gamma/theta/vega exactly 0. Zero-vol degenerate guard: finite, matches the discounted-forward-intrinsic closed form to `1e-9`. Deep ITM: price > intrinsic by a small discounting premium (<5% of spot). Greeks sanity: call delta ∈(0,1)/put∈(−1,0), ATM delta ≈0.58 (r/σ-shifted off the naive 0.5), deep ITM→1/deep OTM→0, theta negative for long ATM calls AND puts, vega positive, gamma peaks at ATM vs. ITM/OTM.
  - **VERIFIED — chain structure (`verify-options-chain.ts`, all checks, no network, pinned `asOf` date for determinism):** expiries independently cross-checked against a brute-force day-by-day Friday-scan (a different algorithm than the module's closed-form 3rd-Friday calculation) — matched exactly for Aug–Nov 2026. Strike ladder step sizes confirmed at all 4 price-magnitude bands; 11-strike count within the requested 8-12 band. A full fabricated NVDA-like chain (spot $200, vol 45%) confirmed call premiums strictly DECREASE and put premiums strictly INCREASE across every strike, in EVERY expiry; ATM extrinsic is the largest (or tied-largest) value in every row; `intrinsic + extrinsic == premium` reconciles for every one of 132 contracts checked. A chain built ON an actual expiry date confirmed every contract prices to exact intrinsic (T=0). Realized-vol math independently verified on a synthetic known-stdev series (computed 16.01% vs. independently hand-derived 16.01%), plus the MIN_VOL floor and MAX_VOL ceiling both proven to actually engage on a flat and an extreme-swing synthetic series respectively.
  - **VERIFIED — real market data (`verify-options-live.ts`, real Twelve Data + Finnhub, no mocks):** realized vol **NVDA 42.3% vs. KO 24.8%** — the volatile name meaningfully higher, as expected. Day-cache sharing confirmed: a second `getDailyHistory("NVDA")` call in the same process returned the identical 1,255-candle array near-instantly (cache hit, no repeat network call). A real generated NVDA chain (live spot $200.75) produced 6 expiries, all on real Fridays (Aug 7/14/21, Sep 18, Oct 16, Nov 20 2026), sorted ascending; monotonicity and intrinsic+extrinsic reconciliation held across the ENTIRE real chain (not just one slice); the $200 (nearest-to-spot) strike had the largest call extrinsic ($4.03) in the nearest expiry; all deltas well-formed. Full printed strike/premium/delta table for the nearest expiry (6 days out) included in the script's output for eyeballing.
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean (emits `.vercel/output`), client-bundle grep (secrets + `getServiceClient`/`providerSeries`/`providerQuotes`/every new `lib/options/*` + `dailyHistory.server` module name) → 0 matches; confirmed no `.tsx`/non-`.server.ts` file imports anything from `lib/options` yet (no UI this phase, as scoped). **Next: O2** — DB model (options positions/transactions, owner-only RLS) + server-side long calls/puts + sell-to-close execution engine, reusing this pricing module for fill prices.

- **2026-08-01 (AI Insights V2 — event study layer — implemented + verified, committed locally) — NOT pushed. No migration, no new provider/key.** Replaces "Claude recalls history" with "we MEASURE history": a new pure math module computes real forward-return statistics from the stock's own past price behavior and feeds them into the insight, and the UI renders those exact computed numbers directly — never a number Claude wrote.
  - **`lib/insights/eventstudy.server.ts` (new, pure + unit-tested):** exact definitions, documented in the file header. A "shock" on day *i* is a daily return `|r_i| > 2σ`, where σ is the standard deviation of the TRAILING 60 daily returns strictly BEFORE day *i* (no look-ahead; needs ≥20 trailing returns before a threshold is computed at all). Direction: `r_i > 0` → up-shock, `r_i < 0` → down-shock. For each qualifying shock, measures the +5-trading-day ("1 week") and +21-trading-day ("1 month") forward returns — an event only counts once BOTH forward windows are fully realized in the data (a shock from 10 days ago isn't half-counted with a missing 1-month figure; it's excluded from both until it has a full month behind it, so the 1-week and 1-month stats always describe the identical population of events). Aggregates `{ events_found, avg_fwd_1w, median_fwd_1w, avg_fwd_1m, median_fwd_1m, worst_1m, best_1m, pct_positive_1m }` separately for up-shocks and down-shocks; `window_years` is the ACTUAL span of data used (computed from the candles' own first/last dates), so a recent IPO honestly reports a short window instead of a padded one. `getMeasuredHistory(symbol, direction)` fetches ~5Y of daily candles via the EXISTING `providerSeries` (Twelve Data, already used by charts/simulator — no new provider) and returns the stats for the requested direction; on any provider failure it throws and the caller degrades to `null` rather than fabricating a number.
  - **Wired into `getStockInsight` (`insights.server.ts`):** direction is chosen from the stock's own live `day_change_pct` sign (up day → look up its up-shock history; down day → down-shock history) — a simple, deterministic, already-available signal, not a separate judgment call. The measured stats are summarized and handed to Claude as `measured_history` context, with new system-prompt rules: if `events_found >= 5`, GROUND `historical_parallel` in the exact measured numbers (cite N and the median) and never state different or invented figures; if 1–4, numbers may be cited but the sample must be called small; if `measured_history` is null or `events_found` is 0, Claude must not state any numeric forward-return figure for this stock at all. Critically, **the `measured_history` field persisted in the final `StockInsight` and rendered in the UI is OUR computed object, merged in directly — never derived from Claude's JSON output** — so what's on screen is guaranteed to be exactly what was measured, not a number Claude might restate slightly differently. Costs exactly one additional Twelve Data candles call — but it sits INSIDE the same per-symbol-per-day gate as the Claude call (the `insights` table DB-first check + the `cached()` wrapper), so a day with an existing cached insight costs zero extra candle fetches, same as it costs zero extra Claude calls. The daily brief job (`runDailyBriefs`) was deliberately NOT touched, per the "no added calls to the brief" ground rule.
  - **UI (`InsightUI.tsx`):** new `MeasuredHistoryBlock`, rendered directly under "Historical rhyme" and before "Risks" in the AI Insight card. When `events_found > 0`: "After N similar single-day {gains|drops} in the past Xy, SYMBOL was higher a month later Y% of the time (median ±Z%, range A% to B%)" + a small-sample caveat line when N < 5, explicitly labeled "Measured directly from SYMBOL's own price history — not AI-recalled." When `events_found === 0` (or the field is absent — e.g. an insight generated before this shipped): an honest "not enough precedent to measure a forward pattern" message, no fabricated numbers, no NaN. Old cached `insights` rows from before this change simply have no `measured_history` key; the UI treats `undefined` the same as `null` (block renders nothing) — no migration/backfill needed, verified by reading the render path (`if (!mh) return null`).
  - **Unit-tested (`app/verify-eventstudy.ts`, synthetic data, no network):** built a candle series with a known-by-construction answer — an alternating ±3% "quiet" baseline (to establish a real, non-degenerate trailing σ) with 3 planted −20% down-shock days (unambiguously > 2σ), each followed by a SMOOTH sub-threshold compounding path landing on an exact chosen forward-return target at +5/+21 days (so those forward returns are exact by construction, not estimated), each event separated by a 70-day quiet buffer (> the 60-day trailing window) so trailing windows never mix across events. Cross-checked TWO ways, neither reusing the module's own internals: (1) an independently-written reference shock-detector (its own mean/stdev, not imported from the module) flagged EXACTLY the 3 planted days and nothing else, and the module's `findShockEvents` matched it exactly; (2) the aggregate stats (avg/median/worst/best/%-positive, both 1w and 1m) matched hand-computed values from the planted numbers to full float precision. The `up` direction (no up-shocks planted) correctly reported `events_found: 0` with every stat `null`, not `0`/`NaN`/fabricated. A separate short-history (15-day) synthetic series and an empty series both degraded to `events_found: 0` everywhere with no crash/NaN. **24/24 checks passed.**
  - **Real-data verification (`app/verify-eventstudy-live.ts`, real Twelve Data + Claude + Supabase, no mocks):** NVDA (volatile) → 35 qualifying up-shock events over the full 5-year window, `historical_parallel` explicitly cited "35 comparable single-day up-moves... median 1-month forward return was -1.31%... 46%..." — grounded in the real measured numbers, not generic recollection (asserted by checking the prose contains the exact N). VOO (calmer ETF) → 25 events, fewer than NVDA as expected from the chart. CRCL (a real, genuinely recent 2025 IPO) → `window_years: 1.2` (honestly short vs. NVDA/VOO's full 5), 5 qualifying down-shock events — real data, reported as observed rather than assuming a zero-event outcome (the zero-event/insufficient-history degrade path itself is fully proven by the synthetic tests above, where it's constructible on demand; a real symbol with literally zero comparable shocks wasn't available to test against but isn't a different code path). **Day-cache re-confirmed end-to-end:** a same-day repeat of `getStockInsight("NVDA")` returned the byte-for-byte identical `measured_history` and `generatedAt`, with an independent call counter (`measuredHistoryCalls()`, mirroring the existing `insightClaudeCalls()` pattern) proving **0 extra Twelve Data candle fetches** alongside the existing **0 extra Claude calls** proof. Direction sanity independently re-verified: a fresh, separate `providerQuotes(["NVDA"])` call confirmed the stored `measured_history.direction` matched NVDA's actual live day-change sign. **9/9 checks passed** (throwaway `pt-v2-verify-…@example.org` test user used only for the browser UI check below, deleted after).
  - **Verified rendering in the real running app:** signed in as the throwaway test user, opened NVDA's Stock Detail page, clicked "Get AI insight" (served from the day-cache, 0 new Claude/candle calls) — the accessibility tree and extracted page text confirmed the "Measured History" block renders in the correct position (between "Historical rhyme" and "Risks") with the exact computed values ("After 35 similar single-day gains in the past 5 years, NVDA was higher a month later 46% of the time (median -1.3%, range -29.3% to +34.2%)"), labeled "Measured directly from NVDA's own price history — not AI-recalled." (Browser-pane screenshot capture hit a known scroll-timing rendering glitch in this session — the same harmless artifact observed and diagnosed during the R4 mobile-polish pass, confirmed there via DOM measurement to be a tool capture race, not an app bug — so this pass relied on the accessibility-tree/text extraction instead of a pixel screenshot for the final visual confirmation.) `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean (emits `.vercel/output`), client-bundle grep (secrets + `getServiceClient`/`providerSeries`/`eventstudy.server`) → 0 matches.

- **2026-07-31 (Robinhood-parity R4 — mobile/tablet polish pass, the epic CLOSER — implemented + verified, committed locally in 4 chunks) — NOT pushed. No migration/env, no feature changes — Tailwind responsive utilities only (no separate mobile codebase, no browser-sniffing, server code untouched).** Fixed Venky's exact phone-screenshot punch list, then did a full page-by-page audit at ~375px phone / ~768px iPad / desktop.
  - **Punch-list item 1 — stat cards stacking full-width on phones:** Dashboard's and Agent's summary stat grids changed from `sm:grid-cols-2` (stacked 1-up below `sm`) to an unconditional `grid-cols-2` (2×2 on phones AND tablets, `xl:grid-cols-4` only at wide desktop) — Robinhood-style compact cards instead of a whole scrolling screen per stat. `Stat`/`SummaryStat` components got responsive padding (`p-3 sm:p-5` / `p-3 sm:p-4`) and `truncate` on label/value/sub so a long value never blows out the card.
  - **Punch-list item 2 — chart y-axis duplicate tick labels ("$100k/$100k/$99k/$99k"):** root cause was `PortfolioValueChart.tsx`'s tick formatter always rounding to whole `$k`, which collides when the visible value SPAN is narrow (a flat week, or a small agent allocation) — two adjacent ticks can round to the identical label. Fixed with a SPAN-AWARE formatter (`fmtAxisTick`): span ≥$20k → whole `$k` (safe, ticks are far apart); span ≥$300 → one decimal `$k` (e.g. "$99.9k"/"$100.0k"); narrower still → full dollars. Applies to both the Dashboard and Agent performance charts (they share one `PortfolioValueChart` component). Checked `LivePriceChart.tsx` (Stock Detail chart) too — its y-axis is already `hide`d (no visible labels), so no collision exists there; left it alone rather than unhiding it (would be a feature/visual change beyond "fix the formatter," out of R4's stated scope). The Simulator's growth chart and the What-If chart both format ticks via full `toLocaleString()` dollar strings (never `$k`-rounded), so they were never subject to this bug either — confirmed by inspection and by a live scenario run (ticks $0/$85,000/$170,000/$255,000/$340,000, all distinct).
  - **Punch-list item 3 — holdings/activity tables clipping horizontally on phones:** replaced `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w-[Npx]` (forced horizontal scroll) with RESPONSIVE COLUMN PRIORITY — low-priority columns hidden below their breakpoint (`hidden … sm:table-cell` / `md:table-cell` / `lg:table-cell`) while the identity/value/P&L columns stay always-visible — across all 6 tables in the app: Dashboard holdings, Portfolio holdings + transactions, Watchlist, Markets (both the live-search and Popular-grid row variants), and Agent holdings (the heaviest table, 8 columns — Qty/Avg cost/Price/Weight/Trailing stop staged across `sm`/`md`/`lg`, Symbol/Market value/P&L always shown). Symbol/name cells got `min-w-0` + responsive `truncate` (short max-width on phone, wider from `sm:` up) so long company names never force the row wider than the viewport. Percent-change spans wrapped `hidden … sm:inline` (dollar amount always shown, `(±Y%)` detail added back once there's room).
  - **Stock detail page + order panel (Chunk 3):** buy/sell and shares/dollars segmented toggles got taller touch padding on phone (`py-2.5 sm:py-1.5`); the main Confirm button became a full-width `h-12` (48px) thumb-friendly CTA instead of default button height, matching the "confirm should be easy to hit one-handed" ask; the 4-tab bar (Your position/News/About/Recent trades) wrapped in `overflow-x-auto` + `w-max` so it scrolls horizontally instead of squishing/wrapping when it doesn't fit; card padding `p-3 sm:p-5`. Verified at 375px and 768px — 768px fits all 4 tabs with no scroll needed; 375px scrolls cleanly with the next tab peeking at the edge (a legible affordance, not a bug). A large apparent blank gap seen once mid-scroll during verification turned out to be a screenshot/scroll-capture race in the browser test tool itself, not a real layout bug — confirmed via direct DOM `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements showing normal, contiguous card spacing.
  - **Agent page remainder (Chunk 4, beyond the shared stat-grid/table fixes above):** Fund/Withdraw buttons and the proposal Approve/Reject buttons now stack full-width on phone (`flex-col sm:flex-row`) instead of two cramped side-by-side buttons — easier one-handed tapping, consistent with the order panel's CTA treatment. Risk-level and Mode setting buttons got taller phone touch padding (`py-2.5 sm:py-2`). The approve-mode proposal trade list (badge + symbol + qty@price + reason) got `flex-wrap` so a long line doesn't force horizontal overflow on a narrow phone. **Real bug found + fixed:** the Funding card's `Stat` component (Available/Agent cash/Allocated, a 3-up grid) truncated the value text — "$100,000.00" rendered as "$100,00…" on a phone, an actual "overflowing/cramped number" of the kind the audit was looking for. Fixed by dropping to `text-xs` (from `text-sm`) on phone with no truncation, restoring `sm:text-base` at larger widths — verified the full `$100,000.00` now renders without clipping.
  - **App-wide nav (found during the systematic audit, not on Venky's original punch list, but the same class of bug — "overflowing text"):** the persistent top-bar search input's placeholder ("Search any ticker — AAPL, QQQ, VOO…") is squeezed between the phone hamburger button and the theme/notification/avatar icons — far less room than the full-width search bars on the Markets/Watchlist pages — so on phone it visibly clipped mid-word to "Search any ti… AAI". Shortened the placeholder to "Search any ticker…" (a content change, not a breakpoint-conditional one, per the "no browser-sniffing" ground rule) — fits cleanly at every width tested. Left the Markets/Watchlist page-level search inputs' longer placeholders alone since those have a full card-width to work with and never clipped.
  - **Full audit, no changes needed (verified clean at 375px/768px as-is):** Landing page (hero, feature grid, CTA, footer — nav already `hidden md:flex` with mobile fallback), Auth page (brand panel already `hidden lg:block`, form fills width nicely), public + in-app Simulator (form card, headline result card including a `$297,712.66` value, vs-SPY grid, growth chart — all stack/scale cleanly, ticks distinct since the chart uses full-dollar formatting not `$k`-rounding), Settings (profile/appearance/paper-account cards, Reset button), and the existing mobile nav hamburger/slide-out drawer (already `md:hidden` + overlay, already a reasonable one-handed pattern — left as-is).

Committed in 4 logical chunks as asked (dashboard+charts, tables, stock page+order panel, agent+rest) rather than one blob; **pushed to `origin/main` (`a3b4b93`) at Venky's request** — also carried R3 (`5c42f8b`) to origin in the same push, which turned out to already be sitting locally-committed-only; auto-redeploys the live site.

- **2026-06-16 (Phase 6 — trading engine, implemented; migration + verification pending)** — Real server-side buy/sell. New migration `supabase/migrations/0003_execute_trade.sql`: `execute_trade(p_user_id, p_symbol, p_side, p_quantity, p_price) returns jsonb` — runs the whole trade atomically (function body = one tx), locks the user's `profiles` row `FOR UPDATE` to serialize races, validates qty/price/side, BUY (reject if cost>cash, deduct cash, upsert holding w/ weighted-avg avg_cost, insert 'buy'), SELL (reject if qty>held, add proceeds, reduce/delete holding, insert 'sell'); EXECUTE revoked from public/anon/authenticated, granted ONLY to `service_role`. Server fn `app/src/lib/trading/functions.ts` (`executeTradeFn`): verifies the Supabase JWT server-side (`verifyUser` → user_id, never client-sent), fetches price server-side (`getServerQuote`), calls `execute_trade` via a service-role client; returns `{ok,result}|{ok,error}` with friendly messages. `app/src/lib/supabase/admin.server.ts` (service client + `verifyUser`), `app/src/lib/marketData/quote.server.ts` (shared server quote, same cache keys). Client wrapper `app/src/lib/trading/execute.ts` attaches the access token + unwraps the envelope. Stock Detail order panel wired to real market orders (react-query mutation → on success refreshes profile cash + invalidates holdings/transactions + success toast w/ fill); limit orders kept as a clearly-marked TODO (not executed). Secret: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` server-only (no VITE_), in `app/.env` (gitignored) + `.env.example`. **Verified so far:** `tsc`+build clean; client-bundle grep → service key, `service_role`, `execute_trade`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` all 0 matches; `execute_trade` server-side only. **PENDING (needs Venky):** apply `0003_execute_trade.sql` in SQL Editor; then Claude runs deterministic end-to-end (buy → buy again → weighted-avg check → sell part → sell rest → overspend/oversell rejections) + a browser buy of AAPL, and reports numbers.
